**From analyse on demand of 27 October** (`feat/analyse-now`)
333. ~~**The interim indicator.** What should the final one be, and should a done track flash "Analysed"?~~ **Answered by Design's Brief A (31a–31g), built:** `AnalysisLine` replaces it everywhere, and a done track says "Canopy is ready to mix" once, for 4 s (Log, "Dev, Brief A … of 28 October").
334. ~~**Tracks' rows** show the indicator only for asked-for tracks in line and the one working: fine, or a quieter mark for the sweep's own order?~~ **Answered by 31c, built:** each row not ready to mix has its two-word marker; a track in the sweep's own order says what it has ("Not analysed", "BPM and key"), never its place, and "Ready" isn't shown on Tracks.
335. **"Analyse these"** is in Tracks' selection "…" (after Start a set from these), an album's "…", a playlist's "…" (before Export to Traktor…) and a set's "…" (first), with "12 tracks · ones already done are skipped" as its second line, and a toast after ("9 tracks will be analysed next. 3 tracks already done are skipped."). Members had no album "…": they get one holding only Analyse these, beside Report issue. Right places and words?
336. **What asks by itself:** the Mix tab's track while the tab is shown (not a track merely playing), a set's page while it's open (all its tracks, at most 500), Add to mix set… and the working set, Up next's next 50 library tracks with Auto mix on (or a set played as a mix), and the lab's picks. Each track is asked at most once in ten minutes by these. Too eager anywhere (a 500-track set opened by accident)?
337. **Stop and asking.** A person's Stop empties the line and holds the automatic sweeps until a restart or Start, as before. While it holds, a page's automatic asks are turned away (quietly; their tracks show "Analysis is stopped"), and only a person's own ask goes in: Analyse these, Analyse now, the Mix tab's button, a pick in the lab (the hold stays), so a member can still analyse their own tracks after an admin's Stop. Right, or should Stop hold a person's asks too?
338. ~~**No crossfade in Auto mix:** is a cut with no grid worth keeping, and should Up next say anything for a play-on?~~ **Answered by Design's 302** (the moves that replace the crossfade), **built in step 2** (Log, "Dev, step 2 … of 28 October"): a cut on the outgoing track's last phrase, an echo out into a ready incoming track, or straight on; nothing without a reliable grid is cut by bar count, and one album's tracks in their order play straight on quietly.
339. ~~**What stays crossfade-like:** the engine's 2-second finish of a mix the system cut off; keep, or end that mix at once?~~ **Answered by Design's 308:** if a call or the lock stops the sound, SYNCHRO.S cuts to the next track when it's back: the mix finishes with a cut, not a 2-second crossfade, and an interrupted countdown plays out straight on. Built (the Gauntlet review, below).

**Open follow-up from step 4 (Dev, not a question for Design):** L9, a phone row's long-press in Brief B (450 ms, then the row drags within its list or onto the Send to… tray) needs a check on a real iPhone (Safari) and a real Android phone (Chrome). Since the review round a non-passive `touchmove` listener is added from the touch's start (`dnd-engine.ts` `pointerDown`), so the browser shouldn't commit the finger to a scroll before the lift; headless Chromium's touch emulation can't show whether iOS still sends `pointercancel` there. Until it's checked on devices, the sheet's Send to… and the Move mode are the phone's sure paths.

## Log

### 2026-10-29: Dev, beat grids on real audio of 29 October

Built on `fix/grid-real` from `integration` (3972ede4), after Ross's report that the latest build got most of his tracks' beats wrong or "not found". **Every earlier grid test used generated audio, and the rules had been tuned to it.** This round measured the grid on real music and rebuilt what failed there. **Grid version 5:** every stored grid is found again by the sweep (nothing at 4 is promoted; Ross's ~110 analysed tracks are a few minutes of grid-only decodes, counted in the analysis indicator's "to do" meanwhile; a mix that needs one of them waits for it, as at every version).

**The bench** (local files only, nothing committed): Ross's seven 2-minute clips (the core), plus 34 openly licensed public tracks (librosa's example tracks, Sonic Pi's CC0 loops, Essentia's techno loop, the three StepMania songs with their charts as an annotated beat grid, Red Eclipse's CC-BY-SA soundtrack, others), each at three BPM hints (the analysis's, as an untagged track gets; the true one, as a tag; none). The reference beat is an independent tracker's (madmom's, not shipped: its models are CC BY-NC-SA), the charts', and Ross's ear where he gave it. `scripts/grid_bench.py` runs the same measurement on any files you point it at (`--list tracks.csv` with an optional reference BPM and first beat, or a beat annotation file).

**What was wrong, measured:**
- **The confidence didn't tell a right grid from a wrong one** (on the bench, AUC 0.68: 14 of 21 grids on the beat scored under 0.5). Its "beats louder than the half-beats" factor sank techno with an off-beat bass (contrast 0.97, so 0.12 although on the beat); its 0.45 caps fired on right grids: the half-beat break move (right 5 times in 6), the ×0.6 "which sixteenth" doubt (four-on-the-floor tracks measure 0.88–1.10 with off-beat hats).
- **The phase follow-up moved two of Ross's tracks the wrong way:** Acid went a sixteenth off at 0.59 (the busy-line move took a bass line whose backbeat share was 0.84 over the kick's), Realizer fell from 0.78 to no beat. Its kick test (`kick_onsets`) called 7 of 16 real kicks a bass: real kicks' sub decays at 0.05–0.88 against the 0.5 bar (generated kicks 0.05–0.10). `UNSURE_PHASE` never caught a wrong phase on real audio and held right ones.
- **The hint was a fence:** a wrong analysis BPM (123 or 89 for songs at 180) kept the grid within 4% of itself, where it found no beat. A hint of 87 kept drum and bass at 87 (Heartbeat Loud): the backbeat vote said "slow".
- **The downbeat was at chance:** its salience agreed with the independent tracker on 12 of 39 tracks; the whole spectrum's attack and the timbre's change follow the clap on two and four.

**What it does now:**
- **The beat's confidence** is the hit rate (no contrast factor) and an independent tracker's agreement (`_agreement`): librosa's beat tracker (already a dependency) held to the grid's tempo, on the low band and on the drums' attack band (500–4000 Hz), in 8-bar windows at fixed times where the drums play; a break's on the whole spectrum. 0.45 of it from the hits, so a grid no tracker agrees with is never ready.
- **Removed:** the busy-line move, `kick_onsets`/`onset_shape` and `UNSURE_PHASE`; the break cap on a half-beat move (kept on a sixteenth's, right 1 time in 4); the ×0.6 doubt on four-on-the-floor grids. A rival line with the backbeat still moves the grid (as at v4 before the follow-up).
- **The hint is a prior** (`_pick_tempo`): its 3/2, 2/3, 4/3 and 3/4 and the grid's own scan are tested, and one the onsets fit 1.5 times better wins; a tempo that ties with a related one within 8% is held (`TEMPO_UNSURE_CONFIDENCE`). **Drum and bass:** a slow hint at 82–89 is doubled when the grid's scan reads the double, whatever the backbeat vote; the file's genre tag (now passed by the worker) decides first: drum and bass doubles, hip hop, R&B, reggae keep it slow.
- **Downbeats** follow the kick, with the harmony's turn at half weight; their confidence is how many 8-bar windows agree. It never decides a beat-match (it only marks the bars as unsure).
- **Kept:** the comb's steady grid, the drift path, the tempo scan, `FLOOR_EVENNESS` (it can't be tightened on real audio: real house 0.13–0.58, a waltz and drum-and-bass stems up to 0.35; it now only decides the ×0.6 doubt and the rival move). CPU: about 0.2–0.9 s more per 10 minutes of audio for the grid (one core; 1.8–2.6 s in all), the agreement itself 0.05 s.

**Results** (the production hint; "ready" is confidence 0.5 or more in 4/4; 4T4 and Realizer left out of the counts, their phase is disputed):

| | v3 | v4 (14564619) | 3972ede4 | v5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ross's clips: on the beat / ready / ready but wrong | 5 / 2 / 0 | 4 / 2 / 0 | 3 / 1 / 1 | 5 / 4 / 0 |
| public dance and electronic (21): on the beat / ready / ready but wrong | 9 / 5 / 1 | 10 / 5 / 1 | 10 / 5 / 0 | 12 / 10 / 1 |
| downbeat agreeing with the independent tracker (of the grids on the beat) | 6 of 20 | 6 of 21 | 5 of 20 | 16 of 23 |

Ross's clips at v5: Acid 0.86, London Thing 0.78 (by ear 127 from the start: right), Acid Lab 0.70, Heartbeat Loud 0.75 at 174; Action 0.39 (on the beat, but librosa's tracker takes its off-beat bass: held); Realizer 0.40 and 4T4 no beat (both disputed; by ear neither starts on bar 1: v5's Realizer downbeat is at 1.59 s, the 4th beat). The two "ready but wrong" are a Red Eclipse track at 2/3 of the reference's tempo and a pop song doubled (88 → 177): the octave of an untagged slow pop song is still a guess.

**Known limits:** a generated bass louder than a generated clickless kick, with no clap (and a rolling tone bass, a "donk"), can be trusted on the bass: the tracker hears the bass too. These are strict xfails in `test_grid_real_mixes.py`; no real track on the bench did this. Untagged hip hop at 82–89 is read at double (a genre tag or the person's ÷2 fixes it). The bench is small: 7 real clips of Ross's, few house or techno tracks among the public ones.

**Tests:** `test_grid_v5.py` (the confidence, the agreement's phase sense and its independence from the grid it checks, the hint as a prior, drum and bass under a slow hint and the genre, the tempo tie, the downbeat and its confidence, an unsure downbeat still beat-matching); `test_grid_accuracy.py` and `test_grid_real_mixes.py` re-expressed (breaks moved half a beat are ready; the bass-over-kick hazards kept as strict xfails); the version tests at 5.

**Review round (the same day).** The independent review found ready-but-wrong grids tripled on held-out Groove audio, all of them doubled tempos, and that the hit term could carry a grid alone. What changed (`core/dj/grid.py` unless named):
- **H1, the octave.** A grid at 150 BPM or more, or at an octave of the raw hint, is held at 0.45 (`_octave_unsure`) unless a genre says which octave it is; the backbeat vote isn't evidence (it says "slow" on most real fast tracks: Heartbeat, breakbeat at 170, jazz). A slow genre halves a fast grid on the scan path too (`_genre_octave`), not only under a hint. Halving the slower of an octave pair is gone (it put an Amen break at 170 at 85).
- **H2, drum and bass's half.** A hint at 82–89 is doubled only with drum-and-bass evidence: a drum-and-bass genre, or the scan reading the double with a backbeat at the double (`_fast_vote`). A held grid no longer sets the BPM shown (`core/dj/store.py` `_GRID_BPM` needs a ready one; the DJ data's BPM stands). `SLOW_GENRES` gains chillout, lounge, ambient, dancehall, downbeat and reggaeton (`core/sample/tempo.py`).
- **M1, the agreement gate.** Under `AGREE_READY` (0.5, the mean of the two bands' trackers) a grid is held whatever its hits, and the agreement counts in full only from hits 0.5. The earlier claim that grids "a sixteenth or an eighth off agree at 0.0–0.05" was wrong: the review measured 8 of 38 grids 1/8 early passing. Now grids shifted 1/8–7/8 of a beat from the reference pass `AGREE_READY` on 4 of 259 (public and Ross's) and 8 of 483 (Groove train); the reference grids on 25 of 37 and 54 of 69. It isn't proof of the phase: where the tracker takes an off-beat bass, it agrees with a grid half a beat off.
- **M2, a bass-only intro.** The phase choice and the agreement are taken only where the drums play (`_drum_frames`: the hats' band over 4 s, used when it covers 15–90% of the track). The 40- and 16-bar bass-intro probes are on the kick now (no longer xfails).
- **M3:** the drift path goes through the same holds and reports the same diagnostics. **M4, downbeats:** each 8-bar window counts by its lead over the second phase, and a downbeat whose top two phases are within 1.5 standard errors is capped at 0.29 (on the bench, of the grids on the beat: right downbeats 29, 21 of them at 0.3 or more; wrong 18, 7 at 0.3 or more; 3972ede4: right 8, wrong 37, 11 at 0.3 or more). **M5:** a pad's flux is floored (`FLUX_FLOOR`, `BEAT_ENV_FLOOR`): a sine is no beat, a chord pad held.
- **L3:** a file with no genre tag takes enrichment's album, then artist genres (`core/dj/worker.py` `_library_genres`); one tag naming two families ("Hip Hop, Drum & Bass") says neither. **L6:** halving keeps the louder parity of the beats (`half_parity`, kept in `mix_json` as `half`; `grid_store.rescale`); on the bench's doubled grids it chose the true beats 31 times in 35, the downbeat's parity 24. **L7:** `TEMPO_TIE` kept: it holds 5 right and 6 wrong grids, no ratio separates them, and the agreement can't settle a tie (grids at 4/3 or 3/2 of the true tempo agree at 0.9+ on 7 of 37 and 6 of 69). **L1/L2:** `_interp` removed, stale comments fixed. **L8:** the first `beat_track` in a process compiles numba (about 4 s): the analysis warm-up runs with `warm=False`, so a grid-only sweep pays it once per process; no change.
- **Tests (L4):** the generated kick has a click (a few ms of noise; every real kick has one), so the steady tracks are ready at 0.7 again, except 100.5 BPM, a strict xfail (the tracker follows its off-beat bass and hats); the 174 is ready with its genre and held without. The Amen test expects ready from the break, not from the grid's own sixteenth (the 160 BPM case with a 0.5 s lead-in is a strict xfail: the tracker takes the kick line in some windows). The pluck rolling basses say what holds them (the tempo tie: an onset as strong on every sixteenth fits 4/3 of the tempo as well). `test_dj_grid_v4` checks the pending ids exactly. New tests for the octave hold, the hint-octave conflict, the agreement gate, the drum mask, the pad, the downbeat cap, `half_parity`, the held grid's BPM, enrichment's genres and mixed tags.

**Groove (Magenta's Groove MIDI, CC BY 4.0), tuned on its train split only**, 69 clips (4/4 beats, rendered audio, the click moved by the players' median deviation): d10/s1_2_jazz-swing_215, d1/s1_100_neworleans-secondline_94, d1/s1_101_dance-disco_120, d1/s1_102_funk_95, d1/s1_146_latin-brazilian-baiao_95, d1/s1_184_reggae_78, d1/s1_201_reggae-slow_64, d1/s1_3_funk_80, d1/s1_48_jazz_93, d1/s1_77_jazz-mediumfast_180, d1/s1_78_jazz-fast_290, d1/s1_79_latin-reggaeton_90, d1/s1_80_dance-breakbeat_170, d1/s1_81_dance-breakbeat_170, d1/s1_82_neworleans-funk_84, d1/s2_7_jazz-march_88, d1/s2_8_jazz-march_176, d1/s2_90_funk-rock_92, d1/s3_10_rock-folk_90, d1/s3_11_afrobeat_94, d1/s3_14_rock_94, d1/s3_16_funk_117, d1/s3_2_jazz-swing_185, d1/s3_4_jazz-linear_182, d1/s3_6_dance-disco_120, d3/s2_16_rock_100, d3/s2_20_rock_92, d3/s2_24_rock_92, d3/s2_26_hiphop_92, d4/s1_1_rock_87, d5/s1_10_latin-brazilian-sambareggae_96, d5/s1_15_funk_100, d5/s1_3_latin-brazilian-samba_89, d5/s2_11_soul_96, d5/s2_18_latin-brazilian-samba_93, d5/s2_5_reggae_126, d5/s2_6_neworleans-secondline_99, d6/s2_1_rock_95, d6/s2_3_rock_88, d6/s3_6_rock_88, d7/s1_10_rock_93, d7/s1_12_funk_102, d7/s1_17_hiphop_100, d7/s1_1_rock_86, d7/s2_106_soul-motown_104, d7/s2_123_hiphop_140, d7/s2_54_soul-motown_148, d7/s2_72_funk_100, d7/s2_87_hiphop_75, d7/s2_97_pop_142, d7/s3_120_rock_93, d7/s3_131_soul_105, d7/s3_151_soul_98, d7/s3_38_hiphop_67, d7/s3_47_funk_112, d7/s3_64_funk_112, d7/s3_67_neworleans-funk_93, d7/s3_91_neworleans-funk_93, d7/s3_92_hiphop_70, d8/s1_10_neworleans-funk_82, d8/s1_14_hiphop_94, d8/s1_23_hiphop_70, d8/s1_25_funk-latin_100, d8/s1_26_afrobeat_94, d8/s1_3_hiphop_90, d8/s1_4_hiphop_90, d8/s1_9_funk_96, d9/s1_25_rock_90, d9/s1_2_rock_100. Its test split is left for the next review. All downloaded audio deleted after.

**Results, ready-but-wrong** (ready = 0.5 or more in 4/4; against the reference beat, F under 0.8), 3972ede4 → now:

| | analysis hint | true hint | no hint | production |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ross's clips (5 scored) | 1 → 0 | 1 → 0 | 1 → 0 | → 0 |
| public dance (22) | 0 → 1 | 2 → 0 | 0 → 1 | → 1 |
| other genres (11) | 1 → 0 | 0 → 0 | 1 → 0 | → 0 |
| Groove train (69) | 4 → 1 | 5 → 1 | 9 → 5 | → 1 |
| mika_x30 (counted now) | 0 → 1 | 0 → 1 | 0 → 1 | → 1 |

Ready and right: Ross 1 → 4 in every mode (Acid 0.86, London Thing 0.78, Acid Lab 0.70, Heartbeat Loud 0.74 at 174 by its tag's genre); public dance 5 → 7 (analysis), 6 → 8 (true); other genres 2 → 5; Groove 12 → 11 (analysis), 16 → 16 (true), 12 → 13 (none). The wrong ones: **redeclipse_06** at 120 where the reference reads 181.8 (2/3; at 120 the half-beats are nearly silent in every band, 0.03–0.25 of the beats, so I'd judge the reference wrong, by ear to be sure); **mika_x30**, a drum loop half a beat off madmom (our beats are the louder line in every band, low 0.88 against 0.51: madmom likely took the off-beat; an ear settles it); Groove **hiphop_70 read at 140** under an analysis hint of 143.6 (no genre on Groove's files; a hip hop tag holds it) and, without a hint, two more hip hop at 70 → 140, a hip hop at 92 → 113 and a rock at 100 → 100.6 a beat-fraction off. redeclipse_07 is held (0.24). CPU: the grid is 0.27 s median per clip (1.4 s per 10 minutes, one core).

**Review round 2 (the same day).** On the reviewer's untouched Groove test split (50 clips), ready-but-wrong went from 14 at df8a20ba to 5 (analysis hint), 8 to 0 (true hint), 12 to 5 (no hint). One blocker remained: untagged drum and bass (Ross's library is mostly drum and bass) could never be beat-matched. Heartbeat Loud with its genre removed was held at 174 under every hint, or read at 87 under a hint of 87. A ×2 moved the grid but never changed its confidence, which the planner and Analyse go by, and the Library showed 117.5 or 87. What changed:
- **H-new 1, a person's octave releases the hold.** The analysis keeps each octave's confidence as if that octave were settled (`octave_confidences`: the hits, the agreement and its holds, the tempo tie, at ×1, ×2 and ÷2; in `mix_json` as `octave`). A ×2 or ÷2 (`grid_store.follow_override`), or a person's BPM at the measured tempo itself, gives the grid that confidence, and a reset brings the measured one back (`measured_json` now keeps it). A new analysis under a person's BPM does the same. The Library shows a grid held only for its octave when the DJ data's BPM isn't an octave of it (`store._GRID_BPM`: 174, not the analysis's 117.5). At an octave of it, the DJ data's BPM shows, and one ×2 or ÷2 from there settles it. Design question 399: a "This tempo is right" action for the case where Halve-then-Double is the only way.
- **H-new 2:** a tag's or enrichment's BPM (not the analysis's) at the grid's octave settles it (`tempo_evidence`, from the worker); at the other octave it holds the grid, as the raw hint did.
- **H-new 3 and M-b, drum and bass by its drums** (`drum_and_bass_beats`), measured at the fast grid in 16-beat windows. Two parts:
  - The snare's crack (1–5 kHz) on the beats either side of the loudest kick, against the beat opposite it, must reach `DNB_BACKBEAT` 0.35. This is anchored on the kick because a real kick has a crack of its own; that crack hid the backbeat from `tempo.backbeat` (Heartbeat: "slow").
  - The two-step's second kick, half a beat after the opposite beat, must reach `DNB_TWO_STEP` 0.5 of the kick.

  Heartbeat scores 0.46 and 0.79. Red Eclipse track 9 (150 BPM) scores 0.68 and 0.68. None of the 45 grooves at 70–100 read at double (Groove train, public set) has both: the backbeat reaches at most 0.49, on a hip hop groove whose second kick is 0.18. Groove's two "breakbeat 170" clips are played half-time (one snare every four fast beats); neither reaches the backbeat. Only one real drum-and-bass track was at hand: nothing else fetchable legally from here (GitHub raw only) was drum and bass. So a genre tag, a tag's BPM or a person's ×2 is still the surer evidence. The drums settle a fast grid's octave even against a hint or tag at half (87 for 174). They replace the backbeat vote for the 82–89 doubling, and they keep a slow-tagged grid fast (ambient jungle at 170 isn't halved to 85, L-c), held because the genre and the drums disagree.
- **H-new 4:** hardcore, UK hardcore, hardstyle, rawstyle, gabber, frenchcore, speedcore, uptempo, terrorcore and hard techno join the fast genres. Jazz, bebop, swing, big band and jazz fusion are a new `free` family: any tempo, with the fast one believable, so the octave is settled as found. "Hardcore hip hop" and "jazz hop" are slow. `_octave_unsure`'s docstring had claimed hardcore and jazz said so by their tag; now they do.
- **M-a:** a grid that the drums' attack band (500–4000 Hz) disagrees with outright (under `AGREE_ATTACK_MIN` 0.1) is held, whatever the low band says. The low band's own disagreement doesn't hold a grid, because a loud bass takes the low band's tracker off a right grid (house with a rolling bass: 0.0 and 0.99). This fixed all six strict xfails of the bass-over-kick and donk limit (they pass now; L-a's label is moot). On the bench it held 5 wrong grids and one right one that wasn't ready anyway; shifted grids now pass on 3 of 259 and 8 of 483.
- **M-c:** enrichment's album and artist genres are merged, and generic ones (Electronic, Dance, Electronica, EDM, Pop, …) are left out. When the genres name the fast family and another, fast wins for a fast reading (liquid drum and bass tagged Soul).
- **L-b:** a soft (clickless) kick and an 808 sub kick, with or without a clap and an off-beat bass, are ready on the beat, and a louder off-beat bass over them is never trusted.
- **L-d:** no loosening of the downbeat's confidence was found that didn't let wrong downbeats through. Across window weightings, penalties and separations, each right downbeat gained above 0.3 brought a wrong one. Kept.
- **The remaining Groove test errors** (a 65 BPM rock groove read at 130, a 190 prog track read at 95) are left for the reviewer, as asked; nothing was tuned on the test split.

Results (Groove train, the public set and Ross's clips, every hint mode): ready-right and ready-wrong are unchanged from the first review round. Ross's 4 are ready with and without their tags (Heartbeat untagged: 174 at 0.74 under no hint, 87, 117.5 and 174). Untagged Heartbeat before → after: held at 0.45 (or read at 87 at 0.35) under every hint, with ×2 leaving it held → ready at 174 (0.74) under every hint, shown as 174. With its drum evidence switched off (a stand-in for drum and bass without a two-step), the hint of 87 reads 87 at 0.35 and one ×2 makes it 174 at 0.79, ready. CPU: the grid is 0.32 s median per clip, about 1.7 s per 10 minutes of audio (+0.25 s, mostly the two octaves' agreements).

**Review round 3 (the same day).** Untagged Heartbeat was fixed, Groove test didn't regress, and the drum-and-bass detector had no false positives in 56 tries. What remained was a person always being able to fix a held track in one press, and half-read fast tracks. What changed:
- **H1, a person's choice releases every cap tied to the octave.** `octave_confidences` scores each octave on its own beats (hits, the agreement and its holds) times the grid's steadiness. It no longer carries the measured octave's caps over (`free/base`), which had held a real Amen at 85 to 0.40 after its ×2. A person's choice of an octave lifts both the octave's hold and the related-tempo tie for that octave (`free_confidence` is now taken before the tie). The agreement's hold stays.
- **M1, "BPM is right"** (`confirm`, alongside double, halve and reset, in `store.set_tempo` and `POST /api/library/dj/bpm`) keeps the BPM shown as the person's, so the grid takes that octave's confidence. On Tracks it leads the "…"'s BPM items for a track whose grid waits only on its octave (`bpm_unsure` in the DJ data). This is Design question 399's provisional answer. The Library also shows a held grid's BPM when its ×2 or ÷2 would be ready and the DJ data's BPM isn't an octave of it: a real Amen read at 85 under an analysis's 117.5 used to show 117.5, whose ×2 was 235.
- **M2, half-read fast tracks:** a 70–100 grid with no slow genre and no tag or enrichment BPM at its octave is held when its double has drum and bass's drums (`drum_and_bass_beats`; Heartbeat read at 87). Nothing else separated half-read fast tracks from slow grooves on the bench. The comb at the double is as strong or stronger on 38 of 45 slow grooves (Groove train and public). The ×2 octave's confidence is 0.35–0.91 on the bench's right slow grids, against 0.77–0.85 on half-read fast ones. Off-beat onset density overlaps too. So half-time drum and bass (a snare every fourth fast beat) and a prog track at 190 read at 95 are still confidently half when a hint says so. These are left for the Beat This! trial. No real slow track is newly held: every table below is unchanged.
- **M3:** the Groove punk at 128 on E-GMD's "Raw Dnb" kit, ready 0.42 of a beat off, is a known limit. No cheap general cause was found, and the kit isn't on the bench.
- **L2:** jazz (`free`) no longer settles the octave hold (acid jazz, jazz funk and nu jazz are grooves at 80–100 that read at double). **L3:** `json_extract` is guarded with `json_valid()`. **L4:** a rescaled grid keeps `octave` in its mix regions, and after a ×2 also `half` (0: the measured beats). **L1:** Ross's `dnb*` clips hadn't arrived, so the detector isn't re-calibrated.

Results: Ross's clips with and without tags, Groove train and the public set are unchanged from round 2 in every hint mode. The press flows, from the measured grid under each DJ BPM:
- **Untagged Heartbeat:** ready at 174 (0.74) under every DJ BPM. ÷2 gives 87, ready 0.77.
- **The forced roller** (Heartbeat with its drum evidence switched off):
  - Under 117.5: 174 held, "BPM is right" offered; it gives 0.74, ready.
  - Under 87 (tag or analysis): 87 at 0.35; one ×2 gives 174, ready 0.80.
- **A real Amen (Sonic Pi, CC0) at 170 and 176:**
  - Under 87, or with no hint (shown 85 now): one ×2 gives 170 or 176, ready 0.80–0.85. Before, nothing released it.
  - Under 117.5 or 174: held at 170 or 176; "BPM is right" gives 0.90–0.91, ready.
- **Generated half-time drum and bass:**
  - With no hint: 172 held; "BPM is right" gives 0.77.
  - Under 86: ready at 86 (0.996). This is the known limit above; one ×2 gives 172, ready.

**Review round 4 (the same day).** The analysis was left unchanged. In the Library's display and offer (`core/dj/store.py`):
- A held grid's BPM never replaces a tag's or enrichment's. A held grid at 5/4, 3/4 or 2/3 of a right tag had shown its wrong tempo (105 for a funk tagged 84), and "BPM is right" made the 96 for a 128 ready.
- A held grid's ×2 or ÷2 counts toward showing it only within 70–200 BPM (`OCTAVE_CHOICE_RANGE`). `octave_confidences` no longer scores octaves outside that range either.
- "BPM is right" is offered only when the BPM shown is the one octave the grid would be ready at (computed in Python, `_octave_choices`).
- When two octaves would do (a track read at double: soul at 86 shown as 172), Tracks' "…" says "It's 172 BPM" and "It's 86 BPM" after Double and Halve, with neither first.
- `set_tempo` answers whether each grid is ready now (`ready`), and the toast says "ready to mix" only then.
- New tests from the reviewer's reproductions, plus the member, shared-track and other-profile cases for `confirm`.

### 2026-10-29: Dev, step 4 (Brief B, drag and drop) of 29 October

Built on `feat/step4-dnd` from `integration` (3972ede4): step 4 of Design's build order of 27 October, approved by Ross, from `Drag and drop (Brief B).dc.html` (32a–32g), README "Added 2026-10-27" and `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-27.dc.html`, with the step 3 entry's leftovers (UX-27's row grid, Up next's earlier / later / remove, the `sendTo` hook, Up next's and a set's own HTML5 drag) and questions 327, 328 and 377. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code identifiers, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. DJ now (step 5) isn't part of it, beyond a hook. **No new route, no schema change** (the review round's one server change adds a field to an answer, below): every drop goes through the routes that exist (`POST /api/sets/<id>/tracks` with `again` and `after`, `GET` and `PUT /api/sets/<id>` with `base_revision` for an Undo, `POST /api/sets/holding`, `POST /api/sets/working/start`, the set page's own autosave) and the player's queue in the browser. Interfaces: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Drag and drop (Brief B)"; patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("Drag and drop", the row grid, Send to…, the Move mode); rows: `SCREENS.md`; `BACKLOG.md`, "Star tracks and mix sets" and "DJ now".

**One drag model, on pointer events** (`webui/src/features/dnd/`): `dnd-engine.ts` (the drag's state, the targets' registry, hit-testing, auto-scroll, the drop, its toast and Undo, the keyboard's pick-up), `dnd-model.ts` (what lifts and why not, move or copy, where a drop lands round locked rows, the step in Sets' words, duplicates, every sentence), `dnd-host.tsx` (the ghost, the line, the amber notes, the scroll cue, the player bar's zone, the sidebar's Your sets, the phone's tray, ⌘Z, Up next's binding), `row-grid.ts` (UX-27), `send-to.ts` / `send-to-ui.tsx`, `set-targets.ts`, `up-next-dnd.ts`, `items.ts`, `grip.tsx`. **Why pointer events and not HTML5 drag and drop:** HTML5's drag doesn't start from a long-press on iOS or Android (32e's lift), draws its own ghost (no 1.5° tilt, no count, no ⊘), gives no reliable pointer position to auto-scroll from at a constant rate (Firefox's `drag` event reports 0, 0), and can't be driven by the keyboard; one pointer path serves the mouse, the pen and a finger alike, with `setPointerCapture`, `elementFromPoint` under a ghost that takes no pointer, and a non-passive `touchmove` that keeps the page still once a row is up. **Up next's and a set's own HTML5 drag are gone** (`draggable`, `dragstart`… and `npQueueDrag*` removed), not kept beside it.

**Board by board**
- **32a, two Tracks rows into a set's list:** every source row has **the grip**, a reserved 24px column (three 10×2 bars at 62% on the row's hover and focus; at 14% and not lifting on a locked row; none without a file): Tracks (`tracks-page.tsx`), Search's library tracks (in the row's left padding, `library-section.tsx`), an artist's and an album's own tracks (`artist-detail/-ui/tracks-tab.tsx`), the Mix tab's matches in the tray and the drawer (`mix-tab.tsx`), a set's rows (`set-editor.tsx`) and Up next's rows to come (`static/media-player.js`). A mouse lifts once the grip has moved 4px; **the source rows dim to 40%**, and a **multi-selection lifts as one**: drag any ticked row in Tracks' select mode and the whole selection goes, the ghost reading "Static Bloom, Undertow" with its count badge. **The ghost** (48px, 1.5° tilt, `#1a1a1e`, accent-light 60% edge) carries title · key · BPM and keeps the offset where it was grabbed. **Targets light at once** (1px dashed accent-light at 70% with a 6% accent fill): a set's list, the sidebar's Your sets, Your sets on the Sets page, the Mix tab's set card, Up next (the tray's list, and the player bar's "Up next · drop to play next" zone). **The sidebar's Your sets** unfold under its Sets row while something is lifted (up to eight, the working set first; not in the collapsed rail), each saying "drop to add · goes last" as the ghost passes. **The insertion line** (2px accent-light) opens a 36px gap and previews both new steps in Sets' words, worked out in the browser by Sets' own rule (a port of `core/sets/steps.py`): "Smooth · 8A → 9A, +1 · 2 tracks · Jump · 7A → 4B, +4 into Brass Tide" (+1 being a step round the wheel, 387). **Auto-scroll** near a list's (or the page's) top or bottom edge runs at a constant 8 rows a second, with a gradient cue "▲ scrolls" / "▼ scrolls".
- **32b, every target and every state:** (1) the grip on hover and focus; (2) the lifted ghost, one and many; (3) a valid hover on a set's list (the line), a set in the sidebar or Your sets ("drop to add · goes last"), the set card ("adds as #9", or "after Night Ferry (#4) · adds as #5" while the working set plays, 293), Up next's top ("Play next") and lower places ("Goes in as #4"), a transition gap (both chips, 32c); DJ now isn't drawn (step 5); (4) the line with its steps; (5) **invalid, in amber on the target itself with the reason** (a note at the target, the ghost gaining ⊘; never a toast): a missing file and a drive that's away "Can't be lifted: its file is missing" with Find it again (said on the source row; nothing lifts; 328), a search result that isn't yours "Only library tracks can be mixed · Download it first" (and it has no grip), a locked row's place "Canopy is locked in its place · drops land after it", **a set you can't edit** "A famous mix can't be changed · Save a copy to edit it" (a saved famous mix, as 32b draws Boiler Room 2019; 386), and mid-mix "Can't replace the incoming track · drop after it"; (6) **a duplicate**, still a valid drop: "In it already · #4 · drop to add again" on the line (a set's list) or the row (a set elsewhere, from `POST /api/sets/holding`, asked once when a drag starts); Up next allows duplicates without a note; (7) auto-scroll; (8) **the drop's toast with Undo for 6 s** (step 3's toast and `dismissToast`, key `dnd-undo`), "Added 2 to Warm-up · #4–5 · analysing, 2nd in line", ⌘Z / Ctrl Z doing the same while it shows (not in a field); one drop is one undo however many tracks; (9) **locked rows hold**: the line never opens at a locked row's place, a drop there lands at the nearest free place after it, and the other rows flow round locked ones exactly as the server's `insert_keeping_locked` and the page's `moveRow` do (`insertKeepingLocked`, its inverse `removeKeepingLocked` for Undo).
- **32c, a Mix tab match into a transition gap:** Up next (`up-next-dnd.ts`, binding the legacy list) is a target at any place after the playing row: its top is Play next; with Auto mix's gaps shown, a place between two rows is a gap, and the line previews both new chips from code's plan for each pair ("Long blend · 32 then Cut") **when both plans are already in the browser's cache** (327: never asked for on a hover); otherwise it says where the track goes and the chips come after the drop. **While a mix runs or counts down the first gap is closed** ("Can't replace the incoming track · drop after it"); the second is open. A drop of an Up next row within it moves it (Undo moves it back); anything else is copied in (`SoulSyncPlayer.insertAt`, Undo `removeTracks` takes exactly those rows out).
- **32d, the keyboard:** **UX-27's row grid** is folded in (`row-grid.ts`, on a set's rows, Up next, Tracks and the Mix tab's matches): a list is one tab stop (a roving tabindex on its rows, the rows' buttons out of the tab order), ↑ ↓ Home End move between rows, → and ← reach a row's buttons and come back, Enter plays the row (its Play, Brief D's visible button), a field keeps its own keys, and the list's hint says so once (`aria-describedby`). **Space picks the focused row up** in a list that reorders (a set's, Up next): the row is drawn lifted (2px accent-light outline, 14% fill), ↑ ↓ move it a free place at a time (locked rows stepped over), ⇧↑ ⇧↓ to the top or the end, each move announced with its new steps through the list's own polite region (the set's `data-set-announce`, Up next's `np-queue-announcement`): "Picked up Canopy, 3 of 8. Arrows move it; Enter drops; Escape cancels." · "Canopy, 5 of 8. After Brass Tide: Jump, 4B to 9A, the keys clash. Before Undertow: Smooth."; Enter (or Space again) drops, with the toast and Undo; Escape cancels ("Cancelled. Canopy is back at 3 of 8."); the line follows in view. In a list that only lifts (Tracks, the Mix tab's matches) Space opens Send to… for the row or the selection (390). **Send to…** fills step 3's `sendTo` hook: a submenu in every track's "…" on a desktop (24a's order, after Add to set…): "Sunday Set (working set) · adds as #9", "Up next · after Static Bloom", "Another set…" (the picker); DJ now joins when step 5 provides it (`provideDjNowEntry`). Tracks' selection "…" has Send to… too. Up next's Delete on a focused row removes it.
- **32e, the phone (390):** a **long-press** (450ms, held within 8px) on a row lifts it at 1.03; moving first is a scroll. It drags within its list (a set's, Up next) with the same line, and **the Send to… tray** rises at the foot: the working set (with where it goes), Up next (play next), Other sets (the picker) and **DJ now, disabled, "laptop only"**; "Let go anywhere else to cancel". The sheet's Send to… (34c's place, between Add to set… and Add to playlist…) opens the same tray to tap. Rows don't select or call up the iOS menu on a long-press.
- **32f, the Move mode:** the phone's way without a drag: a set's row's sheet → Move gives "Moving Canopy · 5 of 8" with Done over the rows and ▲▼ on the row (locked rows stepped over, each move said with its steps); Done says "Moved Canopy to #5" with Undo. Up next (`static/media-player.js` `npStartMoveMode`) has the same, started from a ↕ on each row to come (44px; 392), its Done using the same toast and Undo (`window.SoulSyncDnd.showDropResult`). The desktop keeps Move up, Move down and Move… (UX-30) in a set's "…".
- **32g, who lifts what and where it lands:** built as the matrix says: within one list a drop is a move (a set's rows, Up next's rows, a gap in Up next); everywhere else a copy (Tracks, Search, an artist's or album's tracks and the Mix tab's matches onto a set's list, a set, the set card, Up next or a gap; Up next's rows onto a set copy, a set's rows onto Up next copy). The Mixes panel is gone (step 3). DJ now's column is absent until step 5: `DJ_TARGET_READY` (`dnd-engine.ts`), the bar's zone and the Send to… entry are where it plugs in.

**Design's questions for Dev answered here**
- **327 (answered, with the default Dev built):** a pair's plan *is* cached in the browser (React Query, `planKey`, 60 s, filled as Up next's chips and a set's chips are drawn), so a hover reads it without a request; the line shows both chips only when both are cached, otherwise where it goes and the chips after the drop. The Smooth / Jump step never waits: it's code in the browser from the BPM and key the rows already carry (a port of `core/sets/steps.py`), shown when both tracks have them; otherwise the line says where it goes ("Adds as #5") and the step is drawn after the drop. Nothing on a hover asks the server.
- **328 (answered, the default):** a library track whose file is on a drive that's away is refused at the lift with 32b's reason ("Can't be lifted: its file is missing"), not added and marked.
- **377 (answered):** Up next's earlier and later are gone; a drag, Space and the arrows (32d) and on a phone the Move mode (32f) reorder it. Remove stays on hover and focus (391).

**Ross's rules:** nothing says "crossfade" (the chips' words are the planner's: Long blend, Cut, Echo out…); a drop onto a set, the set card or Up next with Auto mix on queues what isn't ready to mix for analysis the way step 1's triggers do, and the toast says so once ("Added · analysing, 2nd in line"); DJ now is laptop and desktop only (the phone's tray says so); a track's own sections never move (a drag moves rows, nothing inside a track); Play stays the row's visible button (Enter presses it); "set" throughout.

**Measured in a real browser** (Chromium at 1440 and 390, the app's own build with an in-browser stand-in server, `step4-work/probe/dnd.spec.ts`): two ticked Tracks rows lifted as one ghost ("Static Bloom, Undertow", the count), the sidebar's Your sets unfolding, Boiler Room 2019 going amber, Warm-up lit with "drop to add · goes last", the drop posting both ids and the toast "Added 2 to Warm-up · #4–5" with Undo; a set's first row dragged over a locked Canopy (its amber words) and on to before Brass Tide, the line reading "Jump · 9A → 8B, +2 · Jump · 8B → 4B, +4 into Brass Tide", the rows flowing round the locked row as the server would; Space, ↓ and Enter on a set's row with its words and "Moved Harbour Lights to #5"; a Tracks row dropped into the open tray's Up next ("Goes in as #3"), and onto the bar's "Up next · drop to play next" with the tray shut; on the phone a long-press with the tray at the foot (DJ now "laptop only"), let go elsewhere to cancel, and a set's Move mode. Fixed from it: a locked row's grip showed at rest; the keyboard's line could sit out of view; the Move mode's ▲▼ were squeezed on a phone. Screenshots in the session's scratch folder (`step4-work/shots`).

**Left out, and why**
- **DJ now as a target** (Brief E, step 5): not drawn at all (absent, not disabled) on the desktop; hooks: `DJ_TARGET_READY`, `provideDjNowEntry` (Send to…), the bar's zone. The phone's tray shows it disabled "laptop only" (398).
- **A famous set's own page** still edits it (Sets 1); only drops refuse it (386).
- **274's Notice for a stale drop** onto a set that isn't open: the add is one transaction on the server, so there's nothing stale to say; a set open in another tab hears `ss:mix-set-changed` and its autosave's revision check stands. A drop's Undo reads the set again and saves from that revision, trying once more if it moved on.
- **Search's results card** has no row grid (artists, albums and tracks share it); its library tracks' keyboard path is Send to… in their "…". Listening history isn't a source (not in the brief).
- **Tests run in jsdom**, so geometry (the gap under the pointer, the 36px, auto-scroll) is checked in the browser probe, not by vitest.

**Tests:** vitest `features/dnd/dnd-model.test.ts` (lift reasons and 328, 32g's move or copy, the step and its words, the insertion line and 327, locked rows: insert, snap, land after, undo exactly, the keyboard's steps; duplicates; the words), `dnd-engine.test.ts` (a mouse lift after 4px, the source dimmed, the targets lit, amber with the reason, the gap and line, the drop with its 6 s Undo, ⌘Z once, cancel outside and on Escape, the release's click swallowed, a missing file refused with Find it again, a long-press and a scroll, the keyboard's pick-up, moves, drop and cancel with their announcements), `send-to.test.ts` (a set not open: the route, again, the analysis line, a famous mix refused, nothing in said; Undo through GET and PUT from the revision read, tried again when stale, rows gone; the working set after the playing track and started when there's none; Up next's Play next, a place and Undo; the draft), `send-to-ui.test.tsx` (Send to…'s words, 293, a famous working set, DJ now absent until provided, the submenu and the phone tray, focus and Escape), `up-next-dnd.test.ts` (Play next, a place, both chips from the cache, the first gap closed mid-mix, a move with Undo, a copy, Space, the arrows and Enter, Delete), `row-grid.test.ts` (UX-27), `sets-page.test.tsx` (Space, ↓ and Enter on a set with its words, the 6 s Undo and ⌘Z through the autosave; a drop from Tracks with its line, a duplicate, Undo; a locked row's place), `queue-accessibility.test.ts` (Up next's grips, no HTML5 drag, the Move mode with its Undo, earlier and later gone, focus kept), and the menus' order in `tracks-page.test.tsx`, `tracks-tempo.test.tsx` and `sets-page.test.tsx`.

**Review round (29 October, the independent review of 12308d9d):** every finding fixed on the same branch.
- **H1, an Undo that moved the playing track:** `SoulSyncPlayer.moveTrack` (now `npMoveTrack`) refuses a row at or above the playing one (`-2`), so a drop's Undo, the bar zone's and the Move mode's say "Can't undo: it's playing now" instead of taking the playing track to its old place and leaving the rows between as played. A drop finds an Up next row by the row itself, not the index it had when lifted, refuses one that has started playing ("Can't move it: it's playing now"), and a keyboard pick-up whose rows moved under it is refused ("Up next changed while it was lifted · try again"). Found in the browser too: **Up next's grip couldn't be grabbed at all** (the row's stretched Play, `::after` inset 0, sat over it); it's above it now, and the probe drags an Up next row, plays it and sees its Undo refused.
- **M1, an add after the playing track lost the person's transitions on Undo:** `POST /api/sets/<id>/tracks` with `after` now answers `reset: [{track_id, number, transition}]` (`core/sets/store.append_tracks`, the one server change; a field more, no new route or table), and the drop's Undo gives each back while it's still the Auto the add left, then takes the rows out (`send-to.ts` `restoreJoins`). Python test `tests/mix/test_working_set.py::test_m1_…`.
- **M2, a keyboard pick-up left behind:** it ends (with its words) when the focus leaves its list, on Escape anywhere, when its list goes (a route change) and when a pointer starts a drag.
- **M3, the incoming track mid-mix:** refused for the keyboard too (`kbdPreview` carries the reason: amber, said, and Enter keeps it lifted), checked again at every drop, and the player itself never puts a row in its place or moves it (`firstFree()`, `insertAt`, `moveTrack`, `removeTracks`, the Move mode's ▲, Move to the top); Send to…'s and the phone tray's Play next go straight after it ("· after the incoming track"), and lifting the incoming row says why it can't.
- **M4:** a set's rows take their track from the view by track id, so a row picked up just after an edit carries its own track (and its title).
- **Lows:** the step port reads "08A" and " 9B" as the server does and rounds as Python's `round` (0 differences from `core/sets/steps.py` over the reviewer's 6,000 pairs, was 268); `removeTracks` saves the queue; the sidebar's and the Sets page's working set take drops after the playing row; an Undo whose numbered row holds another track now says it can't instead of guessing; Tracks' selection lifts in the list's order and a drag and Send to… both leave out missing files and say so; no auto-scroll or cue over a target that isn't a list (the bar's zone); the drag's state is read by parts (only the ghost follows each pointer move), the row grid's observer tidies only the rows a change touched and the arrows check only the rows they pass; holdings are asked only once a drag comes over another set; the Send to… tray traps Tab and takes Escape for itself; Tracks' and the Mix tab's rows have accessible names; a set's Move mode follows its row through rows adopted from the server; a phone row's long-press listens for touchmove from the touch's start (L9: to check on real iOS and Android devices).
- **The re-review's Lows (all fixed, each with a test):** L-a, during a pick-up with the focus on a control inside the row, Escape now puts it back and any key sends the focus back to the lifted row first (`kbdEscape` steps aside only on the lifted row itself; `row-grid.ts`), `row-grid.test.ts`; L-b, a set holding a track twice gets each copy's own join back (`restoreJoins` matches number and track), `send-to.test.ts`; L-c, an Up next Undo that couldn't take every row out (the incoming track a mix holds) says what stayed ("Took 1 of 2 out · one is playing or mixing in now, so it stays") instead of "Undone", `send-to.test.ts`; L-d, a played or the playing row's empty grip lets a press through to its Play (`pointer-events: none`), `queue-accessibility.test.ts`. L9 stays open: a real-device check (Open questions, after 398).
- **Tests added:** `queue-accessibility.test.ts` (H1 on the real `npMoveTrack`, the Move mode's Undo, M3), `dnd-engine.test.ts` (M2's four ends, M3's keyboard), `up-next-dnd.test.ts` (H1 by identity, the keyboard's moved rows, M3 at the drop), `send-to.test.ts` (M1, L4, Play next mid-mix), `send-to-ui.test.tsx` (the tray's Escape and Tab), `dnd-model.test.ts` (L1). The reviewer's browser probes (R1–R3) and Dev's six, plus H1's, pass on the fixed build.

**New questions for Design:** 386–398 (Open questions, "From step 4 of 29 October").

### 2026-10-29: Dev, beat-grid phase follow-up of 29 October

Built on `fix/grid-phase` from `integration` (14564619), for the gauntlet review's remaining items on the mixing fix (its scripts: `scratchpad/review-mix/`). **No new route, no schema change, no new grid version:** the change only holds grids back (a held grid is still cut on and counted in bars), so grids already at v4 keep their values; Analyse again finds one anew.
- **A rolling bass under the kick, a sixteenth off** (`core/dj/grid.py`, `BUSY_LINE_STRENGTH`, `kick_onsets`). With a bass on the three sixteenths between kicks and no clap or snare, the comb took a bass note for the beat and kept it at 0.98–0.99, 125–128 ms off (a kick at 0.9 or 0.5). The kick's line never reached `RIVAL_STRENGTH` (0.9), so nothing held it. First a rule on the low band's other even lines (two or more at 0.125 of the beat or more) held it, but it held correct grids too; the review round below replaced it with the beat's own onsets. With a clap the rolling bass is ready on the kick (16 ms).
- **The sweep** (the review's `sweep.py`, `sweep2.py`, `probe_r2.py`, `probe_offbeat.py`; integration's v4 → now): two cases change, "techno rolling 3/16 128" 0.98 at 128 ms → 0.45 (held) and "techno 3/16 bass, soft kick" 0.99 at 125 ms → 0.45 (held); every other case is the same (DnB 0.74, breaks 0.38–0.45, half time 0.50, garage 0.66, rolling-bass house 0.76–0.88, off-beat bass 0.4/0.5 1.00/0.99, techno off-beat 0.91, kick alone 0.995, clapped off-beat 0.995, clapped rolling 0.98). Off-beat bass at 0.3/0.5/0.7 over 118–132 BPM: the new rule holds none back (the 0.7 ones held at five tempos are the existing rival rule's, as on integration).
- **A restored grid follows the person's BPM** (`grid_store.restore`): Analyse again's failed decode puts the old grid back inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, at the octave of the ×2, ÷2 or reset as it is then (`follow_override`), as `save_result` does, so one made during the decode isn't undone.
- **A crash in Analyse again's decode keeps the old grid** (`grid_store.mark_analyzing_again`, `AGAIN_PREFIX`): the `analyzing` row keeps the grid it replaces in its reason; `fail_leftover_analyzing` puts that grid back at the next sweep's start (it's current, so it isn't decoded again and can't crash again) instead of an error; a plain decode that crashed is still an error.
- **Playing the working set as a mix before a mix:** verified rather than refused. While auto-mix only loads windows, is ready or has a cut set, a new next track is planned afresh: the old cut never fires into its track, its windows are let go and the decks never take it (`controller.test.ts`); during the mix itself the queue is still left alone.
- **A flaky test made deterministic:** `test_analyse_again_says_in_line_and_leaves_the_grid_until_it_is_taken` asked for a track the fake analysis doesn't hold at its gate, so the job could finish it before the test looked (about 2 runs in 5); it uses the gated track now (12 of 12 alone, and under `-n 4`).

**Review round (the same day):** the busy-line rule also held grids that were on the kick: a kick with a low reverb rumble, low toms on the sixteenths between, toms under an off-beat bass, a quiet rolling bass (0.995 → 0.45); no strength threshold separated them (wrong-phase rolling lines 0.07–0.36 of the beat, correct rumble and toms 0.33–0.64). Now the beat's own onsets decide (`kick_onsets`): in the low band (under 160 Hz) a kick's pitch falls fast (its centroid drops 0.15–0.72 mel bins from the onset to 46 ms on; a bass note or a tom −0.04 to +0.04) and its sub is short (0.05–0.10 of the onset 80 ms on; a held bass note 0.94–1.04). A four-on-the-floor grid with no backbeat and any other even line in the low band (0.125 of the beat or more; the 0.9 rival included) is held only when its beat doesn't sound like a kick; one with the backbeat on another line still moves there. Result on the review's probes (`probe_r3.py`, 124 and 130 BPM): every grid off the kick is held (rolling bass 1.0 and 1.2, 126 ms; toms on 1 and 3, 116–122 ms; a "donk" bass, 230–242 ms; an off-beat bass under rumble, 220 ms; **and the loud off-beat bass, 230 ms at 1.00, which nothing held before**); toms, toms under an off-beat bass, quiet rolling basses and an off-beat bass at 0.7 at 130 BPM go back to 0.87–0.995. **Still held though on the kick:** a kick with a low reverb rumble (both tempos) and an off-beat bass under rumble at 130 BPM: the reverb smears the kick's falling pitch and holds its sub, so its onsets don't read as a kick; they're cut on and counted in bars, only not beat-matched. The review's sweeps otherwise move only where intended: "techno offbeat bass loud" 1.00 → 0.45 (an eighth off), and the clap-less off-beat bass at 0.8 (on the kick) 0.45 → 0.54; `probe_offbeat`'s 1.0 case, an eighth off at 0.53, is held. Also: `mark_analyzing_again` is one write, and each restore at a sweep's start is wrapped (a locked database is logged, and that row fails as a plain crash; the sweep still starts); a controller test for the `ready` stage.

**Second review round:** a pluck bass (its pitch drops fast and dies fast) rolling on every sixteenth passed `kick_onsets`, so the comb's line a sixteenth off the kick was trusted: a 0.9 kick at 134 BPM was 0.589 and 115 ms off, a 0.5 kick at 118/126/134/140 BPM 0.63/0.552/0.807/0.806. `onset_shape` now gives the drop, decay and where the low band's centre ends; when no line has the backbeat, the comb's line has to sound like a kick and no other even line may sound like one too, unless that line ends clearly higher (`KICK_CENTRE_MARGIN` 0.1: a kick ends at 0.01–0.28, a pluck at 0.33–0.46; not an absolute bar, since a rolling tone bass lifts a real kick to 0.47–0.54). All six are held at 0.45 now. The reviewer's probes and sweeps (`sweep.py`, `sweep2.py`, `probe_r2`, `probe_offbeat`, `probe_r3`, `probe_r3b`, `probe_r4`, `probe_r4alt`) are otherwise unchanged; pluck off-beat basses at 0.9 and 1.5 are still ready on the kick. `fail_leftover_analyzing` leaves a row whose grid couldn't be put back as `analyzing`, its `again:` reason kept, so the next start tries again instead of losing the grid.

**Tests:** `test_grid_real_mixes.py` (a rolling bass with no backbeat is held at three levels; a kick alone, off-beat basses and a clapped rolling bass are still ready; the review round: toms, toms under an off-beat bass and a quiet rolling bass ready, off-beat basses 0.5–1.5 never trusted on the bass, rumble, off-sixteenth toms and a "donk" bass never confidently on the wrong line, `kick_onsets` on the kick and on the bass), `test_grid_store_and_worker.py` (a restore follows a ×2 and a reset made meanwhile; a crash keeps the old grid; a restore that fails at a sweep's start doesn't stop it; the M2 test now builds its ×2 the real way), `controller.test.ts` (a cut set, windows loading and windows ready for the old pair).

### 2026-10-28: Dev, step 3 (Brief D and Brief C's tray) of 28 October

Built on `feat/step3-tray` from `integration` (d9a6635b): step 3 of Design's build order of 27 October, approved by Ross, from `Row primary button (Brief D).dc.html` (34a–34c), `Where to go for what (Brief C).dc.html` (33a–33d; 33e and 33f were step 2), `DJ now (Brief E, options).dc.html` (36a only), `Mix tab.dc.html` (29a–29d), README "Added 2026-10-27" and `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-27.dc.html`, with the UX review's findings in full. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code identifiers, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. DJ now (step 5) and drag and drop (step 4) aren't part of it. **Gauntlet:** one server file changes, `core/dj/ranking.py` (on `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` already: each match's `step`, Design's 297); its tests are in `tests/mix/test_mix_ranking.py`; all the Python went in one push. **No new route, no schema change.** Interfaces: `CONTRACTS.md`, "The Mix tab" (`step`, 297, the browser's tray, `openMixWith`, Settings → Library → DJ's deep link) and "Sets" (playing a set: `from_set`, `startIndex`, Duplicate, Remove, Undo); patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` (the row's primary button, the tray's modes and Mix beside Up next, the phone's sheet in two levels, a set's header, Remove this set…, Your sets with the working set, Move… by neighbour, a mix that couldn't start, Settings → Library → DJ; "Mixes well with" marked retired); rows: `SCREENS.md`; `BACKLOG.md`, "Star tracks and mix sets" and "DJ now".

**Board by board**
- **34a, the row's primary button** (`features/dj/row-play.tsx`, `RowPlay`; the paths are the board's): **Play** (a filled triangle, white at 86%, never the accent) is the row's one visible button and plays the track now (inserted straight after the playing track and cut to, so Up next stays; with nothing playing it starts); **Play next** (the triangle and bar) sits beside it on the row's hover and focus, its 44px place kept so nothing shifts, and stays in the "…"; **Playing** (three bars, green on a 10% green wash) takes Play's place on the playing row, which has a 6% green wash and a green title, and pauses when pressed. ⇧-click on Play does Play next; a double-click on the row plays (not on its links, buttons or fields). The star is the only accent left in a row. **Tracks** (Play · Play next · star · "…", the "…" now at rest too; the accent ▶ that meant Play next is gone, UX-25), the phone's playing row has the green bars over its art.

Tested with the real `index.html` and `settings.js`.

Undo is in the notice."), and "Undone: …" in the page's own; Up next's Move to the top says "… moved to the top: it plays next". A roving-tabindex row grid is left for Brief B's keyboard model (step 4), which redefines the rows' keys.
