**Measured in a real browser** (Chromium at 1440 and 390, the app's own build served from disk with a scratch server: the real DJ, Sets and Stars routes and the real analysis job over a library of generated tracks; Ross with Sunday Set as his working set, Mira with none). Seen: Tracks' rows at rest, on hover (Play next and the outline star appear, nothing shifts) and playing (green bars, green title), and the "…" with Add to Up next; the phone's sheet in 34c's order with "Sunday Set is working"; the tray in Now playing with the key line, Up next numbered with Play and Between tracks in its header; the tray in Mix with Sunday Set played as a mix ("Playing Sunday Set · track 1 of 10 · as a mix · (Auto) into Night Ferry"), the 56px record, Up next beside it ("9 from Sunday Set"); "Mixes well with…" from a Tracks row opening Mix following Harbour Lights ("Back to Night Ferry"), with UX-7's "Already in Sunday Set · #2 · Add again"; the set's page with its tags, Play, the toggle, the "…", the playing row's green ▶ and Playing, and Remove's confirm; Your sets with the toggle; the sidebar's "Sets · Sunday Set"; the phone's sheet with one tab row, Mix's set line and Set's first run (Mira); `/settings?tab=library&section=dj` opening the DJ section. Fixed from it: Up next's header overflowed the 360px column at 1440 (Clear and Collapse were cut off; Between tracks now takes a row of its own under the tabs when there's no room); the set card said "10 tracks · 161 min" twice (the picker says it); "Add again" and the keys line's "Settings → Library → DJ" were 36px buttons that broke their sentences (inline links now); the set's playing row showed Play, not Playing; the name field was wider than the name, pushing its tags away (sized to its content); the deep link scrolled the DJ header under Settings' sticky title. Screenshots are in the session's scratch folder (`step3-work/shots`), the boards in `step3-work/boards`.

**Left out, and why**
- **Drag and drop** (Brief B, step 4): Up next's and a set's own HTML5 drag stay as they were; Send to… is a hook in the "…".
- **DJ now** (Brief E, step 5): the DJ mode, key D, the bar's DJ button, "Open DJ now", the lab's nav.
- **UX-8 beyond a set's page:** the Mix tab's Add and Add to set…'s toast write to the server at once; an Undo there needs a route that takes the added row out (382).
- **UX-27's row grid** (fewer tab stops per row): with Brief B's keyboard model.
- The Mixes panel's Camelot wheel went with it. Its "Make a playlist…" and "Export to Traktor…" are back in the Mix tab's matches "…" (the gauntlet review, below).

**Gauntlet review.** An independent review of the branch (with browser probes) found these; each is fixed and has a test.
- **H1, Undo put back an old copy:** Undo restored the rows as they were before the edit, so anything since (a track the Mix tab added, a newer copy the autosave had taken from the server) was saved over. Undo is now the inverse of the one edit on the rows as they are (a removed row back after the row that was before it, a moved row back to its place, a swap's fields back), by the row's track rather than the copy (`features/sets/undo-rows.ts`); if the row has gone it says "Can't undo: that track isn't in the set any more" and changes nothing. The toast's Undo works once and closes the toast, stays 10 s, isn't dropped as a repeat when two have the same words, and is said through a polite live region (`#toast-live`).
- **H2, a double-click on Play put the track in twice:** a second press within 400 ms is the same press (a double-click no longer plays and then pauses); asking for the track that's playing or loading puts nothing in (a paused one plays on); a Play while another track is loading takes back what it put in. A row's double-click doesn't play on the playing row or on a phone.
- **M1:** "Mixes well with…" with a station, an episode or a book playing opens Mix following the track instead of stopping it to play the track.
- **M2:** removing the working set (its page or Your sets) has the sidebar, the Mix tab and Your sets read the working set again.
- **M3:** a set's playing row (the green ▶, "Playing · N of M") is the row with the playing track nearest the queue's mark, so a row removed or moved above it doesn't move the mark; the Mix tab's "track 4 of 12" reads it the same way.
- **M4:** Play after a reload (a restored queue with nothing loaded) goes in where the queue is and plays, instead of going on the end.
- **M5:** UX-28's last `<main>`s (above), with a test that renders them inside the page's.
- **L1:** "Save Up next as a set" says the server's count (`POST /api/sets/working/start` now answers `tracks`), cuts a longer Up next to the first 500 and says so, waits while it saves, and leaves out stations, episodes and books by their kind.
- **L2:** the playing row's button is "Resume …" while paused (Brief D's rows and Up next).
- **L3:** the matches' "…" has Make a playlist from these… (17b's dialog) and Export these to Traktor… (the track the matches follow, then the matches as shown); the retired panel's CSS is gone, Swap's panel says `data-side-panel`, and the panel's `soulsync.dj.mixes.*` keys are taken out of the browser once.
- **L4:** Vibe, remembered, asks the AI only once the AI says it can be used and only while the Mix tab is shown.
- **L6:** a "Settings → Library → DJ" request isn't used up by the tab Settings shows first.
- **L7:** the Sets page's file comment is back at its top.
- **The re-review's Lows:** an Undo whose set page has gone takes its toast down with the page (`dismissToast`), and a late press says "Can't undo here: open the set to change it" (a); a move is undone to after the row that was before it, so a row put in above since doesn't leave it one place off (b); a just-started row's Playing button (Brief D's rows and Up next) does nothing while its track loads, and reads "Loading …", and Play for the loading track leaves it to start (c).

**Merged with the mixing fix** (`integration` at 14564619, "Dev, mixing fix of 28 October", below): its working set's readiness line and "Play Sunday Set as a mix" (`features/mix/working-set-mix.tsx`) sit in step 3's set card in the tray (under Add, and under "Playing …" while a set plays, where its button hides) and under the picker in the phone's Set tab, since step 3's tray has no working-set column or strip.

**New questions for Design:** 374–385 (Open questions, "From step 3 of 28 October").

### 2026-10-28: Dev, mixing fix of 28 October (Ross's report)

Built on `fix/mix-stops` from `integration` (d9a6635b). Ross, on d9a6635b plus his patches after updating past the BPM fix, Brief A and step 2: *"it's saying cant be beat matches, can't see the analyse now button, i've constructed a working set of 4 tracks in the panel, and it didn't mix any of the tracks, and just stopped after the first track ended and didn't even echo"*; then *"they have been analysed, they can't be re analysed, but they all say couldn't beat match"*, his chip reading "couldn't beat-match: I Need the Night has no steady beat". Reproduced on a scratch server (the real DJ, Sets and Stars routes and the real analysis job) with generated house in the browser.

**What broke, and why**
- **"No steady beat" on house (the grid).** House mixed as it is now, a rolling bass on the three sixteenths between kicks as loud as the kick in the low band, sent the comb a sixteenth off the kick. Step 2's sixteenth check (grid v2) moved it back correctly, but then took the track for a break: its confidence was scored on the whole spectrum against the half-beat (where the off-beat hat is) and capped at 0.45. Measured: 0.05 to 0.45, and under `MIN_BEAT_CONFIDENCE` the grid is saved as `none`/`no_beat`, so the planner said "has no steady beat" and readiness said "Can't be beat-matched". A long drumless intro or breakdown also counted against the confidence (every beat without a kick was a miss).
- **No way to ask again.** Those grids were "done" at the current version, so "Analyse" did nothing ("already done") and the line had no action for "Can't be beat-matched". The words didn't tell "no beat found" from "found, under 0.5" either.
- **Stopped after track 1, no echo.** The Mix tab's working set collects tracks; it was never Up next. Ross played one track and built the set around it in the Mix tab, so when it ended nothing was queued after it. Play as a mix (the set page) was fine: in the browser a 4-track set played through all four with every pair's fallback (cut, echo out on the decks, cut; and all straight on), as did plans that miss. Nothing in the controller left the queue stopped, but nothing guaranteed it either.

**The fix**
- **Grid v4** (`core/dj/grid.py`, `GRID_VERSION` 4): a grid the sixteenth check moves onto a kick on every beat of the bar (`four_on_the_floor`: the quietest beat's low-band onset at least 12% of the loudest's in the median bar; breaks and drum and bass measure 0.00, rolling-bass house 0.19 to 0.65) is scored as four on the floor, its contrast taken against the three sixteenths between on the whole spectrum, with no break cap; a comb that isn't steady tries the onsets a sixteenth away; the hit rate counts only the bars where the drums play (a drumless intro or breakdown no longer sinks a steady track; under 40% of the track with drums counts for less). Breaks stay under the bar (they echo out), as Design wanted. On 16 generated house tracks with a loud rolling bass (24 and 64 bars), 15 now get a constant grid ready to mix (one still drifts, at 0.4; one of the 15 sits about 38 ms early); before, most were "no beat" or held at 0.45.
- **The upgrade** (`database/music_database.py` `_promote_ready_grids`, marker `dj_grid_v4`, after `_promote_settled_tempos`): v3 grids already ready to mix (0.5 or more, 4/4), too short, failed, too long or unreachable move to v4 undecoded; every other grid (no beat, under 0.5, no confidence stored, not 4/4) is analysed again by the sweep, and reads "BPM and key · no beat grid yet" (with Analyse) meanwhile; its plan says Waiting, not "couldn't beat-match".
- **The words** (`planner._trust`, `ondemand.readiness`): a grid found but not sure enough says "Night Ferry's beat grid isn't sure enough" (readiness `beat: "unsure"`, the line "beat grid not sure enough"); "has no steady beat" only where none was found.
- **Analyse again** (an admin's own ask, `/analyse` `again: true`, `ondemand.ask(again=True)`): a done track's grid is found again, its BPM and key kept. Offered on a "Can't be beat-matched" line (not too short), the set page's line ("Analyse again" when that's all there is to ask) and the Mix tab.
- **The Mix tab's working set as a mix** (`features/mix/working-set-mix.tsx`, under the strip): "2 of 4 ready to mix" with the line's action (Analyse again / Analyse them now / Try again; Analyse mine for a member), watched only while the tab is shown, and **Play <set> as a mix** (`set-play.ts` `playSetFromPlaying`): from the playing track when it's one of the set's rows (the rest go straight after it, each pair played as a mix, what was Up next after them), else the set from its start. Hidden while the set plays as a mix.
- **Never a stopped queue** (`controller.ts` `unstick`, `STALLED_END_MS` 1.5 s): the playing track's element ended, the decks hold nothing and the player hasn't moved on: the controller starts the next track itself, whatever the plan (play on, a missed mix or cut, Cancel, waiting). The sleep timer's end of track, repeat one, shuffle and the queue's end are left alone.

**Tests:** `tests/dj/test_grid_real_mixes.py` (rolling-bass house on its kick and ready; a drumless intro; a break still a break; noise and a pad still none; `rolling_bass_house` in `grid_fixtures.py`), `tests/database/test_dj_grid_v4.py`, Analyse again in `test_analysis_indicator.py`, the new words in `test_planner.py` and `test_grid_accuracy.py`; vitest: the queue never stops (`controller.test.ts`, each plan), `playSetFromPlaying` (`set-play.test.ts`), the Mix tab's line and Play as a mix (`mix-tab.test.tsx`), the line's Analyse again and "not sure enough" (`analysis.test.tsx`). In the browser (scratch server and Playwright, not in CI): a track played alone, the 4-track working set played from the Mix tab, through all four with a cut, an echo out on the decks and a cut; Analyse again turning "2 of 4 ready to mix" into "Ready to mix · all 4 tracks".

**Gauntlet review** (independent, on 16177a2f; the reviewer's generated drum machine is `pattern_track` in `tests/dj/grid_fixtures.py`, its cases in `tests/dj/test_grid_real_mixes.py`):
- **H1, the sleep timer woke up.** `unstick` inferred a stalled queue from an ended element, so the sleep timer's end of track (which pauses and clears its flag) was followed 1.5 s later by the next track. Now the player says when the next track is owed: `onAudioEnded` sets `npAdvanceOwed` only when it hands the advance to auto-mix or a load in flight turns it away, never for the sleep timer, a station's end or the queue's end; the next load clears it; `state().advanceOwed` carries it, and `unstick` acts only on it. Tested through the real `onAudioEnded` (`media-player-automix.test.ts`) and in the controller.
- **H2, the off-beat bass.** The four-on-the-floor rule ran only after the sixteenth check moved a grid. Now it runs on every steady grid: a kick on every beat is scored against the three sixteenths between (whichever contrast says the beat more clearly) and the sixteenth check's ambiguity doesn't count against it when a clap or snare on two and four is on its beats (`backbeat_share`). An off-beat bass at 0.4 and 0.5 of the review's levels goes from 0.92 and 0.69 to 1.00; with a clap, 0.8 and 1.5 are ready on the kick.
- **M1, confident grids a beat off.** Where the low band has two lines as even and as loud as each other (a kick and an off-beat bass) and no backbeat says which is the kick, the grid is kept but held at 0.45 (`unsure_phase`), so it's never beat-matched on what may be the bass; where a backbeat sits on the other line, the grid moves there. The bass-only intros the review found an eighth off at 0.98 are now held. (Since the phase follow-up of 29 October, above, a grid whose beat doesn't sound like a kick is held too: the loud off-beat bass, an eighth off at 1.00, and a rolling 3/16 bass, a sixteenth off at 0.98, now read 0.45.) Drum and bass with a bass on every beat isn't four on the floor (its snare on every other beat, `_fast_backbeat`; L4).
- **M2, Analyse again wiped grids it didn't queue.** The grid is no longer marked when asked: the job keeps the tracks it took into the line (`DjAnalysisJob._again`; one over the profile's room keeps its grid) and finds the grid again when it takes them (`process_track(force_grid=True)`); a new analysis that fails puts the old grid back as it was, a person's ×2 and its measured grid too (`grid_store.restore`). A track in line for it says "In line".
- **M3, Play as a mix could queue a track twice.** The set's later rows go in through one call (`npQueueAfterPlaying`): a row already up next moves rather than being queued again, drawn and saved once; while a mix holds the next row, the queue is left alone and the toast says to play the set once the mix is over.
- **Low:** `dj_grid_v4` is in `_KNOWN_MIGRATION_SIGNALS` (L1); `_promote_ready_grids`' docstring says what it keeps (L2: ready v3 grids are promoted as they are, so a few that v4 would hold back keep v3's grid until Analyse again; decoding every library again was the other choice); the hit rate's "where the drums play" is any drum-sized onset in the bar, on the grid or off it, so a grid off the beat no longer leaves its own bars out (L3: the Amen fixture is back to v3's 0.38); L5 not done: the old-release fixtures are dumps made by released code, none of which had `track_grid` (the v3 → v4 upgrade is tested in `tests/database/test_dj_grid_v4.py`).
- **The review's sweep, v3 → v4 (confidence, phase error):** drum and bass, half time, garage, breakbeat and Amen unchanged (DnB 0.74 → 0.74, 3 ms; Amen 0.38 → 0.38; rolling-bass DnB and garage held at 0.45); house with a rolling bass 0.06–0.12 → 0.76–0.88 (one "beats" 0.40 → 0.88, 31 ms); off-beat bass 0.4 / 0.5 0.92 / 0.69 → 1.00 / 0.99, 0.8 0.21 → 0.45 (no clap: held), techno off-beat bass 0.43 → 0.91; bass-only intros 0.24 / 0.98 at 239 ms → 0.45 (held); the two cases above unchanged. Scripts and outputs: `scratchpad/review-mix/`, `scratchpad/fix-mix-work/final_table.txt`.

**For Ross now, on his current build:** play the set from **Library → Sets → the set → Play as a mix** (the Mix tab's working set doesn't queue anything until this fix). After the update the sweep finds his grids again by itself; Analyse again does it at once.

**Not done:** real music wasn't available here: the confidence is measured on generated tracks, so a real house track can still score under 0.5; Analyse again then says so in the new words. Breaks (drum and bass with a syncopated break) are still held under the bar by design (`BREAK_CONFIDENCE` 0.45) and echo out or play on: a decision for Ross and Design.

### 2026-10-28: Dev, step 2 (Between tracks, the names, 302's rule) of 28 October

Built on `feat/step2-names` from `integration` (d59285b9): step 2 of Design's build order of 27 October, approved by Ross, from `Where to go for what (Brief C).dc.html` (33e, 33f; 33a–33d read for context only: they're step 3), `Auto-mix (Up next, decks).dc.html` (26a–26j, 26d corrected), README "Added 2026-10-27" and `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-27.dc.html`, with the UX review's findings in full. Ross's rules hold: nothing says "crossfade" (Between tracks is Off / Auto mix; a pair that can't be beat-matched cuts, echoes out or plays straight on), and DJ now (laptop and desktop only) isn't part of this step. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code identifiers, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** one server file changes, `core/dj/planner.py` (on `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` already), with `api/dj.py`'s route docstring; `tests/dj/test_planner.py` is marked `gauntlet` as a whole; all the Python went in one push. **No new route, no schema change.** Interfaces: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Auto-mix stage A" (302's rule, 303, 304) and "Auto-mix stage C" (the settings, the queue's fields, the browser's plans), and "Setup check"; patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, "Auto-mix in the player"; rows: `SCREENS.md`.

**Board by board**
- **33e, Between tracks in its places.** Settings → Library → DJ gets a card (`BetweenTracksSettings`, in `index.html`'s new `#ss-between-tracks-library`, a settings group before "BPM, key and energy"): "Between tracks", two radio cards side by side, **Off** ("Each track plays to its end; the next starts after it.") and **Auto mix** ("Beat-matched where both tracks are ready; an echo out or a cut where they can't be; straight on where nothing's known."), no radio ring, the selected fill and border; "Style when Auto can choose" (Auto's own pick ▾) and "Key lock in DJ now" (Off ▾); the note "Not for podcasts, audiobooks or radio. Auto mix needs SYNCHRO.S on https or opened on its own computer. Kept per browser." They replace 26h's cards: Player settings keeps the small control and "Style when Auto can choose and key lock: Settings → Library → DJ" (a link that opens the tab and focuses the card). **26h's "Mix automatically" is gone** (33e has no row for it): Auto mix mixes by itself, a stored Off is turned on once, and Mix now still mixes early. **The same control, small** (`BetweenTracksSwitch`: a two-way segmented control, 34px segments, the selected fill and border, one tab stop with the arrows) is in Up next's head (a new `[data-automix-slot="upnext-head"]` over the rows, while the queue has a library track auto-mix could take), the player's "…" (first under Playback), Player settings and the Mix tab (Brief C's item 4: under the primary, before the keys line). DJ now's header gets it with Brief E.
- **312 in 33e.** Where the browser can't run the decks, Auto mix is `aria-disabled` in every place with "Needs https or localhost" beside the control (on the card, as its sentence; the long reason as the title); a browser with no AudioWorklet says "This browser can't mix". The setup check gets an info row, **This browser · Auto mix** (an "Info" badge and icon, never a warning; the browser's own answer, so not in the route's counts), and Settings → Connections a card, "Opening SYNCHRO.S on https", saying what this browser can do and how (a reverse proxy with a certificate, or `localhost` on its own computer). **Changed by the Gauntlet review (below):** Auto mix is no longer disabled there, since a cut on the phrase needs no decks; each place says "Cuts and straight on only here · blends need https or localhost".
- **320 and UX-21, Off said once.** Up next's head says "Auto mix is off: tracks play straight on · Turn it on" under the control (Turn it on is the accent text button; "Needs https or localhost" in its place where the decks can't run), while a coming pair would mix with Auto mix on; a set played as a mix doesn't (its chips play whatever the setting). A set's page says the same line once above the list; its chips are dimmed (62%) with no line of their own, and their accessible name ends "(Auto mix is off: plays straight on)". Play as a mix uses the set's chips whatever the setting, as before.
- **33f, the glossary, applied** to every place a person reads, in the React app, `index.html` and the legacy player: "set" for mix set (Add to set…, Save as a set, New set, Your sets, "Start a set to collect tracks", the toasts and empty states); "Working set" for the Mix tab's "Working on"; "Add to Up next" for "Add to queue" (277; the artist page, the dashboard rails, the mirrored playlist, Up next's empty line); "Mixes well with" for the Mix tab's "Matches" sentences; "Auto mix" for DJ mix (the shortcuts list); the chip's name is "How Night Ferry goes into Static Bloom: …", the picker's words, not "Transition from"; "ready to mix" for the picker's "both tracks are analysed"; the visuals' "A 2-second blend" (nothing says crossfade). Code identifiers, file names and storage keys keep their names (`add-to-mix-set.tsx`, `mix_set`). Brief A's analysis words (Not analysed, being analysed) and Design's own 31c "matches follow once it's ready" stay as drawn. The menus and lists whose shape is step 3 (the Mixes panel, the set header's working-set control) keep their places.

**Design's 302, the one rule, and what changed in the planner** (`core/dj/planner.py` `_fallback`). "Ready to mix" is the shared `beatmatch_problem` rule from Brief A (beat confidence at least 0.5 and 4/4). For a pair that can't be beat-matched (Auto, a style that needs a beat-match, or any style whose outgoing grid isn't ready):
- **the outgoing track ready to mix: a cut on its last phrase**, `status: "cut"` at the last phrase line at or before the end of its mix-out region (the outro's last kick), the next track from its start; the player cuts, no decks, so it works without https. A tempo gap, an incoming track with no steady beat, no grid, too long, unreadable or with a BPM set by hand its grid doesn't match: all cut here ("Couldn't beat-match: cutting on the phrase · the tempos are 9.7% apart");
- **only the incoming track ready: an echo out into it**, on the decks, placed **by time** a second before the outgoing track's end (its length, kept to the analysed length), never on a bar counted on a grid it hasn't got; the incoming track comes in on its own first downbeat ("…: echo out · Sand Script has no steady beat"). Only for an outgoing track with no beat found, too short, a beat not steady enough or a BPM set by hand its grid doesn't match, and a length known;
- **neither: plays straight on** ("…: plays straight on · …"); also a track not in 4/4 on either side (304: "Sweet Waltz isn't in 4/4"), an outgoing track too long to analyse, unreadable or missing ("A Mix is too long to analyse", "… can't be opened", "… couldn't be analysed": the decks couldn't read it either), and an outgoing track whose ending wasn't analysed (only its first 10 minutes; `end_not_analysed`, "the end of A Mix wasn't analysed"), which before was estimated: no cut on an estimated grid.

What it replaced: echo out wherever the outgoing track had a grid (even one not trusted: a break held at 0.45, not 4/4), echoed on its outro's first phrase by that grid's bars, and play on without one; Auto's echo out for a gap over 8% or an incoming grid it couldn't trust. `status: "cut"`, kept in the shape for 302 since analyse on demand, is now 302's cut. Kept: Auto's own cut or echo out on a key clash (a pair that can be beat-matched), a person's cut or echo out while the outgoing track is ready, album-in-order play-on (quiet: not a failure, no sentence, no chip), `bpm_mismatch` (now one of the reasons above), the waiting state. Everything that touches audio stays safe: nothing without a reliable grid is cut by bar count, and the only time-placed move is the echo at the very end. The server's `why` is Design's sentence; the browser says it from one place (`features/engine/plan-words.ts`): the chip names the move (**Cut**, **Echo out · 4 bars**, **Straight on**, amber, "couldn't beat-match: <why>" on its line, the sentence on its title), the picker's Auto line says the sentence (its head "8A → 9A, +12 BPM: the tempos are 9.7% apart"), the bar's countdown says it under "Cutting to Signal Fire in 6 bars…", set play uses the same chips, and the lab's plan and "Nothing for the decks: …" say it. Measured on real grids (the scratch server below): Night Ferry → Signal Fire (124 → 136 BPM) cuts at bar 72, the end of its outro; Sand Script (a pad, no beat) → Night Ferry echoes out at 120.0 s of 121; Sweet Waltz either way plays straight on.

**Answer by answer**
- **283:** a chip describes a pair. Up next's choice is kept with the track it was chosen for (`automix.to`), and a set's chip with the set's next row that plays (`mix_set.next`); when the next track is another (a reorder, an insert, a removal), the gap is Auto's again. A stored choice without `to` still applies. (A set's own rows already reset on the server.)
- **303:** read with "Both fine": a long blend still starts on the latest phrase that leaves its bars before the outro ends, in the main part when the outro is shorter; where its bars don't fit before the outro ends at all (a short track), it becomes a short blend of 8 bars and says so: the chip "Short blend · 8 bars" with "auto · the outro is short", the picker's Auto line "Short blend: the outro is short" (`shortened`). See 360.
- **304:** an unsure "one" (downbeat confidence under 0.3) keeps its style with the warning dot and "auto · Not sure where the bars start" (`unsure_bars`); not 4/4 is 302's straight on.
- **307:** "Key lock in DJ now" is a setting under Between tracks, off by default (`soulsync-dj-keylock`); DJ now's hand control comes with Brief E, and Auto keeps the planner's rule.
- **312:** above (33e).
- **314:** an automatic mix counts down over its last 16 bars in the tray and the bar with Cancel, as Mix now's does; so does 302's cut ("Cutting to Signal Fire in 14 bars…", "Couldn't beat-match: cutting on the phrase · …"). Until then the bar keeps "in 2:58" beside Mix now. **UX-15:** the countdown chip is no longer a live region; one polite region says each state once: "Mixing into Static Bloom in 16 bars" (or "Cutting to …") when the countdown starts, "Mixing now", "Now playing Static Bloom", "Mix cancelled", "Back to Night Ferry". The bar count is visual only.
- **316:** Cancel plays the track out straight on: no cut, no decks, no fallback; the chip goes back to its style for the next pair. Its button says so to a screen reader ("Cancel: Night Ferry plays out straight on").
- **317:** after Back, the next track comes in by 302's rule: the controller cuts on the outgoing track's last phrase (`lastPhraseCut`: its grid ready to mix and its ending analysed), else it plays straight on. Never a crossfade.
- **319:** Play as a mix comes before Export to Traktor… on a set's head, and a draft has it (the draft's rows as the queue; "Save it to Draft too?" writes the draft in this browser). The question is asked once, in place; after "Not now" it isn't asked again while the picker is open.

- **324:** a phone's mixing sheet says 308's words under it, one line: "If a call or the lock stops the sound, SYNCHRO.S cuts to the next track when it's back." Player settings no longer has the warning.

Seen: Settings → Library → DJ with Auto mix and Off; Up next's head with the control and the Off line, and Turn it on; Up next with Auto mix on and each 302 move on its chip (Echo out from Sand Script, Cut into Signal Fire, Straight on into and out of Sweet Waltz) and the picker with the sentence, kept on screen; the "…" and Player settings with the control; the bar's "in 2:18", then "Cutting to Signal Fire in 14 bars…" with the sentence and Cancel, and the tray's countdown; a set with Auto mix off (dimmed chips with "auto", the line once, Play as a mix before Export); the page opened over plain http on a LAN address (every Auto mix disabled with "Needs https or localhost", the Connections card, the setup check's row). Fixed from it: the chip's fallback line was cut to "auto · cou…" in Up next's column (it now drops "auto", 26g's shape); the picker's head said the sentence twice; the "…" menu's control wrapped under its label; the legacy Settings page's form styles boxed the two selects (now 33e's quiet "Auto's own pick ▾"); over http Up next said "Needs https or localhost" twice (the Off line leaves it to the control); Player settings' link didn't open the folded Organization section the card sits in (it does now). Screenshots are in the session's scratch folder (`step2-work/shots`), the boards in `step2-work/boards`.

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of 13397f6c: 1 High, 3 Medium, 7 Low; fixed in b1fec18f, the server, and de24ad92, the browser; the reviewer's probes, `review-step2/probe/cut.probe.test.ts` and `probe1.py`, are tests now):
- **H1, a cut plan had no upper bound.** A plan that arrived after its cut point (Play next, Add to Up next, a reorder, Auto mix turned on in the tail) or a seek into the outro cut at once with no countdown. Arming now needs the track before the cut point (within 0.25 s); past it the pair is missed and the track plays out straight on; a cut already set that finds the track sought past it lets go; a seek back before it arms it again. Tests `[review H1]` (late plan, seek past, a set cut sought past, the seek back).
- **M1, the echo out by time used the library's duration.** A file the library says is 100 s and decodes to 121 s was echoed 22 s early. A `status: none` grid row now gives its decoded length (`analysed_s`, `truncated`), the planner places the echo on it where the whole track was analysed (`_echo_length`), and the browser places a time-placed plan (`out.bar: null`) a second before the element's own end once that's known (`placedPlan`). Tests on both sides with mismatched lengths.
- **M2, a chip saved to a draft from Up next was lost on the draft's next edit** (the editor never took the draft's outside changes and wrote its old rows back). The editor now takes in the transitions written to the draft from outside (rows still the same track at the same place) before it writes again. Test: the draft open, "Save it to Draft too?" from Up next, then a rename; the transition stays (it fails without the fix).
- **M3, a 302 cut ignored the downbeat's confidence.** Under 0.3 it still cuts (on a beat) but sets `unsure_bars` and the note; the chip keeps the amber dot and says "Not sure where the bars start · couldn't beat-match: …".
- **Low:**
  - **Mix automatically's migration** runs once a browser (`soulsync-dj-auto-migrated`), and whoever had it off is told once in Up next's head: "Mix automatically is gone: Auto mix now mixes every pair. Choose Off to play tracks straight on." with Off and Dismiss (`soulsync-dj-auto-gone`). The legacy player never read the setting.
  - **One truth over http:** a 302 cut needs no decks, so Auto mix stays a choice on a page that can't run them, and every place says so: the card and the three small controls "Cuts and straight on only here · blends need https or localhost", a pair that needs the decks shows "Straight on" with "blends need https or localhost", Mix now on a cut works there, the setup check's row "Over plain http from another computer, Auto mix can only cut on the phrase or play straight on: …", and the Connections card the same.
  - **A browser without the decks on https** is told apart from plain http in the Connections card and the setup check ("This browser can't run Auto mix's decks…").
  - **A ready track with no mix-out region** plays straight on with "Night Ferry has no outro to cut on" (`no_outro`), not "the end … wasn't analysed".
  - **"Add to Up next"** on the artist page's phone actions.
  - **An echo out placed by time counts down in seconds** ("Mixing into Static Bloom in 12 s…"), there being no bars to count.
  - **Mix now on a cut plan** cuts on the outgoing track's next phrase line ahead, counted down, where its grid is ready to mix; else now, as before.
- **Re-review** (of 13397f6c..18b312d3, which confirmed H1 and M1–M3; three Lows in the cut's timing, each a test from the reviewer's `r2.probe.test.ts`): a cut timer that fires late (a busy page) still cuts up to 1 s late, and only a seek past the point (`soughtPast`, set by the seek itself) makes it straight on; a rate change while it waits works the wait out again; Mix now inside the automatic cut's last 2 s lets that cut's timer go (each timer has a number, `cutGen`; only the newest acts), so it cuts once, on the new phrase; any seek back before a missed cut's point arms it again (a cut needs no windows, so not only before the run-up).

### 2026-10-28: Dev, privacy (VPN for Soulseek and torrents, the privacy check) of 28 October

Built on `feat/privacy-vpn` from `integration` (d59285b9), from BACKLOG "Privacy: a VPN for Soulseek and torrents, and a privacy check" and Ross's yes of 28 October: Proton VPN with port forwarding for **both** Soulseek (slskd) and torrents, behind a kill switch, with a privacy check in SYNCHRO.S. Ross hasn't confirmed his Proton plan, so everything works without port forwarding and says so. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and settings keys keep SoulSync's.

**The design, and why**
- **Gluetun** (`qmcgaw/gluetun:v3.41.3`, the July 2026 release; pinned) in an opt-in override, `docker-compose.vpn.yml`, so nobody else is affected. A `COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml:docker-compose.vpn.yml` line in `.env` makes the usual `docker compose up -d` (and the drives helper's) use it. Needs Compose 2.24 (Docker Desktop 4.27): `ports: !reset []` takes slskd's own ports away, and `configs: content:` writes Gluetun's auth file from `.env`.
- **Proton over WireGuard:** `VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER=protonvpn`, `VPN_TYPE=wireguard`, `WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY`, `SERVER_COUNTRIES`, `VPN_PORT_FORWARDING` and `PORT_FORWARD_ONLY` from one `.env` value, `PORT_FORWARDING` (true/false; Gluetun's parser takes both words, checked in gosettings v0.4.4). Port forwarding needs a key made with "NAT-PMP (Port Forwarding)" on (Gluetun's Proton page).
- **slskd and qBittorrent** run with `network_mode: "service:gluetun"`. Gluetun takes the network names `slskd` and `qbittorrent`, so SYNCHRO.S's `http://slskd:5030` keeps working, and publishes their web pages (5030, 8080). The control server (8000) isn't published. qBittorrent is in its own files (after the review): `docker-compose.vpn-qbittorrent.yml` adds a new one, `docker-compose.vpn-qbittorrent-existing.yml` moves the one already in the compose file and changes only its network.
- **The forwarded port, slskd:** slskd 0.25+ has Gluetun built in (`SLSKD_VPN`, `SLSKD_VPN_PORT_FORWARDING`, `SLSKD_VPN_GLUETUN_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000`, `SLSKD_VPN_GLUETUN_API_KEY`). It polls `/v1/publicip/ip` and `/v1/portforward` every 2.5 s, waits for the VPN before signing in, disconnects from Soulseek when the VPN isn't ready, and applies the forwarded port through its run-time options overlay (`PATCH /api/v0/options`'s `soulseek.listenPort`): no restart, no slskd.yml edit, and slskd's docs say the Soulseek server is told the new port without a reconnect. Read in slskd's source at 0.26.2 (`Integrations/VPN/VPNService.cs`, `Core/OptionsOverlay.cs`, `docs/config.md`).
- **The forwarded port, qBittorrent:** Gluetun's own documented `VPN_PORT_FORWARDING_UP_COMMAND`/`_DOWN_COMMAND` (`wget … /api/v2/app/setPreferences` with `listen_port={{PORT}}`; the image has GNU wget). It needs "Bypass authentication for clients on localhost" in qBittorrent; the guide says so, and the check says so when the port doesn't match. The new-qBittorrent file uses `lscr.io/linuxserver/qbittorrent:5.2.3`. Transmission and Deluge get the check (their port is read) but aren't wrapped; the guide lists them under "What the VPN doesn't cover".
- **The kill switch** is Gluetun's firewall (`FIREWALL_ENABLED_DISABLING_IT_SHOOTS_YOU_IN_YOUR_FOOT=on`, the default, written out). Its source (`internal/firewall/enable.go`) sets every iptables policy to DROP and accepts only loopback, established connections, the Docker network, the VPN server's endpoint and the tunnel; proved live below.
- **Gluetun's control server** answers only `GET /v1/vpn/status`, `GET /v1/publicip/ip` and `GET /v1/portforward`, and only with `GLUETUN_API_KEY` (an `apikey` role; v3.40+ makes every route private by default). It can't stop the VPN or read its settings.

**The privacy check** (`core/privacy_check.py`; routes in `api/setup_check.py`)
- `GET /api/setup/privacy` (admin) reads Gluetun, slskd's options and VPN state, and the torrent client's listening port at once, and answers a state, a tone, one plain sentence and four setup-check rows: **VPN**, **The address peers see**, **Forwarded port**, **Kill switch** (`CONTRACTS.md`, "Privacy check"). The sentences name what was checked: "slskd and qBittorrent go through Proton VPN (Netherlands) · port 51234 forwarded · if the VPN drops, they stop"; "… · kill switch not confirmed" (amber); "Connected, no forwarded port: downloads work but fewer people can reach you"; "SYNCHRO.S can't reach slskd: if Gluetun was restarted on its own, restart it too"; "VPN is down: slskd and qBittorrent are stopped until it's back"; "Not using a VPN: slskd shows your home IP"; and "slskd still looks signed in to Soulseek while the VPN is down: check it". Only a fully proved state is green.
- **Up** means Gluetun's status is `running` **and** it has a public address: measured, Gluetun says `running` with `public_ip: ""` while a dead tunnel is retried.
- **The kill switch** can't be read from Gluetun, so it's proved from where the services are (as tightened by the review): only Docker service names that lead to the same private address on SYNCHRO.S's own Docker network prove a service is inside Gluetun's network. Such names leading apart mean "its own container" (red). Anything else, a LAN address, a name with a dot, the Docker host, a NAS publishing both ports, or slskd's own integration at 127.0.0.1, proves nothing ("kill switch not confirmed", amber). If slskd still looks signed in while the VPN is down in two reads a few seconds apart, the card says to check it.
- **The home address** is looked up only when the admin turns on "Compare with my home IP" (off by default): one `GET https://api.ipify.org?format=json` through the outbound guard (`guarded_json`, 4 KB cap, no redirects), kept an hour, five minutes after a failure. The card says what it asks and how often.
- **Addresses** appear only in the privacy route's `addresses` (admin-only), never in a row's sentence or the headline, and port numbers only in the rows' `data`, so "Copy for a bug report" and the Assistant's summary of the last run don't carry them.
- **Settings:** `privacy.gluetun_url`, `privacy.gluetun_api_key` (a sensitive path: encrypted at rest, redacted from `GET /api/settings` and from the API's settings, never sent back), `privacy.home_ip_lookup`, `privacy.vpn_name` (default "Proton VPN"). `PUT /api/setup/privacy/settings` (admin, JSON, 4 KB) checks the address (http/https, host, port, nothing after) and the key.
- **Setup check** runs the four rows in its full run once `privacy.gluetun_url` is set ("Privacy (VPN)"); never in the local run.
- The torrent adapters gain a read-only `listen_port()`: qBittorrent `/api/v2/app/preferences`, Transmission `session-get` `peer-port`, Deluge `listen_ports`.

**The card** (`webui/src/routes/setup-check/-ui/privacy-card.tsx`): under the tiles, above the groups. The headline in a tinted strip with its status word, the addresses ("Peers see … (Proton VPN, Amsterdam, Netherlands)", "Your home connection …"), the rows (the same row component as the groups), "Check privacy again", and "Privacy check settings" (Gluetun's address, the key as a password field that keeps a saved key when left empty, "Compare with my home IP"). Not set up, it says why it matters, names `Support/VPN.md` and opens the settings; with neither Soulseek nor a torrent client set up it's one line with the settings folded. "Run the check again" in the header refreshes it too. Its rows are filtered out of the groups (questions 368–373).

**The guide:** `Support/VPN.md` (Proton key with NAT-PMP, the `.env` lines, starting, the card, what to do when it's red, Tailscale, the media centre), with a pointer in the README.

**Tested, and what couldn't be**
- **pytest** (`tests/test_privacy_check.py`, 55 tests): fake Gluetun, slskd and torrent answers for up, down, running-without-an-address, no port, a mismatched port (each service), slskd in its own container, a leak, Gluetun unreachable, something else at the address, a refused key on each route, the key header, not set up (no request made), the home address (same, different, off: never asked; once an hour; retry after failure; garbage; the outbound guard), no address in any row or headline, the setup-check wiring, the address rules, the routes (admin only, the shape, saving, clearing the key, bad values, a form, 413) and the three adapters' `listen_port()`. Four are `gauntlet`: admins only, the look-up never runs when off and at most hourly, no addresses in shareable text.
- **Live, with Docker** (a local daemon in the build container): Gluetun v3.41.3 started with a WireGuard endpoint that can't answer. From a container in Gluetun's network, a web server outside Docker didn't answer (timed out) while the same request from an ordinary container got it, and a TCP connect to 1.1.1.1:443 failed: the kill switch. `iptables -S` inside showed `-P INPUT/OUTPUT/FORWARD DROP` and only lo, established, the Docker subnet, the endpoint's UDP port and `tun0` accepted. The control server with the VPN file's role: the three routes 200 with the key, 401 without it, with a wrong one, and for `/v1/vpn/settings`. The real check against that Gluetun said "VPN is down…", and with a wrong key "Gluetun refused SYNCHRO.S's key". slskd 0.26.2 inside Gluetun's network: its options show `integrations.vpn`, it logs "Waiting for VPN client" and doesn't sign in, and the check found it inside, with the kill switch OK. The whole VPN file on Ross's Mac compose (`tests/drives/fixtures/ross-mac-compose.yml`) with a probe in SYNCHRO.S's place: `docker compose up -d` started gluetun, slskd and the probe; the probe resolved `slskd`, `gluetun` and `qbittorrent` to Gluetun's address, reached slskd's API through it and Gluetun's control server with the key; slskd's 50300 was no longer published.
- **`docker compose config`** passes for the VPN file over Ross's Mac compose, the scratch home compose and the repository's `docker-compose.yml` (slskd uncommented), with and without the `torrents` profile; without `GLUETUN_API_KEY` or `WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY` it stops with "Set … in .env".
- **Not tested here:** a real Proton connection, NAT-PMP port forwarding, slskd following a real forwarded port, Gluetun's qBittorrent command against a real qBittorrent, and Docker Desktop on a Mac (the build container is Linux with no IPv6; slskd there needed `SLSKD_HTTP_IP_ADDRESS=0.0.0.0` and no HTTPS, which Docker Desktop doesn't). Proton's own help pages couldn't be reached from the build, so the Proton menu names in the guide follow Gluetun's Proton page; Ross should check them.

**Checks:** ruff (whole repository); pytest on the new file, `tests/test_setup_check.py`, the torrent adapter, settings-redaction, clients and debug-info tests; `pytest -m gauntlet` (449 passed). Web: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check`, vitest (the setup-check route tests gain seven for the card) and `npm run build`. `core/privacy_check.py`, `api/setup_check.py`, `docker-compose.vpn.yml` and `vpn.env.example` are added to `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt`, and `docs/GAUNTLET.md` lists the privacy check's scenarios. **Gauntlet territory** (it edits a person's set-up and makes an outbound call): it wants an independent review before Ross's build.

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of 1ef7cef1; fixed in 3c190848, the check, 9fd206ef, the compose files, and the web and docs commits after them; each finding has a test or a `docker compose config` run):
- **H1, a shared address read as "inside Gluetun".** A LAN IP or host name SYNCHRO.S used for both slskd and Gluetun (a NAS publishing 5030 and 8000, `mac.local`, a Tailscale name) turned the card green while slskd used the home IP. Now only Docker service names (one label, not an address) that resolve to the same private address on a network SYNCHRO.S's own container is on (its routing table) count; anything else is "not confirmed". Tested: a NAS name, a LAN IP, `mac.local`, a Tailscale name, `host.docker.internal`, and service names that resolve to a LAN machine.
- **H2, slskd's integration at 127.0.0.1 counted as proof.** slskd on the host (or with `network_mode: host`) and Gluetun's 8000 published answers the same while its traffic goes out directly; slskd's own docs say the integration doesn't route traffic. It's supporting evidence in `how` now, never proof; the old loopback test became `test_slskd_reaching_gluetun_on_loopback_is_not_proof`.
- **M1, a dead or unread service still got "protected".** A service SYNCHRO.S can't reach, or whose listening port it can't read, makes the port and kill-switch rows amber and the headline not green: "SYNCHRO.S can't reach slskd: if Gluetun was restarted on its own, restart it too" (`service_unreachable`) or "… · couldn't read qBittorrent's listening port" (`not_confirmed`).
- **M2, the API's settings list.** The Gluetun key is redacted from the API's settings like the web settings (one entry added to `api/settings.py`'s list).
- **M3, a qBittorrent already in the compose file.** The `torrents` profile hid it (or left it on 8080 beside Gluetun's), and the VPN file's volumes replaced its own. qBittorrent now has two files: a new one, or the existing service moved into Gluetun's network with only its network changed (`ports`, `networks`, `hostname`, `dns` and `extra_hosts` reset; its image and volumes kept). Without either file an existing qBittorrent stays as it was and Gluetun doesn't publish 8080. `docker compose config` passes on Ross's compose, the home compose, the repository compose, a base with qBittorrent (both ways) and a base whose slskd sets `hostname` and `dns`.
- **M4, coverage.** The guide lists what the VPN doesn't cover (YouTube, usenet, indexer searches and SYNCHRO.S's own `.torrent` fetches, the streaming services, metadata, Transmission, Deluge and aria2), and the headline names what was checked ("slskd and qBittorrent go through …").
- **L1, the 15-second deadline** is kept: the pool is shut down without waiting (`cancel_futures`), and a late source is "didn't answer in time", never dropped. Tested with a 2-second slskd and a 0.5-second deadline.
- **L2, Gluetun's answers:** no redirects (the key would go with them), a 16 KB cap, and only Gluetun's own status words (anything else reads `unknown`).
- **L3, the settings save** checks every value before writing any (a 400 changes nothing) and takes a printable-ASCII key only.
- **L4, "running" with a stale address:** Gluetun's `/v1/publicip/ip` carries no time (its `PublicIP` model has none), so freshness can't be checked; Gluetun empties it while the tunnel restarts, which the check already reads as down. "Still signed in while the VPN is down" now needs two reads 4 s apart within the deadline; one alone is "Run the check again in a minute" (amber).
- **L5–L7, the guide:** a newline before appending to `.env`; `.gitignore`'s env patterns anchored to the repository's root (`/.env`, `/.env.*`, `/*.env`), so `webui/.env.*` isn't caught; after a solo Gluetun restart, `docker compose up -d --force-recreate slskd qbittorrent`.
- **L8,** the card folds to one line when neither Soulseek nor a torrent client is set up (`services_configured`).
- **L9,** port numbers are out of row text (in `data`), so copied reports and the Assistant's summary carry none.
- **Checks after the fixes:** ruff; pytest on the privacy, setup-check, settings-redaction, clients, MCP and AI-settings tests (74 in `tests/test_privacy_check.py`, 8 of them `gauntlet`); `pytest -m gauntlet` (457 passed); web `tsc`, `npm run check`, the setup-check vitest (33) and `npm run build`.

**Gauntlet review, second round** (a re-review of 1ef7cef1..9edbac60, which confirmed H2, M1–M4 and most of the Lows fixed; each item has a test or a `docker compose config` run):
- **H1, case A (host networking, macvlan or ipvlan, outside Docker).** There the home network is a directly connected route, so two LAN names for one machine (slskd outside the VPN on it, Gluetun's 8000 published) still read as one container. Names now prove a shared network only when SYNCHRO.S asks Docker's embedded DNS (`127.0.0.11` in `/etc/resolv.conf`), the two names differ (`slskd` and `gluetun`; one name for both, `http://gluetun:5030` included, is amber, and the guide says so), and the addresses are on a network of SYNCHRO.S's own that isn't the home network: the network carrying SYNCHRO.S's default route counts only inside Docker's own address pool, 172.16.0.0/12 (a compose network on Docker Desktop is there; Unraid's br0 or a Synology macvlan isn't). Tested: LAN names with and without Docker's DNS, one name for both, no Docker DNS, a Docker network beside the LAN, and the routing-table reader.
- **H1, case B (a name with several addresses).** Every address of the service's name must now be one of Gluetun's; an old slskd container still on the shared network makes it "the name leads to more than one container" (amber). Tested.
- **L-a,** while slskd looked signed in once with the VPN down, the headline says "VPN is down, and slskd looked signed in to Soulseek for a moment: check again in a minute".
- **L-c,** the qBittorrent and slskd wrappers also reset `dns_search`, `dns_opt`, `mac_address`, `links` and `domainname`. The guide says a qBittorrent on another network (an `arr` network with Sonarr and Radarr) leaves it, and gives the `docker-compose.vpn-local.yml` lines that put gluetun on that network with the name `qbittorrent` (checked with `docker compose config`).
- **L-d,** the new-qBittorrent file and the guide warn loudly that over an existing `qbittorrent` service it replaces its settings and save path; use the `-existing` file.
- **Ross's defaults:** `vpn.env.example` shows `PORT_FORWARDING=true`, `SERVER_COUNTRIES=Netherlands` (any country with Proton P2P servers works; the UK has fewer) and the existing-qBittorrent `COMPOSE_FILE` line.
- **Checks:** ruff; pytest on the privacy (81 tests, 10 of them `gauntlet`), setup-check, settings-redaction and clients tests; `pytest -m gauntlet`; `docker compose config` on Ross's compose, the home compose, the repository compose, a base with qBittorrent (both ways), one whose slskd sets `hostname` and `dns`, and one whose qBittorrent is on an `arr` network with `dns_search`, `mac_address`, `domainname` and `links`.

**Gauntlet review, third round** (a check of 80e074c6):
- **M, the env example's active line.** `vpn.env.example` made the existing-qBittorrent `COMPOSE_FILE` line active, which stops `docker compose up -d` for the whole stack on a compose file with no `qbittorrent` service. The slskd-only line is active again, the two qBittorrent lines are commented and explained, and Ross's existing-qBittorrent line is in his own instructions instead.
- **L, a home network inside 172.16.0.0/12.** The pool exception now also needs the default route's interface to be a Docker bridge: its kernel driver, read with ethtool's GDRVINFO (no privileges), must be `veth`. macvlan, ipvlan or an interface that can't be read stays "not confirmed". Tested (case A3: macvlan, ipvlan and unknown).
- **L, the amber advice** no longer suggests `http://gluetun:…`, and says (as does the guide) that a `soulsync-network` on a 192.168.x subnet can't be told from a home network, so amber is expected there.
- **The guide** says that with the arr-network lines gluetun's firewall lets slskd and qBittorrent reach the arr network's containers: local traffic, not a leak.

**Questions for Ross:** which Proton plan (Plus or higher for port forwarding)? Does he use torrents, and with which client (the file ships qBittorrent)? Which country for the VPN server?

**New questions for Design:** 368–373 (Open questions, "From privacy … of 28 October").

### 2026-10-28: Dev, Brief A (the analysis indicator) of 28 October

Built on `feat/brief-a` from `integration` (1b82f612): step 1 of Design's build order of 27 October, approved by Ross, from `Analysis indicator (Brief A).dc.html` (31a–31g), README "Added 2026-10-27" and `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-27.dc.html`. It replaces analyse on demand's interim indicator everywhere (333). SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** `api/dj.py`, `core/dj/ondemand.py` and `core/dj/worker.py` change (all on the list already); the new server tests (`tests/dj/test_analysis_indicator.py`) are marked `gauntlet`; all the Python went in one push. **No schema change:** the line's order, reasons, who stopped it and each step's start are in memory, like the line itself. Interfaces: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Analyse on demand"; patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, "Analysis indicator"; rows: `SCREENS.md`.

**Board by board**
- **31a, the line in every state.** One component, `AnalysisLine` (`features/dj/analysis-line.tsx`), in three sizes. The line: a 4px bar on 10% white filled in accent-light by step (n of 6; the tag write counts as energy's), hidden from screen readers, over one 14px sentence with an 8px dot (green ready, 40% white has data or in line, accent-light analysing, amber waiting / stopped / can't be beat-matched, red failed) and the action link. Every state's words are Design's: Ready to mix · beat grid and phrases; BPM and key · no beat grid yet (Analyse); Not analysed · next sweep in about 25 min (Analyse now; the server now says when the next sweep comes); In line · 3rd (Move up, admins; it puts the track at the very front); Analysing Canopy · reading audio (1 of 6) … phrases and sections (6 of 6); Waiting for 3 downloads to finish, for the import to finish · 42 files, for Music HD · it's away (Drives), for the move to finish (Open the move), for tags to be written; Analysis stopped by Ross · 14 in line (Resume); Couldn't be read · won't auto-mix; plays straight on (Try again, admins: a new `retry`); Skipped, 2 h 14 min is too long to analyse · plays straight on (neutral; the server sends the length); Can't be beat-matched · no steady beat · echo out or cut (also "not in 4/4", "too short"). State 10: "Canopy is ready to mix", green and full, held 4 s and said once by a polite live region, then state 1 (or nothing where the page hides a ready track). State 11 is the set's line (31b).
- **31b, a set with tracks not ready, the topbar's count and its popover.** The set's head carries the line: "Analysing Canopy · beat grid (5 of 6) · 11 more in this set" with Analyse all 12 (admins; Analyse mine for a member), "3 tracks aren't ready to mix" with Analyse them now (or Try again when only failed ones are left), "Ready to mix · all 40 tracks" when all are. Under the head, a warning Notice counts what isn't ready by kind and says what Play as a mix does meanwhile. Every row has its marker ("Ready", "In line · 1st", "BPM and key", "Analysing · beat grid (5 of 6)" with the row's bar, "Couldn't be read · plays straight on"); "Being worked out" goes. The topbar's count (`AnalysisCountHost`, first in the top bar's end, desktop) is the 44px pill "Analysing · 14 in line", on the selected fill while it runs, "Analysis idle · next sweep in about 25 min" plain, "Analysis stopped by Ross" or a wait with the amber dot. Its popover holds the track and "Tidewater · 5 of 6 · 14 in line after it", the six-step checklist with each finished step's time ("1.2 s") and "now", the line in order with why each track is there ("you added it to Sunday Set", "Mira asked", "Up next"), "and 10 more from the sweep · Finding beats · 1,240 of 4,980" (to Activity), Stop the sweep (admins; Resume when stopped, Start now when idle) and Open Activity.
- **Triggers' toasts (31b, 31d).** Add to set and the working set say once where the track went in line: "Added Canopy to Sunday Set · #7 · analysing, 2nd in line" (the ask answers with its place); a ready track says only "· #7". The player's Play next and Add to queue do the same with Auto mix on ("Playing next · analysing, 2nd in line"), through a hook the automix host sets.
- **31c, where else it lives.** Tracks: the page's line is the sweep's ("Analysing Canopy · key (3 of 6) · 2,140 of 4,980 ready to mix"; idle "Analysis idle · next sweep in about 25 min · …" with an admin's Analyse all, Resume when stopped) and every row not ready to mix has its marker; "Ready" isn't shown there. Up next: the Waiting chip's line is the analysis ("Analysing Canopy · beat grid (5 of 6)" with its bar, "In line · 2nd"), Analyse now only when the track isn't in line; once ready the chip turns to its style in place, as before. The Mix tab: the line heads the matches column ("Analysing Night Ferry · tempo (2 of 6) · matches follow once it's ready", Cancel while it's in line), over 29d's card, then "Night Ferry is ready to mix". The lab: the chosen track's line, each picker row's marker ("Ready" too there, "Can't be beat-matched · no steady beat", "In line · 2nd" with an admin's Move up). DJ now isn't built: `IncomingLaneAnalysis` is its hook ("Analysing Static Bloom · energy (4 of 6) · the lane fills in when the grid is there"), and the server takes `source: "dj_now"`. Activity's DJ row shows the sweep's line with "Finding beats · 1,240 of 4,980".
- **31d, the member's view.** Members get Analyse mine where admins get Analyse now; a member's own track in line says "In line · 1st · yours go first" once theirs are ahead; "Analysis stopped by an admin" with Resume shown disabled; the popover lists only the member's own tracks, counts the rest ("and 11 more in line"), says "yours: 2, both next" and offers "Analyse mine first" (`{mine: true}`: every track of theirs in line moves to the front).
- **31e, a phone's sheet.** The Mix sheet's head shows the line with "2 in line" at its right (also for a match being analysed); a Waiting chip is two lines, the chip then its analysis.
- **31f, the Activity sheet as a phone's popover.** The phone's top bar has no room for the count: Activity heads with the same panel while something is analysed (a card with the title, the bar and "beat grid (5 of 6) · 14 in line"; the checklist and the line at 44px; "Analyse mine first" for a member).
- **31g, reduced motion and the glossary.** With reduced motion the bar is six cells lit to the step, with no width transition and no pulse on the dot. The words follow the glossary: "ready to mix" only with a grid at this version, "BPM and key" never called analysed, "plays straight on", nothing says crossfade.

**325 (queue_position as an ordinal that follows members' jumps, and why each track is in line): yes, built.** `/progress`'s `position` was already an ordinal counting the asked-for line first; it now follows each jump. A person's own ask (Analyse these, Analyse now, Analyse mine, a lab pick, the Mix tab's button) goes to the front of the line, after the other person-asked tracks and before every page's; a page's track a person then asks for moves up; `{mine: true}` moves all of a profile's tracks there; an admin's Move up (`/start {track_id}`) puts one at the very front. Each ask says why (`source`: asked, set with its id, up_next, mix, lab, dj_now, page), kept per profile that asked. `/progress {line: true}` returns the line in order (8 at most) with each track's why and who: a member sees only the tracks it asked for and a count of the rest, an admin every track in its library and who asked (by name); another profile's set is never named; tracks outside the caller's library are only counted. Only the popover and the phone's Activity panel ask for the line, so the pages' polls stay as cheap as before.

**326 (step times per track): cheap, published.** Measured: keeping each step's start costs 4.4 µs a track (seven clock reads; `scratchpad/brief-a-work/measure-326.txt`), against 2–7 s of analysis for a 3½-minute track (here: reading audio 0.9–1.1 s, tempo 0.5–4.8 s, key 0.3 s, energy and phrases under 0.1 s, beat grid 0.2–0.5 s), and reading them for the progress route 2.5 µs. `current.times` has each finished step's seconds (to 0.1), `now` the step it's on and `elapsed` its time so far, only for a caller whose library holds the track (as its title). The popover shows "< 0.1 s" for the quick ones. A grid-only run (the top-up) has no tempo, key or energy times: those rows show done with no time.

**Measured in a real browser** (Chromium at 1440 and 390, a scratch server with the real routes and the real analysis job over a library of generated tracks: three analysed with grids, one with BPM and key only, six generated mp3s never analysed, one with no steady beat, a 2 h 14 min file and an unreadable one; profiles Ross, admin, and Mira K., member). Seen: a set's line stepping through each track's steps with "N more in this set", rows going In line · 3rd → Analysing · step (with the row's bar) → Ready; the topbar's count and popover with real step times; Tracks' line and markers; the Mix tab's line, then "Glass Harbour is ready to mix" green for 4 s with the matches filling in; Up next's Waiting chips with the analysis in their line (two lines on a phone); the lab's markers; Stop from the popover ("Analysis stopped by Ross", "by an admin" for Mira); a tag write in progress ("Waiting for tags to be written"); Mira's view with Analyse mine first moving her tracks up; Activity's panel at 390; six cells with reduced motion; the settled set (can't be beat-matched, couldn't be read, too long). Fixed from it: the Mix tab's line moved into the matches column (31c); the phone's two-line chip; the popover 6px under the top bar; "0 in line" for the last track; between tracks the sweep's line says how many are left; a too-long track counted as unreadable in the set's Notice; the lab's picker buttons stretching beside a taller picker. Screenshots and board-by-board comparisons are in the session's scratch folder (`brief-a-work/shots`, `brief-a-work/compare`).

**Also fixed:** `DjAnalysisJob.join` raised when a run's end had made the next run's thread but not started it (a flaky on-demand test under load); it now waits.

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of fa9ad96f; fixed in 360a2f5e, the server, and 59cf363f, the browser; each finding has a test):
- **M1, "ready to mix" said yes where the planner wouldn't mix.** The grid was saved from beat confidence 0.3, the planner beat-matches from 0.5, and a meter not worked out passed. Now one rule, `core/dj/grid.py` `beatmatch_problem`, is used by the planner, by `/progress`'s `ready` and `beat` (an unsteady beat is `no_beat`, Design's "no steady beat"; a meter other than 4/4 is `not_4_4`) and by the status count (`grids.ready`, which Tracks' "X of Y ready to mix" and the popover read).
- **M2, Tracks polled every 1.5 s through a whole-library sweep.** Its head reads the sweep at the slow pace (the topbar's summary watch). The poll goes fast only while the page's own rows are being analysed, asked for or near the front. The test renders the page.
- **L1, switching the Mix tab to a track that's ready already announced "is ready to mix".** The flash and the live region are keyed by track id.
- **L2, Cancel could take the run's total under its done.** The total drops only for a track an ask added to this run and the run hasn't taken.
- **L3, `left` never reached 0 when a track was put back.** A deferred or stale outcome counts as finished.
- **L4, Cancel was offered on tracks the viewer hadn't asked for, and refused while analysis was off.** Each queued or waiting entry has `yours`; Cancel shows only with it, says so when it takes nothing out, and is answered before the `off` 409.
- **L5, Move up with nothing running started a full sweep and lifted a Stop.** `/start {track_id}` is the admin's own ask: it starts a run for that one track (the Stop stays), reads "you asked", is limited to one a second, and says when there's nothing to move up (409 `nothing_to_move`).
- **L6, an admin's line could come back empty.** The first 8 tracks were cut before other libraries' were filtered out. It now filters by library first, in batches of 50, then cuts.
- **L7, Activity mounted the phone's panel on every screen.** It's mounted behind the phone media query (768px), so a desktop doesn't watch the line.
- **L8, `join` could still race on Python 3.13, and spun forever on a thread whose start failed.** It catches the unstarted thread's `RuntimeError` and retries for at most 5 s.
- **L9, the numbers in a wait.** A member's "Waiting for 3 downloads" counts its own downloads. The import has no owner, so only admins get its file count; a member gets the words without the number.
- **L10, the order of the line (the coordinator's decision).** Up next's next transition (`soon`: the playing track and the next, two at most a profile) goes to the very front, after only an admin's Move up. Then come people's asks, then pages' asks, then the sweep. Tested: a member's 200-track Analyse mine doesn't delay the admin's next mix.
- **L11, smaller fixes.** Analyse mine keeps why each track is in line. A set's "Analyse all N" counts the tracks it asks for. A `/progress` call without `line` no longer copies the line under the lock.
- **Tests.** The topbar's slow pace checks its 20 s interval, the 200 cap has a route-level test, and the join window has its own test.

**Gauntlet review, second round** (a re-review of fa9ad96f..be1e6624, which confirmed M1, M2, L1, L3–L9 and L11 fixed; each item has a test, the reviewer's probes among them):
- **N1, Up next pairs let a member jump earlier asks.** A pair dropped a tier but kept its place, so it headed its new tier. Each track now gets a number when it enters a tier, and the line sorts by tier, then number. A track that changes tier, up or down, goes to the end of its new tier; Move up's tier stays newest first. The probe (Sam asks 2 and 3, Mira sends four pairs) now puts Mira's earlier pairs behind Sam's asks.
- **L2, Cancel after a retry.** A track the run has taken leaves the "added by an ask" set, so a Cancel after its retry doesn't take it off the total again.
- **L10, three gaps.** `soon` with `playlist_id`, `mine` or `cancel` is refused (400). A new pair that needs no analysis still puts the old pair back in its own tier. Up next asks for the pair as `soon` again after a Stop turns it away, so after Resume it still goes first. An ask also keeps the order it was sent in, whatever order the library's rows come back in.
- **L5, two small fixes.** Move up retries once after a 429's Retry-After. Over the 200 cap it says "You have 200 tracks in line, the most at once: Move up waits until some are done." (409 `over_limit`).
- **L8.** `join`'s 5 s wait for an unstarted thread counts from finding it unstarted, not from the call.
- **L6.** The popover's line reads the library in batches of 100, four at most a poll; past that, other libraries' tracks are only counted.

**Left out, and why**
- "Analyse all" on Tracks' line while a sweep runs: a running sweep already covers the library, and `/start` can't widen a run that's under way; it shows when nothing runs (352).
- The set's line sits under the head's buttons, not right-aligned beside them: the set's header is redrawn by Brief C (33c) (353).
- Cancel in the Mix tab only while the track is in line: the track being analysed finishes its few seconds (354).
- No count for a drive move or tag writing, and no drive name for a move ("Waiting for the move to finish"): the move's target and the Write Tags batch's count live in `web_server.py`'s state and the move's JSON; not worth a gauntlet change for a number (355).
- 31d's "another member's track" (the line with no action): `/progress` never answers for a track outside the caller's library, so a member's pages only show their own library's tracks, each with Analyse mine.
- Stop still empties the line (337 is still Design's): "Analysis stopped by Ross · 14 in line" counts what people asked for since the Stop (351).
- DJ now's panel (Brief E): only its hook.

**New questions for Design:** 349–357 (Open questions, "From Brief A of 28 October").

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/dj` (new `test_analysis_indicator.py`: the front of the line, Analyse mine, Move up, `source` and `set_id` checks, what a member and an admin see of the line, another profile's set and library never named, the 200 cap and the held 409, step times and their scope, readiness, who stopped it, the next sweep, the waits' counts, the sweep's tracks left, the toast's place, Cancel, Try again, the join race), `tests/activity`, `tests/sets`, `tests/mix` (962 pass) and `pytest -m gauntlet` (425 pass; after the Gauntlet review's fixes, with `tests/stars` and the DJ database and import tests, 1,009, and 437 for `-m gauntlet`); tsc, `npm run check` (no errors), `npm run build`; vitest on the touched features (new `analysis-line.test.tsx`: every state of the line, the marker, the count, the popover for an admin and a member, the phone sheet, reduced motion, the ready flash and live region; the words for every state in `analysis.test.tsx`; updated Tracks, sets page, Mix tab, chips, automix host, lab, picker, Add to set, album header and Activity tests) and the full suite once (691 files, 12,105 tests: all pass but two that timed out under the full run's load, the Mix tab's Vibe test and the wishlist's nebula test, which pass on their own). CI on the fork: sanity-check, webui and Gauntlet green; webui-visual red as expected (the baselines predate the indicator).

### 2026-10-27: Dev, UI latency of 27 October

Ross: "things hang for ages in the UI, you click on things and they take ages to load", meaning SYNCHRO.S and slskd's own page (localhost:5030). His setup: Docker on a MacBook Air (7.7 GB), slskd beside it and very busy (64 GB down, 42 GB up), SYNCHRO.S at about 25% of a core and 1.1 GB when sampled. Built on `perf/ui-latency` from `integration` (20742433), with `integration` (2434c7a7) merged in. Spotify was left to its own fix.

**How it was measured.** A scratch server with the production settings (`gunicorn.conf.py`: one worker, 8 threads), a fresh database with 12,000 tracks, 4,000 albums, 2,000 artists, 25,000 history rows, 20,000 plays, 8,000 downloads and 300 wanted tracks, and 100 generated three-minute tracks for the DJ sweep. slskd was a fake one on the port the config names: it logs every request, holds 2,500 transfers (1.5 MB as JSON, a busy install that keeps its finished transfers), finishes searches after 3 s, and can answer late or not at all. In each 2-minute run: Chromium with a Home tab and a Downloads tab open; a probe imitating those tabs (their polls at the measured intervals, a page load every 15 s as Chromium makes it, 36 API calls six at a time, and one cheap request a second to show queueing); a SQLite probe timing how long the write lock stays taken; slskd's request log; py-spy dumps. The sandbox's four cores were shared with other work (load 9–22), so latencies are comparable within a pair of runs, not absolute.

**What it found, by impact.**
1. **slskd hung or slow stalled every page (thread exhaustion).** `/status` (asked on every page load) checked slskd on the request thread whenever its 2-minute cache ran out, and each request ran its own check: `check_connection` tries three endpoints, each with a 120 s timeout. With slskd not answering and three downloads running, py-spy found 7 of the 8 request threads waiting in `get_status` → `check_connection`; page loads took 116 s, the one-a-second request timed out at 60 s (26 timeouts in 2.5 minutes), and Chromium gave up on 109 of 299 requests. With slskd answering in 5 s, each refresh cost the page that asked 5 s. Two smaller holds of the same kind: `/api/downloads/all` (the Downloads page's 2 s poll) read slskd's list once per downloading row with `tasks_lock` held, which the monitor, every download worker and `/status` also need; and `get_cached_transfer_data` held its lock across the slskd read, so callers queued behind a slow one.
2. **SYNCHRO.S was itself a heavy load on slskd.** slskd's whole downloads list (`GET transfers/downloads`: every user, folder and file slskd still holds) was read by each poller on its own: the download monitor every second, the completion checker every second while downloads await completion (twice: directly and through `get_all_downloads`), the socket's batch push every 2 s (even with nothing queued), the dashboard's speed every 10 s, the Downloads page's poll. Measured: **194 full lists a minute** (3.2 a second, 600 MB in two minutes from the fake's 1.5 MB list) with one Home tab and three downloads waiting for completion; 83 a minute with three downloads searching; **33 a minute with a tab open and nothing queued**; 45 a minute with downloads running and no tab. slskd builds that list from its database each time, and its own page asks for it too, which is why its page slowed.
3. **The DJ sweep, a little.** With the sweep running (in this process, librosa holding the GIL) the one-a-second request's p95 went 78 → 306 ms and a page load 0.35 → 0.74 s on a loaded host; 22 → 61 ms and no change on a quieter one. The sweep already rests as long as it works and waits while downloads run, so with slskd busy downloading it is usually paused. Not changed (see below).
4. **Not causes.** SQLite: WAL and a 30 s `busy_timeout` were already on; the write lock was never held 0.5 s or more in any run (longest 0.30 s, under 1% of tries busy). Heavy endpoints: every GET the main pages make answers in under 100 ms one at a time against the 12,000-track database (slowest `/api/downloads/all` 84 ms, `/api/discover/personalized/daily-mixes` 76 ms); no N+1 found. The two Deezer Discover calls took 6 s each here only because the sandbox has no network (retries). A full page load asks 40–120 API calls (most of them the shell's badges and the video counters, on every page), which is why a full page load is 0.3–0.7 s here rather than tens of ms.

**What changed, with before and after** (same scenario each time):
- **`/status`'s two network checks** (media server, download source) run on threads of their own, one at a time each (`_start_status_probe`, `_probe_media_server`, `_probe_download_source` in `web_server.py`). The request that starts a check waits at most 2 s for it (`STATUS_PROBE_WAIT_SECONDS`), so a quick answer shows at once as before; requests while it runs answer from the cache; a slskd check over 15 s counts as not connected (`STATUS_SLSKD_PROBE_SECONDS`). slskd hung, downloads running: page load **116 s → 0.29 s** (p50; 2.1 s at most, the one request that waits for its check), one-a-second request p95 **60 s → 0.05 s**, timeouts **26 → 0**, Chromium's failed or slow requests **109 → 6**, all six the CDN scripts the sandbox can't fetch.
- **One read of slskd's list for every poller.** `SoulseekClient.get_transfers_snapshot` keeps one copy: a read younger than 2 s (`TRANSFERS_FRESH_SECONDS`) is reused; readers that arrive mid-read wait for that read (one per event loop, shielded, so a caller giving up doesn't cancel it for the others); a request thread takes a copy up to 30 s old at once and leaves the read to refresh it (`SLSKD_LIST_STALE_OK_SECONDS`), waiting at most 5 s when there's none (`SLSKD_LIST_REQUEST_WAIT_SECONDS`); a successful enqueue or cancel through `_make_request` drops the copy so the monitor sees the new transfer; a failed read isn't kept. The monitor, `get_all_downloads`, the completion checker (`get_download_status`), `get_cached_transfer_data` and the dashboard read it (`DownloadOrchestrator.get_soulseek_transfers`, `_slskd_transfers`). The batch push doesn't read slskd when there are no batches. Downloads running, two tabs: **83 → 28.5 lists a minute** (255 → 88 MB in two minutes; 29.5 after the review fixes below. The 2 s copy bounds the reads that the event loop shared by the pollers makes for them to one per 2 s while the list doesn't change; on top come a fresh read after each enqueue or cancel, the cancel-confirmation reads, reads from other event loops (the Clients tab, audiobooks, streaming prepare) and the video monitor's own. So the 194 case falls to about 30 plus those, not to a hard 30); idle with a tab: **33.5 → 6** (103 → 18 MB; the dashboard's speed every 10 s). Page load p50 with downloads running 0.57 → 0.36 s, one-a-second p95 127 → 48 ms. Worker CPU in that run 30% → 19% of a core (parsing the list three times a second).
- **The Downloads page's poll** reads the live list once, before `tasks_lock` (`build_unified_downloads_response`), and only when a row is downloading, searching or queued; `get_cached_transfer_data` holds its lock only to read and write the cache.

**SYNCHRO.S's calls to slskd, after this change** (requests a minute measured with three downloads searching and two tabs open; with none queued and one tab; with no tab, nothing runs but the 6-hourly and hourly jobs):

| Call | Who and when | Active | Idle tab |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET transfers/downloads` (whole list) | the shared copy: download monitor (1 s while a batch runs), completion checker (1 s while downloads await completion), batch push (2 s, with a tab and a batch), Downloads page (2 s), dashboard speed (10 s, with a tab), each plugin's `get_all_downloads`; video downloads read it on their own every 3 s while a video slskd download runs (`core/video/slskd_download.py`) | 28.5 (was 83; up to 194) | 6 (was 33.5) |
| `GET searches/{id}/responses` | each running search, every second for up to slskd's timeout + 15 s; each answer holds every response so far | 72 (three searches) | 0 |
| `GET searches/{id}`, `POST searches` | each search: state after 15 polls when quiet; one create per track searched | 9 | 0 |
| `GET rooms/joined`, `POST rooms/joined`, `GET rooms/joined/{room}/messages`, `GET conversations` | the chat push, every 6 s while any tab is open (the join only when not joined); chat auto-prove every minute | 39 | 39 |
| `GET server` (`server/state`, `session`) | `/status`'s check, every 2 minutes at most, now one at a time | 0.5 | 0.5 |
| `POST transfers/downloads/{user}`, `POST users/{user}/directory`, `DELETE transfers/...` | enqueue, album folder browse, cancel: per download | as needed | 0 |
