| `GET searches`, `DELETE searches/{id}` | Clean Search History (hourly) and Full Cleanup (12-hourly): SYNCHRO.S's own searches are kept until there are over 200, then trimmed to the newest 50; nothing deletes a search when it finishes | hourly | hourly |
| `DELETE transfers/downloads/.../completed`, `GET/PUT shares`, `GET application` | Clean Completed Downloads (every 5 min, its own transfers), share rescans after imports | rare | rare |

**Left as they are, for a later change.** Search polling fetches every response so far once a second; asking `searches/{id}` (its counts) first and the responses only when they grew would cut that, but the search loop's tests pin the current sequence and its value depends on real result sizes. Searches are deleted only when over 200 (to 50), never when done. The video monitor's 3 s read could share the copy (its enqueue and cancel now drop it). The DJ sweep could analyse in a child process (no GIL contention), which would touch `core/dj/worker.py`, where the DJ fixes are under way.

**For Ross's side:** give Docker Desktop at least 4 CPUs and 4 GB (Settings → Resources); in slskd, keep finished transfers for less time (its `retention` settings for transfers, so the list SYNCHRO.S and slskd's page read stays short) and limit upload slots or speed if uploads (42 GB) are taking slskd's time; keep slskd's page closed when it isn't needed (it polls the same list).

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of eb8a90ad: 1 High, 3 Medium, 8 Low). Each fix has a test in `tests/test_slskd_shared_transfers.py` or `tests/test_status_probe_off_request_thread.py` that fails on eb8a90ad; the reviewer's `probe_snapshot.py` and `leak_mem.py` are the H1 and M1 tests.
- **H1, the in-flight table leaked a list per throwaway event loop.** It was keyed by loop with the task as value, and the task holds its loop, so entries never went, each keeping its whole list (the Clients tab's `asyncio.run` every 10 s, audiobooks every 20 s per book, streaming prepare). Entries now leave as their read ends (in the read itself and in a done callback; a read cancelled before it ran is swept). Measured on the scratch server, the Clients tab's slskd poll 40 times with the copy over 2 s old each time: eb8a90ad **+166 MB, then +182 MB** for the next 40 (4.1–4.5 MB a call); now **+25 MB while the app warms up, then −4 MB**. Test: 40 `asyncio.run` reads leave no entry and grow under 5 MB (tracemalloc).
- **M1, a reader after an enqueue could join a read from before it** and get the old list. Each read carries the list's generation; a reader joins only a read of the current generation.
- **M2, the completion checker** (`/api/downloads/status`) waited 15 s, not 5, then re-read slskd through `get_all_downloads` with no limit only to drop those rows. It waits 5 s for slskd's list and asks only the other clients (`engine.get_all_downloads(exclude=('soulseek',))`, 10 s at most, `DOWNLOAD_CLIENTS_WAIT_SECONDS`).
- **M3, a slow check was cached as "slskd down" for 2 minutes**, which switches off every slskd read. A check with no answer now keeps what was known and is asked again in 15 s (`STATUS_RETRY_SECONDS`); a failed one is asked again in 15 s too. In hybrid mode the sources are checked side by side, each with its own 15 s (`DownloadOrchestrator.check_connection_within`), so a slow torrent client no longer uses up slskd's time.
- **Re-measuring found a side effect of M3:** with slskd hung it now stays "connected", so once the copy was over 30 s old each Downloads poll waited the full 5 s for the same hung read (1–5 s each, 19 slow in 2.5 minutes). A request thread that finds a read already under way now takes the copy it may use, or nothing, at once: the Downloads poll with slskd hung is **255 ms at most**.
- **L1:** the exploding orchestrator stub in `test_backend_review_downloading.py` now explodes in `get_soulseek_transfers`. The pre-existing half is fixed too: the monitor reads "slskd didn't answer" as unknown and skips the tick for up to 180 s (`SLSKD_UNANSWERED_GRACE_SECONDS`), so a busy slskd no longer gets every download over 90 s restarted as stuck with its peer blacklisted; not when slskd refuses connections (slskd down: handled as before), and after 180 s the old handling resumes. (Narrowed in the second round, N1: only Soulseek tasks wait; the tick runs.)
- **L2:** no consumer changes the shared list: the transfer cache and the completion checker work on copies of each file's dict.
- **L3:** the two cancel-confirmation loops (slow-download replacement, album pending cancel) drop the copy before each read.
- **L4:** one transfer-cache build at a time: callers that find it old while a build runs wait for that build (bounded) and get its result; the build never holds the lock and its engine call is bounded.
- **L5:** the old enqueue endpoints (`users/{name}/downloads`, `users/{name}/enqueue`, `transfers/{name}/enqueue`), any transfer-changing request that failed or timed out, and the video downloader's enqueue and cancel (`invalidate_all_transfer_snapshots`) drop the copy.
- **L6:** the "30 a minute is the most" claim above now says what the copy bounds.
- **L7:** a media-server check still running after 20 s (`STATUS_MEDIA_PROBE_SECONDS`) is reported as not connected; no second thread starts on top; its own answer is used when it comes.
- **L8:** the dashboard's speed is a read of now (3 s at most, as before the first change), not the copy its own last tick left.

**Re-measured after the review fixes** (same scenarios): downloads running, two tabs: 29.5 lists a minute (90.7 MB in two minutes), page load p50 0.29 s, one-a-second p95 41 ms; slskd hung: page load p50 0.26 s (2.2 s for the one that waits for its check), p95 26 ms, no timeouts, the Downloads poll 64 ms p50 and 255 ms at most; idle with a tab: 5.5 lists a minute. Still synchronous on a request thread and left as it is: the dashboard's setup card asks slskd for its shares (8 s timeout, twice) at most every 2 minutes until slskd shares something, then never again.

**Gauntlet review, second round** (a re-review of eb8a90ad..2d9ec25d: round one's findings verified fixed; 2 Medium, 3 Low, 1 follow-up). Each fix has a test that fails on 2d9ec25d.
- **N1, an unknown slskd list skipped the whole monitor tick**, so in hybrid mode YouTube, Tidal and torrent tasks got no completion pass and no stuck handling for up to 180 s. Now the tick runs: only Soulseek tasks get no stuck/error decision while slskd's list is unknown (`_slskd_unanswered`, per episode of up to 180 s, reset when slskd answers). "No answer" is now told from "an error answer": `_make_request` records slskd's status code in a context variable, so a 401 or 500 (slskd is there) is handled as before, like refused connections. Test: hybrid, slskd not answering, a finished YouTube download goes to post-processing in the same tick while a 10-minute-old Soulseek task is left alone.
- **N2, a hung slskd could stay "connected" for good**, every check ending without an answer. The last state is kept at most 60 s after the last real answer (`STATUS_UNKNOWN_KEEP_SECONDS`), then it's not connected; connected again as soon as a check gets an answer.
- **L7 follow-up, a check that never returns blocked every later one.** Plex, Jellyfin and Navidrome's connection checks go through requests with HTTP timeouts (Plex 30 s per request, Jellyfin at most 20 s, Navidrome 30–60 s); a standalone library's check is `os.path.isdir`, which has none (a hung network mount). A check still running after 120 s (`STATUS_PROBE_ABANDON_SECONDS`) is now given up on and a new one starts, at most two left running per kind; the old one's answer, if it comes, is ignored.
- **N3, progress rows blinked out after each enqueue or cancel** while the next read was under way (and a burst could keep them empty). The Downloads page now reads a display lookup (`get_cached_transfer_data(display=True)`, `StatusDeps.get_display_transfer_data`) that may hold the last list slskd sent; batch status, which decides failed or cancelled from the rows, still reads only the current copy.
- **N4:** a transfer-cache waiter never gets a lookup over 15 s old (`{}` instead), as the builder.
- **N5:** each /status check run has an id; only the current run publishes, once, and the overrun report applies only while it hasn't published.

**Third round** (a re-review of 2d9ec25d..27ebbf64): the monitor's read of slskd's list had no time limit, so with slskd hung each tick still waited up to aiohttp's 120 s and the other sources' completions with it; it now waits at most 10 s (`SLSKD_LIST_MONITOR_WAIT_SECONDS`; the read is shielded and carries on for the others) and counts the rest as "didn't answer". And the answered-or-not flag was one field on the client, which another failed read could overwrite between the monitor's read and its check (turning "no answer" into "answered", the direction that restarts healthy downloads); it now comes back with the read's own result (`get_transfers_snapshot(with_answer=True)` returns `(list, answered)`). Tests: hung slskd, a YouTube completion handled in the same tick in under 5 s; the reviewer's `answer_flag.py` race (each reader gets its own read's answer); and the cap of two given-up `/status` checks.

**Re-measured on the second round's head** (same scenarios): downloads running, two tabs: 29 lists a minute, page load p50 0.29 s, one-a-second p95 35 ms; slskd hung: page load p50 0.34 s (2.2 s for the one that waits for its check), p95 50 ms, no timeouts, the Downloads poll 69 ms p50 and 289 ms at most, the only failed browser requests the three CDN scripts the sandbox can't fetch.

**Tests:** new `tests/test_slskd_shared_transfers.py` (five pollers share one read; a fresh copy reused and an old one read again; `get_all_downloads` twice is one read; a failed read isn't kept; `stale_ok` answers at once and refreshes; a caller timing out doesn't cancel the read; an enqueue drops the copy; a read in flight during a change isn't kept; the Downloads page reads once and outside `tasks_lock`, and not at all without live rows) and `tests/test_status_probe_off_request_thread.py` (six `/status` with slskd hung answer within 3 s with one check; a quick check answers in the same request; a check over its budget counts as disconnected; four transfer-cache callers with a slow slskd each give up after their own wait). `tests/test_websocket_idle_gating.py`: the batch push needs a batch, and skips slskd without one. The `/status`, transfer-cache and Downloads-page tests fail on the old code. After the third round: 53 tests in the two new files. ruff; pytest on the slskd, Soulseek, status, websocket, performance, downloads and video slskd tests and every test that imports the changed modules (3,692 after the third round, all pass; after the second, all but one timing test, `tests/downloads/test_download_pool_and_healing.py::test_more_threads_really_do_run_more_at_once`, which failed once with the sandbox at load 13 and passes on its own; in round one `tests/video/test_parity_batch.py::test_probe_failure_fails_open` did the same, as it reads the sandbox's real free disk space), and `-m gauntlet` (322; one timing failure in a loaded run, all 322 on the rerun).

### 2026-10-27: Dev, drum and bass BPM (half-time) fix of 27 October

Built on `fix/dnb-bpm` from `integration` (20742433, rebased onto 17cea646 after analyse on demand merged), from Ross's report: "the bpm for much of the drum and bass tracks looks to be registering as half time or close to. DnB is notoriously known for 170+ bpm and it says most are in a region of 80-90bpm". The classic tempo octave error, fixed for drum and bass and its cousins, with a person's Double BPM / Halve BPM for what the analysis still gets wrong. **Gauntlet:** a schema change (three `track_dj` columns and a one-time upgrade step), a new route scoped to the caller's library, and the rule every library file goes through: `core/sample/tempo.py` is added to `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` (`api/dj.py`, `core/dj/store.py`, `core/dj/grid*.py` and `database/**` were already there); the scope, body, stickiness and upgrade tests are marked `gauntlet` (`docs/GAUNTLET.md`, "Double BPM / Halve BPM"). Routes: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Double BPM / Halve BPM", and the DJ data and stage A sections. Nothing in `core/dj/worker.py` changes (analyse on demand was rewriting it while this was built): the rule lives in the analysis, the grid and the store the sweep already calls.

**The cause.** Every BPM the library shows came from a tracker that leans towards 120 and was then trusted:
- **The analysis** (`core/sample/analyze.py:178`, DJ stage 1 and Sample Studio): `librosa.beat.beat_track` with its default tempo prior around 120 BPM. 87 is nearer 120 than 174 is, so drum and bass at 172–176 came out at 86–89 (every drum and bass fixture below).
- **What's kept** (`core/dj/store.py:51–56`, `decide`): the file's tag, else enrichment (`tracks.bpm`: Deezer often says 87 for drum and bass), else that analysis; and what's shown (`store.py:233`, `_BPM_SQL`) was only that value: the beat grid's BPM was never shown (300 wasn't built).
- **The grid** (`core/dj/grid.py:579–581`): its tempo is the DJ data's BPM (`worker.py:433` and `:505` pass it as `bpm_hint`), folded into 70–180 (`_tempo`, `:233–252`) and trusted: a half-time hint gave a half-time grid. Its own scan (`_scan_tempo`, `:374`, with `_prior` at `:366`, and `_estimate_bpm` at `:199`) leans to 124 within an octave either way, so 87 and 174 score almost the same.

**The fix: the octave from evidence** (`core/sample/tempo.py`, one rule for the analysis and the grid):
1. Only a reading of 70–100 or 140–200 is looked at; 100–140 is never changed (neither its half nor its double is a dance tempo).
2. A fast reading is kept: a tracker that leans to 120 and still says 174 heard the fast pulse, and the known failure is the other way round. A slow genre (hip hop read at 180) halves it unless the backbeat is at the fast tempo (soul at 150 with a snare on two and four stays 150). The analysis says why (`tempo_basis`): only `genre` or `backbeat` count as evidence that a fast result is fast; `kept` (the tracker's word) and `range` don't (after the review, H1).
3. A slow reading is doubled when the **file's genre tag** is drum and bass or a cousin (drum & bass, dnb, jungle, neurofunk, liquid, breakcore, footwork, juke, halftime …), or dubstep, trap, grime or drill when the double lands at 125–160 (a slow genre, hip hop, R&B, reggae …, keeps it slow unless the backbeat is at the double: reggae at 160 read as 80 is 160); when it's **under 78** (DJ software's BPM range: dubstep's 70 is 140); or when **the backbeat is at the double**: the snare's band (160–2500 Hz) alternates on every beat of the fast reading (two and four of a 174 bar) rather than on every other beat (two and four of an 87 bar). At 87, a drum and bass snare falls on *every* beat; at 180, a hip-hop snare falls on every fourth. It's measured sixteen fast beats at a time and the medians decide (a tracker that slips a beat, a pickup or an odd bar swaps the positions; one fold over a whole track washed hip hop's backbeat out, and a 10-minute hip-hop track came out at 178 until it was windowed: a test keeps it). The fast backbeat leads by 2.76–13.3 times on the drum and bass fixtures, at 30 s and at 10 minutes, and by at most 0.79 on everything else; it must lead by 1.5.
- **In the analysis:** `analyze_track` passes librosa's reading and its beats through the rule (`ANALYZER_VERSION` 5). The backbeat costs one more mel spectrogram, only for a slow reading that genre and range haven't decided.
- **In the grid** (`GRID_VERSION` 3): a slow tempo, **the hint included**, is tested at double the same way (the range rule only for the grid's own scan; a hint has already been through it or is a tag). A fast hint is never halved, and a hint is folded only from 200 up (a 180–200 tag stays where it is; after the review, M1). A grid at 140–200 whose backbeat is on every other beat counts its beats on the whole spectrum as well as the kick (half its beats are snares); without that, drum and bass grids at the right tempo scored 0.16–0.35 on the kick alone.
- **What's kept and shown** (`core/dj/store.py`): **300 is built on the server.** The BPM shown is a person's (`override`, below), else the grid's measured one once the track has a grid at this version (`grid`; tags no longer win over a measured grid), else the tag, enrichment or analysis; `bpm_tag` carries the tag for the title "Tagged 87". `decide` also keeps a tag or enrichment at half the analysis's fast reading at the analysis's octave (Deezer's 87, analysed 174), only when that octave came from evidence (the genre or the backbeat, `track_dj.analyzed_bpm_basis`), never halving one. Browse's range and sort, Mixes well with, the Mix tab, sets, the planner and Export to Traktor all read this one value.

**Ideas weighed and left out:** the high band (hats) and onset density: hip hop's sixteenths at 90 are drum and bass's eighths at 180, and off-beat hats over a kick on every beat (`dj_track(90)` in the existing tests) read as a backbeat at double: measured, it doubled that 90 to 178, so the snare is read in the mids only. How well the onsets line up at T against 2T: twice the tempo has twice the grid lines, so more onsets fit whatever the music. A wider prior or a different centre: that moves the error to other genres instead of deciding. Album or artist genres from the library: not visible to the analysis without a change to the worker; the file's own tag is read instead (a question below).

**Before and after** (generated fixtures, `tests/dj/tempo_fixtures.py`, made at test time with fixed seeds, about 30 s each; "before" is `integration` at 20742433 on the same audio; the grid as the sweep runs it, hinted by the analysis, and hinted at half time as a tag or Deezer would):

| Fixture | True | Analysis before | Analysis after | Grid before | Grid after | Grid confidence | Grid from a half-time hint |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dnb_174 (kick 1 and the "and" of 2, 16th hats, reese) | 174 | 86.1 | **172.2** | 87.0 | **174.0** | 0.48 → 0.74 | 87.0 → **174.0** |
| dnb_172_two_step (8th hats) | 172 | 86.1 | **172.2** | 86.0 | **172.0** | 0.50 → 0.76 | 86.0 → **172.0** |
| dnb_176_roller (syncopated kicks, swung hats) | 176 | 89.1 | **178.2** | 88.0 | **176.0** | 0.71 → 0.45 | 88.0 → **176.0** |
| amen_170 (Amen-style break) | 170 | 136.0 | 136.0 | 170.0 | 170.0 | 0.38 → 0.38 | no grid → **170.0** |
| amen_chopped_172 (chopped Amen) | 172 | 86.1 | **172.2** | 86.0 | **172.0** | 0.49 → 0.45 | 86.0 → **172.0** |
| liquid_174 (soft hats, pads) | 174 | 86.1 | **172.2** | 86.9 | **174.0** | 0.40 → 0.68 | 86.9 → **174.0** |
| house_124 | 124 | 123.0 | 123.0 | 124.0 | 124.0 | 1.0 → 1.0 | (124) 124.0 → 124.0 |
| hiphop_90 (boom bap, 16th hats) | 90 | 89.1 | 89.1 | 90.0 (no grid) | 90.0 (no grid) | 0.13 → 0.13 | (90) 90.0 → 90.0 |
| hiphop_90_eighths | 90 | 89.1 | 89.1 | 90.0 (no grid) | 90.0 (no grid) | 0.12 → 0.12 | (90) 90.0 → 90.0 |
| dubstep_140 (half time) | 140 | 92.3 | 92.3 | 140.0 | 140.0 | 0.49 → 0.49 | 70.0 → 70.0 |
| track_100 | 100 | 99.4 | 99.4 | 100.0 | 100.0 | 0.48 → 0.48 | (100) 100.0 → 100.0 |
| dnb_halftime_174 (snare on three) | 174 | 117.5 | 117.5 | 174.0 | 174.0 | 0.49 → 0.49 | 87.0 → 87.0 |

The same audio repeated to 10 minutes (the sweep's longest decode): drum and bass 86.1 → 172.2 (grid 87.1 → 174.2), house 123.0 and hip hop 89.1 unchanged (grids 123.87 and 90.0).

Two analysis readings aren't octave errors and are left alone: the Amen break at 136 (4/5) and dubstep at 92.3 (2/3); the grid gets both right, and its BPM is the one shown. The hip-hop fixtures had no grid before either (their sparse kicks score under 0.3); their grids' BPM still reads 90. Half-time drum and bass (the snare on three) is read at 117.5 by librosa (2/3); its grid stays at whatever its hint says (174 from a tag, 87 from Deezer). Two drum and bass grids drop from 0.71 and 0.49 to 0.45: the sixteenth check isn't sure which sixteenth the beat is on at the real tempo, so they echo out rather than beat-match (stage B's rule).

**Re-analysis.** Both versions go up (analyzer 4 → 5, grid 2 → 3), so the sweep revisits what's changed, but **only what the rule can change is decoded again**: the new rule only ever changes a reading of 70–100 or 140–200, so a track's analysis read at 100–140 comes out the same (tested). The one-time upgrade step `_promote_settled_tempos` (`database/music_database.py`, marker `dj_tempo_octave_v5`, ledger signal added) moves to the new versions, without a decode: DJ data analysed at 100–140 (or with no tempo), errors and too-long rows; Sample Studio's own analyses whose reading folds into 100–140; grids at 100–140 **found from a hint at 100–140 (or none)**, too short, errors, too long and unreachable. (The first round promoted grids by their own BPM, and said "bit for bit": the review showed a grid searches within 4% of its hint, so one hinted at 96–99.9 could land at 100 and would now be tested at double. Grids now check the hint too.) Everything else, typically hip hop, drum and bass, dubstep, trance and anything over 140, is analysed again by the normal sweep, one decode for both (its grid is due at once after its analysis), with the usual rests and waits, and shows its old values until then. Cost: each re-analysed track is one decode of up to 10 minutes and its analysis, as for a new track: measured here on a 10-minute track, about 7–11 s for BPM, key and energy and 3.5–6.5 s for the grid (a busy shared machine; the sweep runs at nice 10 and rests as long again after each track). The rule itself adds about 0.05 s of CPU on 10 minutes, for a reading at 70–100 or 140–200 the genre hasn't decided: it reads the mel spectrogram the analysis already makes for its onset envelope (the same envelope, exactly, so readings at 100–140 don't change); nothing for anything else. On a library of 10,000 tracks of which 3,000 sit in the bands (hip hop, drum and bass, dubstep, trance and up), that's about 3,000 × 25–35 s with the rests, roughly a day of background work, against three or more for the whole library; a house and techno library is mostly promoted and has little to do.

**Double BPM / Halve BPM.** `POST /api/library/dj/bpm` `{track_ids, action: double | halve | reset}` (1–500; within the caller's library scope (the store's `_scope`): an admin any track there, a member only their own library's; JSON only, 64 KB, one a second; `CONTRACTS.md`). The BPM shown is doubled or halved and kept in `track_dj.bpm_override` (with when and which profile), which analysis never writes, so no later analysis undoes it, and a track not analysed yet keeps it too. **The grid follows at once, without a decode** (`core/dj/grid_store.py` `follow_override`, `rescale`), **always worked out from the grid as measured**, which is kept beside it (`track_grid.measured_json`): doubling is exact (each beat stays a beat with one added halfway, a drifting grid's halfway points where its off-beats fall; the downbeat stays the one; bars, sections and mix regions double; the phrases are counted again from the same section changes); halving keeps the downbeat's beats (exact) and pairs bars so the most section changes stay on a bar line (an odd one rounds down, so a halved grid isn't exact). Halve then Double, or any series that ends at the measured tempo, is the measured grid again exactly (the first round compounded rescales, so odd section changes or an odd downbeat lost a bar or the first beat: the review's M2). A grid found later at the other octave (a new grid version, a re-analysis) is rescaled to the person's BPM as it's saved, in the same transaction that reads it, so a change during a save is never lost (L5); a grid that isn't an octave of it can't be rescaled, and isn't mixed on (L6). `reset` ("Use the measured BPM") clears it and puts the measured grid back, no decode (M4). A shared track's BPM (everyone's library) is an admin's to set; a member sets their own library's (403 `shared` otherwise; L3); one change a second a profile (L4). A person's BPM is the one written into the file's tag when Settings writes them. **In the browser:** Tracks' row "…" (after Add to playlist…: Double BPM, Halve BPM, and Use the measured BPM on a BPM set by hand) and the selection's "…" (Double BPM, Halve BPM; a member's items are disabled on shared tracks with "Only an admin can change a shared track's BPM"; past 500 picked, the first 500 are sent and the toast says so); a toast says what changed, and the rows, Mixes well with, grids and plans are read again. The BPM's title says where it came from: "174 BPM, measured from the beats · Tagged 87", "172 BPM, set by hand".

**Mixing with half and double tempos, as it is** (nothing changed; it was already right): "Mixes well with" and the Mix tab match at the same tempo, half or double within ±6% (`core/dj/camelot.py` `bpm_relation`, `bpm_windows`), ranking the same tempo before half and double; a set's steps (`core/sets/steps.py` `tempo_step`, the browser's `stepOf`) compare after halving or doubling and say "half time" or "double time"; the planner (`core/dj/planner.py` `tempo_relation`) picks same, half or double by the smallest gap and the engine plays it beat for every other beat (`perMaster` ½ or 2). So 87 into 174 was already a real half-time mix; what changes is that two drum and bass tracks now meet at "same", and hip hop at 90 and drum and bass at 174 meet at "half" instead of a misleading "same".

**Left out, and why:** album and artist genres from the library (above); Sample Studio's own rows don't keep the tempo's basis, so a track whose analysis came from Sample Studio never lets a half-time tag yield (its grid still measures, and its BPM is shown, 300). Double / Halve elsewhere than Tracks (344).

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of 17cea646..eb0c3411: 1 High, 4 Medium, 9 Low; all fixed after merging `integration` (2434c7a7) into the branch, each with a test; the reviewer's experiments are tests now):
- **H1, a correct 70–80 BPM tag was replaced by double.** librosa reads boom-bap at 72 or 76, and a 75 BPM half-time ballad, at 144–152, and the half-tag rule then showed that over the tag (and hinted the grid, and Traktor, at 144). `analyze_track` now returns why its tempo is in its octave (`tempo_basis`), kept in `track_dj.analyzed_bpm_basis`; a tag or enrichment at half yields only when the fast octave came from evidence (a fast genre, or the backbeat at the fast tempo, now measured for fast readings too), never from `kept` or `range`. The grid's hint is then the tag, and it stays slow. Tests: `test_a_correct_slow_tag_stands_when_the_double_isnt_evidence` (72, 76, 75), `test_a_half_time_tag_takes_the_analysiss_octave_only_with_evidence`.
- **M1, 180–200 hints were halved** by the grid's fold (70–180), so a 195 tag on a four-on-the-floor track showed 97.5 from its grid. Hints are folded only from 200. Tested at 185, 195 and 199, four on the floor and drum and bass. (With no hint at all, the grid's own scan still reads those at half; the sweep always has a hint.)
- **M2, Halve then Double didn't restore the grid** with odd section changes or an odd downbeat. Every rescale is worked out from the measured grid, kept in `track_grid.measured_json`; tested for constant and drifting grids, downbeats 0–3, changes on even, odd and mixed bars, and ÷2 ÷2 ×2 ×2.
- **M3, a slow genre halved a fast reading without evidence** (soul at 150, reggae at 160, rap at 148 read 76, 80.7, 73.8 while the grid found the real tempo). A slow genre now asks the backbeat, in both directions; the analysis and the grid agree. Tested.
- **M4, "Use the measured BPM" left no grid until the next sweep** (or for ever with analysis off), and re-decoded tracks whose grid was never rescaled. Reset puts the measured grid back in the same transaction, no decode, nothing requeued (so no `ondemand.ask` is needed: there's always the measured grid, or the grid is the measured one). Tested.
- **L1** "drum-n-bass" and "Drum_n_Bass" are drum and bass (punctuation first). **L2** grids are promoted only when their hint (the DJ data's BPM) was at 100–140 too (or there was none). **L3** a shared track's override (everyone's) is an admin's: a member gets 403 `shared` with a plain reason, and the menu items are disabled with it; a member sets their own library's tracks. **L4** one change a second a profile (its own `ondemand.RateLimit`). **L5** `save_result` reads the person's BPM and writes the grid in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, as `set_tempo` writes the override and the grid; tested with a ×2 and a reset sent during a grid save, and the other order. **L6** a grid that isn't an octave of the person's BPM is no grid to mix on (`bpm_mismatch`: the planner plays on or echoes out; the BPM shown is the person's). **L7** past 500 picked, the toast says only the first 500 were changed. **L8** the upgrade step's failure test (an ERROR naming it, `schema_health`, rolled back whole, runs again next start). **L9** Sample Studio's own analyses at 100–140 are promoted too.
- **The tag write:** with Settings' "Write BPM and key into files" on, a person's BPM is the one written (`core/dj/worker.py`, read at write time). Tested through the real `process_track`.
- **Second round** (a re-review of 82f41a41: H1, M2–M4 and L1–L9 confirmed fixed, M1 for hinted grids; no new High or Medium; five Low, all fixed with tests):
  - **N2, a reset after the tag was written came back.** With "Write BPM and key into files" on, a doubled track's file got the person's 174; after "Use the measured BPM", the next whole analysis read that tag (a tag is never halved) and showed 174 for good. A BPM written from a person's value is now remembered (`track_dj.bpm_tag_written`, whole, as the tag holds it), and a file tag equal to it is ours, not the file's own: the analysis ignores it, so the measured BPM comes back and the grid looks near it. A BPM someone else writes into the file later is the file's own again. Tested through the whole sequence (double, tag written, reset, analysed again, another tag).
  - **N1** Sample Studio's own row keeps the tempo's basis (`sample_analysis.tempo_basis`), so DJ data reused from it keeps the evidence.
  - **N3** the octave check computed a spectrum of its own (about 0.8–1.9 s of CPU for 10 minutes on every 70–100 or 140–200 reading). The analysis now makes one mel spectrogram for its onset envelope (exactly the envelope `onset_strength(y=y)` made: tested) and the check reads its mids from it: about 0.05 s. The leads moved a little with the shared spectrum (drum and bass 2.76–13.3, everything else at most 0.79); the before/after table is unchanged.
  - **N4** `set_tempo` takes `is_admin` as a required keyword: nothing is an admin by default.
  - **N5** the selection's items say "only an admin" only when every picked track is loaded and shared; a pick the list no longer has (filtered since) leaves them on, and the server's answer says what it skipped.
- **Corrected here:** "halving then doubling returns the original" held only for even section changes and an even downbeat (true now, from the measured grid); "bit for bit" held for the DJ data, not for grids, which now check their hint.

**Checks:** ruff; pytest `tests/dj`, `tests/sample`, `tests/database`, `tests/sets`, the migration ledger test, and `-m gauntlet`; new `tests/dj/test_tempo_octave.py` (the fixtures above through the analysis and the grid, the rule, genre families, exactness at 100–140), `tests/dj/test_bpm_override.py` (precedence, Double / Halve / reset, exact rescaling, stickiness through the real sweep code, the route's scope and limits) and `tests/database/test_dj_tempo_promotion.py`. Web: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, vitest on `src/features/dj` and Tracks (new `tempo.test.ts`, `tracks-tempo.test.tsx`; the "…" order tests in `tracks-page.test.tsx` gain the two items). Questions for Design: 344–348 (above, "From the drum and bass BPM fix").

### 2026-10-27: Dev, Spotify throttling of 27 October

Built on `fix/spotify-gentle` from `integration` (17cea646). Ross: "spotify just won't connect / throttle constantly" (Spotify had answered 429 with a Retry-After of about 15 hours), then "things hang for ages in the UI… Spotify is permanently throttled, no connection to playlists". Both are the same problem: SYNCHRO.S asked Spotify for too much, ignored some of Spotify's answers, forgot the rest on a restart or a reconnect, and waited for Spotify inside web requests. **Gauntlet:** `core/spotify_client.py` (already on the list) and the new `core/spotify_gate.py` (added under Outbound HTTP); the new tests are marked `gauntlet` and described in `docs/GAUNTLET.md`. No new route or table. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code keeps SoulSync's.

**The audit: every Spotify Web API call, with the defaults before this change** (a library of about 10k tracks, 50 playlists, 800 artists, 1,000 albums, 100 watched artists; the pace was 350 ms between decorated calls, about 171 a minute, shared by everything)

| Call | Trigger | How often | Requests per run | ≈ requests/hour |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Enrichment worker: `search_artists/albums/tracks`, `get_artist_albums` (every page, to tell same-name artists apart), `get_album_tracks`, `get_album`, `get_track_details`, credits backfill | background thread | continuous, 1.5 s between items, 500 items a day (kept in memory: every restart started a fresh 500) | 2–5 per item | ≈1,500 in 20–40 min after midnight or a restart (2,000–4,000/h while it runs), then 0 |
| Watchlist scan: one discography page per artist (all pages on a full-discography lookback), `get_album` per new release, `search_artists` for unmatched | automation, every 24 h | daily | 150–250 | ≈1,500/h for ~8 min; ≈8/h averaged |
| Sync page: `current_user_playlists` (1–2 pages), liked-songs count, `current_user` | page load (no cache) | each visit | 3–4 | a few |
| Open or sync a playlist: `playlist` + `/items` pages (100 each) | person; mirrored-playlist schedules | each sync | 1 + tracks/100; all 50 ≈ 100–150 | per sync |
| Liked Songs: `current_user_saved_tracks`, 50 a page | person, mirror sync | each sync | ≈200 for 10k, **back to back, unpaced**, and a 429 there set no pause | 200 in about a minute |
| Discovery of a ListenBrainz/YouTube/Beatport/Tidal playlist: 1–3 searches per track, "extended" at limit 50 | person, pipeline automations | per playlist | 100–150 per 50 tracks; dev-mode apps refuse limit 50 (400), one wasted request per track | per run |
| Artist page: `artist` (30-day cache), discography pages (30-day cache), `artist_top_tracks` | page load | each visit | 1–6; top tracks is gone for dev-mode apps (403, uncached, every visit) | a few |
| Search: tracks, artists, albums | person | each search | 3 (7-day cache) | a few |
| Discover → Your artists / Your albums: followed artists, saved albums | page load when stale, background thread | rarely | 1 per 50 | ~0 |
| Look for releases you don't have | person | per run | a discography per artist, 1/s | per run |
| Sign-in check `current_user` | 15-minute cache | 4/h | 1 | 4 |
| Token refresh (accounts.spotify.com) | any call with an expired token; the worker reloads every 30 s while Spotify is unavailable | — | 1 | up to 120/h with a refused refresh token, forever |
| `/status` (5 s), Home (30 s), other polls | browser | — | 0: event-driven, nothing reaches Spotify | 0 |

Idle, SYNCHRO.S asked Spotify for 5–20 requests an hour; with the enrichment burst, a liked-songs sync and a watchlist scan landing together, well over 3,000 an hour, at a pace already at the ceiling people measure for Spotify's rolling 30-second window (Spotify publishes no number; development-mode apps get less).

**Why it was throttled constantly**
1. **The pause lived in memory.** Restarting the container (what you do when Spotify "won't connect") forgot Spotify's Retry-After and gave enrichment a fresh 500: every job fired into a limit that was still on, and Spotify answered with a longer wait. Repeat, and a few minutes becomes 15 hours.
2. **Reconnecting, Disconnect and "Try again now" cleared Spotify's own wait** and the cooldown after it, so background jobs stampeded the moment the button was pressed.
3. **Many 429s set no pause at all.** Nine methods swallow their errors (your playlists, a playlist's tracks, Liked Songs, saved albums, the count, your profile, audio features, the artists batch, the user id), and routes call `client.sp` directly; their 429s were logged and the next request went straight out. Pages of Liked Songs, playlists and saved albums weren't paced at all.
4. **Wasted requests on what Spotify took away** from development-mode apps in February 2026: the several-ids lookups, an artist's top tracks, search pages over 10 (and audio features since 2024), each refused every time.

**Why the UI hung** (gunicorn: 1 worker × 8 threads, `gthread`, timeout 120 s). Inside web requests: the decorator slept through any Retry-After up to a minute, up to three times (≈3 min a request), 5–7 s without a header, 2 + 4 + 8 s on a 502/503; the pacing slept *while holding one process-wide lock*, so every Spotify call in every thread queued behind it (2–3 s a call for up to 45 minutes after a pause, a background backlog in the same queue as your page); long paginations ran inside the request; the token refresh had no timeout; and spotipy's interactive sign-in (it binds 127.0.0.1:8888 and waits, or reads stdin) was one missing token away. Eight such requests (a page firing four Spotify-backed calls, twice) and every thread sleeps: nothing answers, static files included. Nothing slept a 3,600-s Retry-After (that was already a pause); the hangs were the short ones, the shared lock and the paginations.

**Why it wouldn't connect**
1. **During a pause, signing in "failed".** The callback exchanged the code, then checked the sign-in by asking Spotify, which the pause refuses, so it said "SYNCHRO.S could not confirm an authenticated Spotify session" and Connections showed not connected, for the whole 15 hours.
2. **The callback refreshed the old token instead of using the new code** (`get_access_token(code)` checks the saved token first, refreshes it if expired, and returns that). Spotify now expires a refresh token six months after sign-in; once it's refused (`invalid_grant`), every sign-in failed until Disconnect.
3. **Redirect URI:** the defaults are right (`http://127.0.0.1:8888/callback`, which Docker Desktop on a Mac reaches through the published port), but in Docker the Authenticate button showed manual steps telling a Mac browser to change `127.0.0.1` to `localhost`, and nothing checked the configured URI, so a `localhost` one (Spotify refuses it since 2025) only failed on Spotify's page.

**What changed**
- **One gate for every request** (`GatedSpotifySession` in `core/spotify_client.py`, pieces in `core/spotify_gate.py`): spotipy's session, so every request is covered, pages and routes' direct `client.sp` calls included. A token bucket (one request a second, a burst of 15, `spotify.min_api_interval` overrides) whose last 5 tokens are a person's; background work also has an hourly share (1,200, `spotify.background_requests_per_hour`) and goes one request at a time, so a 429 never meets a volley already in flight; it waits in short jittered steps. Any 429 pauses everyone for exactly Spotify's Retry-After (else the estimate ladder) and empties the bucket.
- **Web requests never wait on Spotify:** while paused, the gate refuses at once (`SpotifyRateLimitError`), unsent; a person's request waits at most 2 s for the budget, else answers busy (`SpotifyBusyError`) and the caller falls back to your other sources; the decorator never sleeps on a 429 (background jobs retry a 502/503 twice); every request has connect/read timeouts of 4/10 s; `/api/spotify/playlists` and `/api/spotify/playlist/<id>` answer "Spotify asked us to wait until 21:40. Your other sources carry on meanwhile." (429) or show the last list Spotify gave.
- **The pause is stored** (`spotify_rate_limit`, top level of the config, so a Settings save can't round-trip it) and read back after a restart, cooldown included; it belongs to the client id that earned it (a new Spotify app isn't paused by the old one's wait). Enrichment's daily count is stored too (`spotify_enrichment_budget`).
- **Reconnect, Disconnect and Try again now:** signing in or disconnecting no longer lifts a wait Spotify asked for (our own estimate is cleared); "Try again now" lets your own requests go at once but background jobs still cool down and ease in. A sign-in during a pause says "Spotify is connected. Spotify asked us to wait until 21:40, so SYNCHRO.S starts using it then".
- **Sign-in:** every SpotifyOAuth is `make_spotify_oauth` (`GentleSpotifyOAuth`): 10 s timeouts, never the interactive sign-in, one refresh at a time, a failed refresh waits 1, 2, 4… up to 60 minutes, and a refresh token Spotify refuses is never tried again ("Spotify sign-in expired… Authenticate again"). All three callbacks (8008, 8888, a profile's) exchange the new code (`check_cache=False`).
- **Redirect URI:** `redirect_uri_problem()` applies Spotify's rules (https, or http only for 127.0.0.1 / [::1]; never `localhost`); Authenticate shows the problem and the URI to use instead of sending you to Spotify's error. A browser on the same computer goes straight to Spotify (no manual steps, no switch to `localhost`).
- **Fewer requests:** the Sync page's list, liked-songs count and profile are reused for 2–10 minutes; an endpoint Spotify refuses with 403/404 for this app (top tracks, the artists batch, audio features) isn't asked again for a day; search drops to pages of 10 after Spotify refuses 50 once. The several-ids endpoints aren't an option for development-mode apps (Spotify removed them in February 2026); metadata caching was already there (30 days for artists, albums and tracks, 7 for searches) and is unchanged.
- **The pause in words** (`spotify-pause.ts`): from an hour on, the title and sentences say when it ends: "Spotify asked us to wait 15 h, until 21:40 (after reading your liked songs)"; tags stay short ("Paused by Spotify · 15 h"). The legacy Settings card uses the same view.

**Left as is, and why:** the opt-in "Spotify (no auth)" source and the public playlist fallback (used when Spotify answers "Premium required") read Spotify's web player, not the Web API; this change adds nothing of the kind and never falls back to them while paused. Whether to keep them is Ross's call (Spotify's terms don't allow scraping).

**For Ross:** register exactly `http://127.0.0.1:8888/callback` in the Spotify dashboard (and nothing with `localhost`), with the same value in Settings → Connections → Spotify, and click Authenticate from a browser on the Mac (http://127.0.0.1:8008). From another device it needs an https address. Development mode needs Premium on the app owner's account and allows five users (add yours under User Management). After the update, the first 429 records Spotify's wait for good; there's no need to restart or reconnect to get past it.

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of 94ee570f: 2 High, 5 Medium, 7 Low). Each fix has a test in `tests/test_spotify_gentle.py` (marked `gauntlet`).
- **H1, pool threads and the background slot:** a pool thread a web request waits on counted as background work, and a background job held the one-at-a-time slot while it slept up to an hour for budget, so `/api/match/suggestions`, `/api/settings/verify` and the search fan-outs could stall. Now the gate waits holding nothing and takes the slot only around the send; `spotify_context(fn)` carries a person's request into pool threads (the search orchestrator and sources, multi-source search, art and artist-image lookups, settings verify, match suggestions, manual and streamed search); a background request gives up after 5 minutes (or at shutdown) and the enrichment worker waits for budget between items (`spotify_background_wait()`); verify and suggestions wait at most 60 s / 20 s for their pool. Test: with the hourly share spent and a job waiting for it, a request's pool thread answers in under 2 s.
- **H2, profile sign-ins on 8888:** the callback server on 8888 ignored `state`, so a profile's sign-in was exchanged into the account's token. Both callbacks now finish through one function (`_finish_spotify_sign_in`), so `state=profile_N` always goes to that profile, and a profile with no app to finish with is refused rather than signed into the account. Tests: both callbacks, with profile and account sign-ins, and during a pause.
- **M1, paginations in web requests:** `/api/spotify/playlist/<id>?async=1` runs the load as a job a person is waiting for (paced like background work, so pages keep their share; not held to the hourly share) and the pages poll `/api/spotify/playlist-load/<job_id>` (React `fetchSpotifyPlaylistTracks`, the classic `fetchAndCacheSpotifyPlaylistTracks`). The plain route stays for other callers.
- **M2, storing in the request thread:** the stored pause (and the last playlist list) is written by one background writer, coalesced per key, latest sequence number wins; a slow store no longer holds the thread that got the 429, and two 429s close together can't store the older one last.
- **M3, endpoints marked gone too eagerly:** only a 403 whose message is about the endpoint, or 403s for three different ids within an hour; never a 404, Premium lapsing or "user not registered"; forgotten on a new sign-in, Disconnect or another client id.
- **M4, the Sync page hid the pause:** a pause or busy part way through your playlists is raised, not a partial list; `/api/spotify/playlists` answers the last list (stored, so it survives a restart; `X-Spotify-Stale: 1`) or 429 with the wait in words, and the Sync page and Mirror show that error instead of "No Spotify playlists found".
- **M5, no bound on Retry-After:** `inf`, `nan`, `1e400` and garbage are no header (the estimate); anything past a day (999999999999, a 2099 date) pauses for a day as an estimate; a stored pause is cut to a day; `describe_pause_until` never raises.
- **L1:** the redirect-URI check reads the URI the sign-in actually sends (normalized, with the 127.0.0.1 default), survives a port that isn't a number, and checks a profile's sign-in too. **L2:** a profile signing in during a pause is told it's connected. **L3:** threads waiting on a refresh reuse any unexpired saved token, so a rotated refresh token is refreshed once. **L4:** a profile's own Spotify app has its own pause, and a new client id starts the estimate ladder afresh. **L5:** the bucket, the hourly share and the refresh backoff run on the monotonic clock (the stored pause stays on wall time). **L6:** the stored pause loads under the lock and the later end wins. **L7:** tests for busy within 2 s, the page answering at once while a playlist loads, the 8888 callback during a pause; the suite keeps the stored pause in memory whenever `core.spotify_client` is first imported (`SOULSYNC_SPOTIFY_STATE_IN_MEMORY`).
- **Second round** (the re-review of 94ee570f..0cd6027c confirmed H1, H2 and M1–M5 and found nothing new above Low):
  - **An empty redirect setting:** the account's callback exchanged with `redirect_uri=''` while Authenticate had sent the 127.0.0.1 default, so Spotify refused it; both now use the client's URI (the setting, or `http://127.0.0.1:8888/callback`), and so does the export sign-in. Tested end to end (Authenticate, then the callback).
  - **The last playlist list isn't a setting:** it lives in the database's metadata table (`spotify_last_playlists`), not the app config, so it's never in `GET /api/settings` or an export; it's written only when it changes, belongs to the account that fetched it (another account never sees it), and is forgotten on every sign-in and on Disconnect. The Sync page says "Spotify asked us to wait, so this is the last list it gave." over it.
  - **Wanted's bulk add** (`POST /api/wishlist/bulk`) looks releases up as the person's request (`spotify_context`) and waits at most 90 s; a release not looked up by then says so. The wrappers on the manual and streamed search pools are gone: those pools search download sources, never Spotify.
  - **The slot tests now prove it:** the waiting job's budget frees in about a minute (inside the 5-minute cap), and both tests fail on 94ee570f's behaviour (the slot held while waiting; no context for pool threads).
  - **Small:** the hourly share's check, the token and the count are one step (eight waiters released together can't overshoot); `cancel_spotify_waits()` wakes a waiting request at once; finished playlist loads are pruned on a poll too; a profile's own-app pause is stored with the account's and survives a restart.
- **The sanity-check failure on 94ee570f** (`test_recreates_staging_dir_when_post_process_cleans_it`) is a flake on `integration` too (it fails about one run in four on 17cea646); left as is.

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/test_spotify_gentle.py` (new, `gauntlet`), `tests/test_spotify_rate_limit.py` (the decorator's tests now say it never sleeps; "Try again now" keeps the cooldown) and the 174 test files that touch the Spotify client, worker, registry or routes; `pytest -m gauntlet`; Bandit and Semgrep (the pinned versions and rules) on the changed files, nothing new above Low; `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, vitest on `features/spotify-pause` and `routes/dashboard`.

### 2026-10-27: Dev, Visuals panel and Equalizer fix of 27 October

Ross: in Visuals the Visuals button no longer opened its panel, so MilkDrop and Classic were out of reach and V only stepped through the SYNCHRO.S visuals; and Equalizer in the player's "…" menu "triggered nothing". Built on `fix/visuals-eq` from `integration` (20742433).

**Equalizer.** The click did switch the EQ, but nothing on screen said so. The menu follows the legacy switches (EQ, A–B loop, Keyboard shortcuts) by redrawing after their clicks bubble up to it (`src/shell/player.ts`), but `static/media-player-v2.js` wires those buttons with a handler that calls `stopPropagation()`, so the menu never heard them: "Equalizer · Off" stayed Off, and with the Flat preset nothing could be heard either. The menu now follows them in the capture phase, before their own handler runs, so Equalizer reads "Flat" (or the preset) as soon as it's on and "Off" when it's off. The same change closes the menu when Keyboard shortcuts opens its list.

**The Visuals panel.** The panel, MilkDrop, Classic and the header's Visuals button all come with the lazily loaded Visuals chunk (`features/visuals/visuals-mount.ts` imports `visuals-host` the first time the overlay opens). When that import failed, the failure was swallowed and never tried again while the page stayed open, and the legacy player went on drawing the SYNCHRO.S visuals and stepping them with V: exactly a Visuals button that does nothing and no way to MilkDrop. The usual way to get there is a page left open across an update: every build renames the chunks, so the open page asks for files the new build no longer has (reproduced by refusing the chunk; on a fresh page of this build the panel opens). Now the Visuals button loads the pieces itself and opens the panel when it's clicked before they're in (also while the first load is still on its way); a load that failed is tried again on the next open and the next click; and when the pieces can't be fetched it says so in a toast with **Reload**, since a browser keeps a module that failed to fetch failed for the rest of the page's life: "The Visuals panel didn't load. If SYNCHRO.S was updated after this page opened, reload the page."

**One panel at a time (29b).** M in Visuals opened the Mix drawer over an open Visuals panel without closing it, unlike the header's Mix button. The panel stayed open underneath, the Visuals button still read pressed, and the next click on Visuals closed both, so it looked as if Visuals did nothing. M now goes through the Mix button's own path (`toggleDrawer` in `features/mix/mix-host.tsx`), which closes the panel first.

**Tests.** `src/shell/player.test.ts` ("Equalizer says what its click did…", with the real `npv2WireTransport` lifted from `static/media-player-v2.js`), the new `features/visuals/visuals-mount.test.ts` (one load per open, a failed load tried again, the button loading the pieces and opening the panel, a click during the load, the Reload toast, and the real host on `index.html`'s overlay clicked before it has loaded), `features/mix/mix-tab.test.tsx` ("M opens it in the visuals panel's place"); each fails without its fix. `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check` (0 errors) and `npm run build` are clean; vitest on `features/visuals`, `features/mix`, `features/automix`, `src/shell` and the media-player V2, Mix keys and auto-mix tests passes. No server change.

**Checked in Chromium on a scratch server** (two generated tracks in a scratch library), at 1440: Visuals opened from "…" and from F, with and without the Mix drawer: the Visuals button opens the panel, MilkDrop lists its presets, a preset loads and V steps through MilkDrop's; Equalizer reads Flat after a click; M with the panel open closes it and opens the drawer, and Visuals then opens the panel; with the Visuals chunk refused (as for a page left open across an update), V still steps the SYNCHRO.S visuals and a click on Visuals shows the Reload toast instead of doing nothing.

### 2026-10-27: Dev, analyse on demand of 27 October

Built on `feat/analyse-now` from `integration` (9f3a83ba), from Ross's ask (BACKLOG "Analyse on demand, with progress", Building), with three more of Ross's asks after he tried auto-mix (below). No boards: the indicator is interim and Claude Design explores the final one (333). SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** two new routes that act on the caller's library and a change to the background job that decodes library files: `core/dj/ondemand.py`, `core/dj/progress.py` and `core/dj/worker.py` are added to `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` (`api/dj.py`, `core/dj/grid.py` and `core/dj/planner.py` were there); the access, cap, rate-limit and line tests are marked `gauntlet`. **No schema change:** the asked-for line is in memory (a restart empties it). Interfaces: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Analyse on demand" and the stage A and C sections; patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, "Analysis progress" and "Auto-mix in the player"; rows: `SCREENS.md`.

**The queue.** The sweep (`core/dj/worker.py` `DjAnalysisJob`) gains an **asked-for line** in front of its own order. `POST /api/library/dj/analyse` (any profile, its own library's tracks, `{track_ids}` up to 500 or `{playlist_id}`; JSON only, 64 KB) checks which need analysis (no DJ data at the analyzer version, or no grid at `GRID_VERSION`, or a file that wasn't there) and puts them in line, **200 a profile at most**, **one request a second a profile** (429 with `Retry-After`). With no sweep running, asking starts one for the asked-for tracks only, as a `library_jobs` row under the asking profile (so a member sees it in Activity), without lifting a person's Stop on the automatic sweeps (337). Each ask says whether a person made it (`reason: "asked"`: Analyse these, Analyse now, the Mix tab's button, a lab pick) or a page did (`"auto"`); while Stop holds, an automatic ask is turned away (409 `held`, which the browser says nothing about). Asked-for tracks don't wait for downloads or imports; they wait while the library's drive is away, a drive move runs or the Write Tags batch runs, saying so. Everything else is as it was: one track at a time at nice 10, the rests after every track (asked-for ones too), the 25-failures limits, the grid top-up and `GRID_VERSION`, Stop's hold. A request that lands just as a run finds nothing left is taken by that run's end (tested by forcing the race). A person's Stop, or a run that fails, empties the line.

**The progress.** `core/dj/progress.py` binds a reporter to the sweep's own thread; the analysis reports each step as it reaches it: `read` before the decode, `tempo`, `key`, `energy` in Sample Studio's `analyze_track` (a new optional `on_step`; Sample Studio's own analysis passes none), `tags` before a tag write, `grid` and `phrases` in the grid's analysis. Each step has a fixed percentage on one of two scales (a full analysis, or a grid-only one: the top-up or Sample Studio's analysis reused). `POST /api/library/dj/progress` answers each of the caller's tracks as `done`, `queued` (position, `asked_for`), `working` (step, percent), `waiting` (why) or `failed` (why), and the sweep's own state; the track being worked on is named only for a caller whose library holds it. The browser polls it once for the whole page (`analysisStore`, 500 ids a request), every 1.5 s while a track it shows is being worked out, asked for or near the front of the line, or while Activity's DJ row is open and the sweep runs, else every 20 s; finished tracks are gathered for 3 s, then what shows them is read again (the rows, the Mix tab, its join step and plans that name them, and the lists: browse, the sweep's status, sets' rows; never Vibe), so BPM, key, energy, chips and the energy graph fill in by themselves, and a pair waiting for it is planned again.

**Where it shows, and what asks:** the Mix tab (asks for its track, and shows its progress, only while the tab is shown; its Not analysed line and card show the bar instead of Analyse it now while it's in line), a set's page (asks for its tracks; Being worked out rows show the bar under the artist), Add to mix set… and the working set (ask), Up next with Auto mix on or a set played as a mix (asks for the next 50; a Waiting chip shows the bar of the track it waits for, and Analyse now for anyone when it isn't in line), the lab (asks for its picks; every picker row shows its state), Tracks' rows (only asked-for tracks in line and the one working), Activity's DJ row (the sweep's bar, the track and its step), and **Analyse these** in Tracks' selection, an album's, a playlist's and a set's "…" (335).

**Measured in a real browser** (Chromium, a scratch server with generated tracks: three analysed, three 3½-minute tracks with no DJ data or grid, the real `worker.JOB` and the real analysis): on a set's page, three unanalysed tracks added one after another went in line ("2nd in line", "Next in line") and stepped through "Reading the audio · 5%", "Finding the tempo · 20%", "Finding the beat grid · 60%" each, then their rows filled in by themselves (BPM, key, energy, the step chips and the energy graph) and "Being worked out" went. Found there: two asks within a second drew a 429 (waited out, but needless); asks are now spaced a second apart in the browser.

**Ross's three asks after trying auto-mix** (each its own commit with tests):
1. **The lab's track picking.** Each deck has one picker: Playing & Up next, Recent (Listening history), Starred, Sets & playlists (choose one, then its rows; a playlist's rows matched to the library by title and artist), Mixes well with the outgoing track (the incoming deck: the Mix tab's order with reasons) and Search as a fallback. Every row says its BPM, key and analysis state, with the indicator while it's in line or worked on; picking one asks for its analysis. Play the mix says why when the plan is a cut or plays on.
2. **Download a mix report** (the lab): a JSON file made in the browser, no route: both grids' summaries (BPM, confidence, kind, the first downbeat, phrases, sections, mix-in and mix-out), the plan, the last run's measurements (rates and modes, the beat correction every 4 beats, start times and windows, each deck by its map and by the worklet's report, and the beats' drift, four a second) and the browser's and audio context's details. No audio, no file paths (path keys dropped, path-like strings replaced), no names but the tracks' titles and artists.
3. **No crossfade** ("we either mix auto or manual or nothing"). Player settings → Between tracks is **Off / Auto mix** (stored `dj` stays); a stored Smart crossfade and the old crossfade switch become Off, once, on both sides; the "…" menu's Crossfade and the crossfade length setting are gone. The planner never falls back to a crossfade: echo out where the outgoing track has a grid (even one not trusted to beat-match: a gap over 8%, a break held at 0.45, not 4/4), else play on (`status: "play_on"`): a track with no beat found or too short for a grid is never cut by bar count, and one album's tracks in their order play on (`status: "cut"` stays reserved for Design's 302; the player still handles one). The chip shows the move, nothing for a play-on in Up next, and the reason only in the picker (338). A page that can't run the decks and a mix that missed its windows play on. Off is plain playback, as before. Smart crossfade's code (and the waveform fetches it made) is removed.

**Gauntlet review** (an independent review of the branch: no High, 4 Medium, 8 Low; all fixed on the branch, with tests):
- **M1, automatic asks undid an admin's Stop.** The Mix tab is always mounted and asked for every playing track, and an ask started a run whatever the hold said. Now the Mix tab asks (and watches) only while it's shown (the tray in Mix and open, or the Visuals drawer), every ask carries `reason` (`asked` or `auto`, `auto` when left out), and while Stop holds the server turns automatic asks away with 409 `held`; the browser stays quiet about it (and, since the second round, forgets those tracks so a person's later ask for one goes). A person's ask (Analyse these, Analyse now, the Mix tab's button, a lab pick, a playlist's Analyse these) goes in. Tests: both paths on the server, the reason on every trigger, the held answer kept quiet, separate requests for the two kinds, the tab shut asking and watching nothing.
- **M2, the fast poll ran on every page while any sweep ran.** Fast (1.5 s) now only while a watched track is in line or working, or while Activity's sweep row is open and the sweep runs; else 20 s. The hidden Mix tab watches nothing.
- **M3, every finished track read every DJ, set and plan query again.** Finishes are gathered (one refresh every 3 s at most) and the refresh is narrowed to the queries naming a finished track (`['dj', 'tracks', ids]`, `['dj', 'mix' | 'mixes', id]`, `['automix', 'plan', from, to]`) and the lists that show them (`['dj', 'status' | 'browse']`, `['sets', 'set' | 'view' | 'list' | 'working']`); never Vibe.
- **M4, tracks without a grid were cut by bar count.** No beat found and too short now play on (`fallback.kind: "play_on"` with the code), and one album's tracks in their order (same album, track n into n + 1) play on (`code: "album_in_order"`). The rule Design's 302 asks for comes in its own round.
- **L1, a run's end could be overwritten by a run starting in its tail.** The end state is taken inside the lock and handed to the save, and `start()` doesn't replace a run whose thread is still finishing (the race forced in a test).
- **L2, a cut set while paused was lost, and its wait ignored the playback rate.** A timer that finds the track paused (or short of its bar) lets the cut be set again on the next tick, and the wait is in the track's seconds at its rate. Tests: a pause during a cut, double and half speed.
- **L3, a member's Activity row offered a Stop the server refuses.** `stop_path` is given to admins only.
- **L4, the poll sent at most 500 ids.** It asks in chunks of 500 and puts the answers together.
- **L5, a track the run had already taken was accepted into the line, shown in line, then dropped.** It gets one more try in the same run; asked again after that, it's counted with `cant` at once.
- **L6, the mix report's path check missed paths with spaces, `C:/…`, shares, a file at the root and paths inside sentences.** Any such path in a string is replaced (drive letters either way round, `\\share\…`, `/x.flac`, two or more separators, inside text). Tests for each.
- **L7, `/api/library/dj/status` named the current track to every profile.** `job.current` is given to admins only.
- **L8, `start()` read the library while holding the job's lock.** The ids are read first; `running` is checked again under the lock.
- **Clean-up:** unused state gone from the job; the player's old crossfade (`npCrossfadeTick`, `npStartCrossfade`, `npFinishCrossfade`, the window API's `crossfadeInto` and the theater's crossfade-length wiring) removed, with the controller's port; a mix the system cut off now finishes with a cut once sound is back (Design's 308; the executor's `cutNow`, the phone note's words "SYNCHRO.S cuts to the next track when it's back"); the playlist path is rate-limited before its rows are read; a chunked body past the cap is refused (413, tested); the poll's timer is cleared when the last row goes (tested).
- **Second round** (a re-review of the fixes; M1, M4, L1–L5, L7, L8 and the clean-up held):
  1. **The fast poll still ran for a library mid-sweep:** any `queued` row, or `waiting` with a place, counted, and the sweep's own order gives every track one. Fast now only for a track being worked out, one someone asked for (`asked_for`), or one within 5 of the front. Tested.
  2. **The Mix tab's join step wasn't read again** when its track finished: `['sets', 'join', last, playing]` is matched by its ids. Tested.
  3. **The report's path check still let parts through:** names with brackets, commas, `&` and `'` (`Album (2019)`, `Crosby, Stills & Nash`, `Artist (UK)`), percent-encoded paths; and it scrubbed `27/10/2026` and `AC/DC/Back`. Now once an anchor is found (a drive either way round, a share, `file:`, `~/`, or a `/` that starts a path of two parts or a file) the rest of the line to the next double quote goes; percent-encoding is decoded first; without an anchor only a relative path to a file counts. Tested case by case.
  4. **A person's ask after a Stop, during the stopped run's last track, was taken then dropped.** The run's end clears the line only when it failed or the setting was turned off; after a person's Stop (the line already emptied, automatic asks refused) what a person asked for runs next. Tested both ways.
  5. **Asking for the track being worked out ran it twice** (and `done` passed `total`). The current track is reported as already in line, set the moment it's taken; what's counted never passes the total. Tested.
  6. **The scheduler could start a full sweep after a Stop** that landed while it read the library: `start()` checks the hold again under the lock. Tested.
  7. **A held 409 kept its tracks in the browser's ten-minute memory**, so a later lab pick of one sent nothing: they're forgotten. Tested.
  8. **An album's order ignored the disc:** track n into n + 1 on the same disc, or a disc's last track into the next disc's first (`scoped_rows` reads the disc and its last track number). Tested on the planner and through `/plan`.
  9. The stale `crossfadeInto` mocks are gone from the controller's and the host's tests.
  10. Checked: the tray's `hidden` follows the phone breakpoint (the player's `matchMedia` listener runs `applyLayout`), and `#ss-player-tray` is the one static node in `index.html` (the player reads it once too), so the Mix tab's observer stays on the right node. Nothing to fix.
  Checks for the second round: ruff; pytest on `tests/dj`, `tests/sets`, `tests/mix` and `tests/activity` (802 pass) and `-m gauntlet` (322 pass); tsc, `npm run check` (no errors), `npm run build`; vitest on the touched files and the full suite (687 files, 12,041 tests, all pass).
- **Checks for the round:** ruff; pytest on `tests/dj`, `tests/sets`, `tests/mix` and `tests/activity` (795 pass) and `-m gauntlet` (316 pass); tsc, `npm run check` (no errors), `npm run build`; vitest on the touched files (42 files, 582 tests) and the full suite (687 files, 12,040 tests: all pass; four tests in the artist page and automations timed out under the load of a parallel pytest run and pass on their own).

**Left out, and why:** the final indicator (Claude Design, 333); a per-profile limit on how much analysis time a profile can use (the line's 200 and the rate limit are the limits); a persistent line (in memory by design: the automatic sweeps reach everything anyway, and a migration wasn't worth it); Decks.

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/dj` (new `test_analyse_on_demand.py`: access, caps, rate limit, the 200 limit, playlists, waiting rules, Stop's hold, rests, error limits, the end-of-run race, every progress state, scope, steps from the real analyzer; the planner's no-crossfade cases), `tests/sets`, `tests/mix`, `tests/activity`, `tests/sample`, the waveform test and `pytest -m gauntlet`; tsc, `npm run check`, `npm run build`; vitest on the touched features (new `analysis.test.tsx`, `track-picker.test.tsx`, `mix-report.test.ts`; updated Mix tab, sets page, Tracks, Activity, album header, playlist modal, automix, controller, chips, player and lab tests) and the full suite before the push.

### 2026-10-27: Design's Briefs A–E and answers 260–324, and Ross's yes

From Design's session of 27 October: `design_handoff_syncfm_ui/README.md` ("Added 2026-10-27", its build order and prompt) and `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-27.dc.html`. New boards: `Analysis indicator (Brief A).dc.html` (31a–31g), `Drag and drop (Brief B).dc.html` (32a–32g), `Where to go for what (Brief C).dc.html` (33a–33f), `Row primary button (Brief D).dc.html` (34a–34c) and `DJ now (Brief E, options).dc.html` (35a–35q, 36a–36b). Corrected: `DJ area map (six briefs).dc.html` (the Decks and Settings rows, key D) and `Auto-mix (Up next, decks).dc.html` (26d's "Watch it on Decks" is "Open DJ now"). Ross's four rules hold throughout: nothing says "crossfade", analysis is on demand with a bar and a line, DJ now is laptop and desktop only, and a track's own sections never move.

**Ross's yes (27 October)**
- **DJ now is E1:** the tray's third mode, Now playing · Mix · **DJ** (key D), with the rail's two best ideas folded in: B's sections dragged against A's and locked (35a's lanes), and the Build row as the Mix tab's set card with "Save as a set". 36a draws the Now playing mode with the three-way switch; 36b lists the boards E1 corrects (24a, 26d, 26e, 26h, 29a, 17a–17d, 21e/26j). E3's dock may follow later as the same component; the rail as a separate surface is not built.
- **The docked Mixes panel goes** (33a): "Mixes well with…" opens the tray in Mix, following that track ("Following Copper Sky · Back to Night Ferry").
- **The row's visible button is Play** (34a): Play next (a triangle with a bar) on hover, focus and in the "…"; the accent ▶ goes.
- **"Set", not "mix set"** (33f): "Add to set…", "Save as a set", "Between tracks: Off / Auto mix", "ready to mix" means has a beat grid.

**What each brief decided**
- **A · The analysis indicator (31a–31g).** One component, `AnalysisLine`, in three sizes:
  - **the line:** a 4px bar on 10% white, filled in accent-light by step (not time); one 14px sentence with an 8px dot; an action link at the right;
  - **the row marker:** a dot and two words;
  - **the topbar count:** a pill, "Analysing · 14 in line" (accent while running), with a popover holding the six-step checklist with times, the line in order with why each track is in it, the sweep's progress ("Finding beats · 1,240 of 4,980"), Stop (admins) and Open Activity. On a phone, the Activity sheet is the popover.

  Dot colours: green ready to mix; 40% white has data or queued; accent running; amber waiting, stopped or can't be beat-matched; red failed. The words: "Ready to mix" · "BPM and key · no beat grid yet" · "Not analysed · next sweep in about 25 min" · "In line · 3rd" · "Analysing Canopy · beat grid (5 of 6)" · "Waiting for 3 downloads to finish" (and imports, a drive away, a drive move, tag writing) · "Analysis stopped by Ross · Resume" · "Couldn't be read · won't auto-mix; plays straight on" · "Skipped, 2 h 14 min is too long to analyse · plays straight on" · "Can't be beat-matched · no steady beat · echo out or cut" · "Canopy is ready to mix" (once, 4 s). Members get "Analyse mine" (their tracks go first); admins Analyse now · Analyse all · Resume. A trigger adds "analysing, 2nd in line" to its Added toast. Reduced motion: six cells, no fill animation. Phone: the sheet header's line and a two-line chip.
- **B · Drag and drop (32a–32g).**
  - **Grip:** a reserved 24px column, three 10×2 bars at 62% on hover and focus (none on rows without a file).
  - **Lift:** source rows at 40%; a 48px ghost with a 1.5° tilt, `#1a1a1e`, an accent-light 60% border, title · key · BPM, and a 24px count badge for many.
  - **Targets** light up at lift: a 1px dashed accent-light 70% frame with a 6% accent fill on a set's list, Your sets, the set card, Up next and DJ now.
  - **Insertion line:** 2px accent-light, a 36px gap, the new steps in Sets' words; in a transition gap, both new chips. Never at a locked row's place; a drop there lands at the nearest free place (275).
  - **Invalid:** amber (40% border) on the target itself, with the reason (file missing, not in the library, a locked row's place, a set you can't edit, "Can't replace the incoming track · drop after it"). Duplicate: "In it already · #4 · drop to add again".
  - Auto-scroll at the edges, 8 rows a second, with a gradient cue. A drop gives a toast with Undo (6 s; ⌘Z). Locked rows hold.
  - **Phone:** long-press lifts (scale 1.03), drag within the list, a "Send to…" tray at the foot (the working set · Up next · Other sets · DJ now disabled "laptop only"), let go elsewhere to cancel; a Move mode with ▲▼ and Done.
  - **Keyboard:** Space, ↑↓ (announced with the new steps), ⇧↑↓, Enter, Esc, ⌘Z; "Send to…" in the "…". The matrix is 32g.
- **C · Where to go for what (33a–33f).**
  - The tray has three modes, Now playing · Mix · DJ (keys M, D). In Mix the right column **is** Up next (not a copy), with Between tracks (Off / Auto mix, a two-way segmented control) in its header; the record column shrinks to 56px and a set card takes the top ("Playing Sunday Set · track 4 of 12 · as a mix · Long blend · 32 into Static Bloom"). Matches have Play · Play next · "Add as #5" · More like this.
  - A set's header: Play (primary) · Play as a mix · Export to Traktor… · a "✓ My working set" toggle · "…" (Open its playlist · Duplicate · Make this my working set · Remove this set…). Tags beside the name: Working set, "Playing · 4 of 12" (green); the playing row's number is a green ▶. The sidebar's Sets row shows the working set's name.
  - Settings → Library → DJ: Between tracks as two radio cards (replacing 26h), "Style when Auto can choose", "Key lock in DJ now", and the https note. Off is said once at Up next's head, never per step.
  - Phone: one tab row, Now playing · Mix · Set; first run "Start a set to mix into" with "New set with Night Ferry" / "Choose a set…".
  - Glossary (33f): set · Add to set… · Save as a set · working set · Between tracks: Off / Auto mix · Play as a mix · step Smooth/Jump · style · ready to mix · BPM and key · plays straight on · Mixes well with · Up next · Decks.
- **D · The row's primary button (34a–34c).** **Play** (a filled triangle, white 86%, never accent) is the one visible button; **Play next** (a triangle and bar, `M4 5l10 7-10 7zM16 5h3v14h-3z`) on hover, focus and in the "…"; **Playing** (three bars, green) replaces Play on the playing row; **Move to the top** (an arrow to a bar) is Up next's second icon; the star is the only accent. Reserved columns, so nothing shifts. ⇧-click Play is Play next; a double-click plays. The same five glyphs in Tracks, Listening history (the star's column before them), the Mix tab (Play and Play next always shown), Search (library rows gain the "…"), a set's page and Up next ("Play now" cuts to it). Phone: a tap plays; the sheet is Star · Play next · Mixes well with… · Add to set… · Send to… · Add to playlist….
- **E · DJ now, E1 (35a–35k, 35o, 35p; 36a).** The tray's third mode, 520px tall, columns 280 / 1fr / 360 at 1440 and 240 / 1fr / 300 at 1280. A is always the playing track and B is Up next's first row; opened with nothing next, B is empty and asks to be cued (35a2).
  - **Left:** the tray tabs, then the A (accent-light edge) and B (cyan `oklch(0.85 0.1 200)` edge) records with key · tempo and the stretch ("124.0 (−0.8%)", moving while mixing: "124.6 → 126.0") · readiness; then a status card (Auto / Auto is mixing / You're mixing / Won't beat-match / Waiting for the grid) with its buttons (Mix now · Change style · Cancel · Take over · Back to Night Ferry · Finish now · Hand back to auto · Take it by hand · Echo out ▾).
  - **Middle:** badges (Beats locked · Phrase-aligned · Long blend · 32 bars, or "Won't beat-match: Sand Script has no steady beat" in amber), Between tracks, Preview output, Expand to Decks. The lanes on one bar ruler (phrase ticks every 8 bars, bar numbers in 10px mono): A's sections over A's wave, B's wave over B's sections, the blend window shaded with its label, the bass swap as a dashed amber line, the filter lane (A's colour into B's), the echo lane and the playhead. Drag B along the ruler (snaps to phrases, then bars; ⌥ for beats) and Lock. A text equivalent on the lanes.
  - **Below:** ten hand controls (crossfader, Vol A/B, EQ A/B hi · mid · lo, Filter, Echo, Sync, Nudge, Cue · Loop A), each tagged AUTO (cyan) or YOU; touching one takes over and auto holds.
  - **Right:** Next, with source tabs (Up next · Mixes well with · the working set · Starred · Vibe); rows with a readiness dot · key · Smooth/Jump · reason · Cue (Cued in cyan; Analyse where not ready).
  - **States (35a–35k):** idle, counting down, auto mixing, taken over, handed back, can't be beat-matched, analysing the incoming track, a drag hovering, Vibe thinking, the AI off.
  - **35o:** Preview in the headphones (a second output; Play the pair · Play B; Test left and right) or "Preview takes over the speakers" (pauses what plays, Back to Night Ferry); the way in (D, the bar's DJ button beside Mix now, dragging a row to the bar's bottom edge, "Open in DJ now" at the picker's foot, the DJ tab beside Mix); invalid drops with "Analyse now and cue".
  - **35p:** Decks expanded, with continuity (what you touched stays touched).

**Build order for Dev (Design's, after Ross's E1)**
1. **Brief A** (31a–31g) on top of `feat/analyse-now`: the indicator everywhere, the topbar count and popover, member actions, the Added toast line.
2. **Brief C's names and Between tracks** (33e, 33f): Off / Auto mix in Settings → Library → DJ and Up next's header; the glossary applied; 302's fallback rule and words.
3. **Brief D** (34a–34c) and **Brief C's tray** (33a–33d): three modes, Mix beside Up next, the Mixes panel retired, the set header and working-set control, the two-level phone sheet.
4. **Brief B** (32a–32g): drag and drop across the surfaces that now exist.
5. **Brief E · DJ now** (35a–35k, 35o, 35p; 36a), after Dev's answers to 325–332.

The UX review's other Dev-tagged findings are folded into the step whose surface they touch.

**Design's answers 260–324** (every one is also on its board; "Fine" means build it as described)

**Sets 1**
- **260.** Words fine; show "Mixes.wiki". Name what would be searched, not what was: "Mixes.wiki isn't searched yet: SYNCHRO.S is still checking its terms of use." Admin-only switch, no Settings control until then: right.
- **262.** Fine, and the saved set's "…" gains Duplicate and the working-set toggle (33c): Open its playlist · Duplicate · Make this my working set · Remove this set… (33c)
