- **263.** Keep them in place, muted, with a "Hide 4 not in your library" fold on the set's head for when the gaps are known. The steps skipping them is right.
- **264.** Yes, leave it out until DJ now: its Preview plays the pair in the headphones or takes the speakers (35o). Drop it from Swap's panel meanwhile. (35o)
- **265.** Follow, while the set still owns the playlist. Once the playlist was changed elsewhere (274) the set stops touching it, name included.
- **266.** Words fine; place it under the tab pair as one 14px meta line, not beside it, so the tabs don't stretch.
- **267.** A proper confirm on 15a's frame: "Remove Sunday Set? Its playlist stays." Remove · Keep. Never a browser confirm.
- **268.** Fine. Under the title and meta, over the count card.
- **274.** Words and tone right. "Make a new playlist from this set" as the secondary, "Not now" as the quiet one; the Notice is dismissable for good per set.
- **275.** Right, and 32a draws it for a drag: the line never opens at a locked row's place; a drop on it lands at the nearest free place. (32a, 32b)

**Phase E**
- **270.** Fine. The drive named once, the folders in mono under it.
- **271.** Fine. Last part as the name, full path as the title; the date left out when unknown.
- **272.** Above the bar, anchored to it, as a popover pointing down at the Add to playlist… button, not centred: the eye is already there.
- **273.** Right.

**Stars and mix sets**
- **276.** Add the "…" with 24a's order to Search's library rows (34b). Drag and drop's "Send to…" lives there too. Results that aren't yours keep Download only. (34b)
- **277.** Keep it, as "Add to Up next", in the shared "…" everywhere, after Play next: appending without jumping the queue is a real need, not an Artist-page one.
- **278.** Yes, History follows 28d with the star's column before Play and "…" (34b). (34b)
- **279.** Beside the key line once there is one (29a draws it there). Until then, first in the control row is right.
- **280.** Fine. Two rows, two stars.
- **281.** Right. Saved sets only in Recent.
- **282.** Right. "An admin rescans the library", disabled, as 31a's member rule.
- **283.** Reset it to Auto when the next track changes: the chip describes a pair; a different pair gets Auto's pick and says "auto" again. Starting length: 318's rule.
- **284.** Fine.
- **285.** Right.
- **286.** One line is enough. Up to 500 characters, cut with an ellipsis, whole on the title.

**The Mix tab**
- **288.** Right words. "…and one step on the wheel. Wider: 10% and any key."
- **289.** Keep the rule; 29a's example is corrected to Jump on 33b. (33b)
- **290.** 500 whenever a track of yours plays, 360 for the rest; in DJ it's 520 (35a). Fine.
- **291.** Two levels now (33d): one tab row, Now playing · Mix · Set, under the handle; no title, no back row. Swipe down closes from any tab. (33d)
- **292.** Hide the primary while the card shows. New set makes "Set from Monday 27 Oct" without asking; the name is inline-editable after (33d, 33e). (33d)
- **293.** Right. "Add as #5" says where it goes (33b).
- **294.** Good enough, and better now: a queue started by Play as a mix is marked "from Sunday Set" (33b), so the set's state doesn't need to be inferred for that case.
- **295.** Fine. The menu as described; the working set also has its toggle on set pages and Your sets (33c, 33e).
- **296.** Remember it per browser, but only while the AI is on; with the AI off, Vibe is off and says so (35k, UX-17).
- **297.** Yes, direction weighs more when a set is working: a track that turns a rising set down drops below same-key tracks that keep the direction, with "energy ↓2" as its reason. With no working set, harmonic fit leads as now.
- **298.** First, before Play next, as a headphones icon, and only when a preview route exists (a second output, or "takes the speakers" accepted once). Until then the slot stays hidden.
- **299.** Fine, and with Brief A it becomes the indicator: "In line · 3rd" when queued, the bar when running (31c). (31c)

**Auto-mix stage A**
- **300.** Show the grid's BPM once a track has one, everywhere, to one decimal where it differs from a whole number, with the tagged value as the hover title ("Tagged 123"). Tags no longer win over a measured grid.
- **301.** Say how far, in the indicator's popover (31b): "Finding beats · 1,240 of 4,980". Chips keep "Waiting" and the step line; Up next doesn't repeat the sweep. (31b)
- **302.** Rewritten without a crossfade. The rule: the outgoing track has a grid → "cutting on the last phrase"; only the incoming has one → "echo out into Static Bloom"; neither → "plays straight on". So: "Couldn't beat-match: cutting on the phrase · the tempos are 9.7% apart" · "…: echo out · Sand Script has no steady beat" · "…: plays straight on · Sweet Waltz isn't in 4/4" · "…: plays straight on · A Mix is too long to analyse" · "… can't be opened" · "… couldn't be analysed". 35g shows it in place. (35g)
- **303.** Both fine; a long blend that doesn't fit the outro becomes a short blend, and the chip says "Short blend · 8 · the outro is short", rather than shrinking in silence.
- **304.** Show it: an unsure "one" keeps its style with the amber dot and "Not sure where the bars start" (26g's warning dot). Not 4/4 is 302's "plays straight on", never a crossfade.

**Auto-mix stage B**
- **305.** Disabled with the reason: "Too near the end of Night Ferry to go back". Hiding it would make the hand control vanish mid-mix.
- **306.** The incoming track's own BPM with the change at the start, fixed, in the tray's record ("126.0 · from −1.6%"); DJ now's lanes show the moving figure (35d: "124.6 → 126.0").
- **307.** Offer Key lock as a hand control in DJ now and Decks, off by default, and a setting under Between tracks (33e). Auto keeps the planner's rule.
- **308.** New words, no crossfade: "If a call or the lock stops the sound, SYNCHRO.S cuts to the next track when it's back." The mix finishes with a cut, not a 2-second crossfade; the interrupted countdown plays out straight on.
- **309.** Keep Test: left and Test: right on both routes.
- **310.** Fine; no evening-out setting. The player plays at its own level after.
- **311.** For Ross. Design's part: until the check is lifted the chip is honest, "Echo out · breaks aren't locked yet", not "Crossfade".

**Auto-mix stage C**
- **312.** Words right, said "Auto mix needs…"; yes: the setup check gets an info row and Settings → Connections points at https. The control is disabled with "Needs https or localhost" in all three places (33e).
- **313.** Fine. The bar reads the element; DJ now's lanes read the deck. A second's drift at the end is acceptable in the bar.
- **314.** Show it for automatic mixes too, the last 16 bars, in the tray and DJ now with Cancel (35c); the bar keeps "in 2:58" until then.
- **315.** Right.
- **316.** Cancel plays the track out, straight on. No fallback; the chip returns to its style for the next time.
- **317.** Fine, and the next comes in by 302's rule: a cut on the phrase where both have grids, else straight on. No 6 s crossfade.
- **318.** Fine.
- **319.** Right place, and it moves before Export (33c). Drafts have it too; a chip changed in Up next asks once "Save it to the set too?" instead of being lost.
- **320.** Show what plays, once: with Auto mix off, the list's head says "Auto mix is off: tracks play straight on · Turn it on" and each set chip is dimmed with no line of its own (UX-21). Play as a mix uses the set's chips whatever the setting.
- **321.** Acceptable now; DJ now's Decks note says "the decks skip the player's EQ". Pass-through later if it's cheap.
- **322.** Rule right, without the crossfade: an album in its order stays gapless; out of order or a playlist plays straight on (gapless where the files allow); Auto mix beat-matches where it's on and both are ready.
- **323.** Hide it, and bring it back as "Open DJ now" (35a) when E lands.
- **324.** Yes, add it back under the mixing sheet as one line, with 308's words.

### 2026-10-27: UX review of the DJ features, and Briefs A–E for Design

A usability review of everything built for the DJ area (Sets 1, stars and mix sets, the Mix tab, auto-mix stages A–C and the lab), run in a browser at 1440 and 390 with a library of real, analysed tracks, against 24a's model and WCAG 2.2 AA. Ross accepted all 31 findings (27 October). **Dev** fixes the Dev-tagged ones: UX-1 (Play as a mix never played a set) is fixed on `fix/play-as-mix`; UX-2, 4 and 5 and the lab's sources (UX-14) are being built on `feat/analyse-now` with analysis on demand; the rest follow in a Dev fix round. **Design** has the five briefs below, which cover the Design-tagged findings (UX-3, 10, 11, 14, 25) and Ross's "DJ now" (BACKLOG, "DJ now").

**Ross's decisions that shape the briefs (27 October)**
- No crossfading at all: Between tracks is **Off / Auto mix**. A pair that can't be beat-matched quietly uses echo out or a cut, or plays straight on; nothing says "crossfade". So the open questions that assume a crossfade (302 and 304's fallback, 308's 2-second crossfade when a phone cuts a mix off, 316's Cancel, 320's line and 322) need answering against that: a cut, an echo out or straight on.
- Tracks are analysed on demand where they'll be mixed, with a small progress bar and a line naming the step.
- Drag and drop for mix sets, Up next and the DJ panels.
- DJ now is laptop and desktop only, and full Decks is DJ now expanded.

**The 31 findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| UX-1 | Blocker | Play as a mix never plays a set | Dev. |
| UX-2 | Major | Analysis is reported as in progress when nothing is running | Dev. |
| UX-3 | Major | You can't follow analysis | Design. |
| UX-4 | Major | Analysis isn't triggered where a track will be mixed; members can't trigger it | Dev. |
| UX-5 | Major | "Analysed" means two different things | Dev. |
| UX-6 | Major | Up next at 1440 cuts off the reason and "Analyse now" | Dev. |
| UX-7 | Major | A, and the Mix tab's primary button, add a duplicate when the track is already in the working set | Dev. |
| UX-8 | Major | No undo for set edits | Dev. |
| UX-9 | Major | Mix now fails silently when the decks can't start | Dev. |
| UX-10 | Major | Drag and drop missing between DJ surfaces and on touch | Design. |
| UX-11 | Major | No clear home for mixing | Design. |
| UX-12 | Major | You can't tell whether a mix is beat-matched, or why it isn't | Dev. |
| UX-13 | Major | The DJ mix setting is buried | Dev (placement with Brief C). |
| UX-14 | Major | The auto-mix lab is hard to use as it stands | Design. |
| UX-15 | Minor | The countdown floods the polite live region and is silent on Cancel | Dev. |
| UX-16 | Minor | Phone touch targets under 44px in the DJ controls | Dev. |
| UX-17 | Minor | Vibe switches on even when the AI is off | Dev. |
| UX-18 | Minor | Famous mix says it searches sources it won't search, and tells members to do admin things | Dev. |
| UX-19 | Minor | An empty draft points to a path that can't reach it | Dev. |
| UX-20 | Minor | Names drift | Dev. |
| UX-21 | Minor | "DJ mix is off: plays straight on (Play as a mix plays this)" repeats on every step | Dev. |
| UX-22 | Minor | The style picker clips off-screen | Dev. |
| UX-23 | Minor | Tracks' selection bar is cramped at 1440 | Dev. |
| UX-24 | Minor | The "New here? Click for setup help!" nudge sits above sheets and bars | Dev. |
| UX-25 | Minor | Tracks rows' accent ▶ means "Play next" | Design. |
| UX-26 | Minor | The phone Set view mislabels a missing file and shows "— — BPM" | Dev. |
| UX-27 | Minor | Keyboard reordering is silent, and long lists are tab-heavy | Dev. |
| UX-28 | Minor | No main landmark; set headers become extra banners | Dev (app-wide). |
| UX-29 | Polish | A set's chip doesn't show "auto" or "yours" on screen | Dev. |
| UX-30 | Polish | Move… asks for a bare number | Dev. |
| UX-31 | Polish | The lab looks unfinished and shows others a blank page | Dev. |

**The three concerns behind the briefs**
1. **Analysis you can't see:** "Analysing" showed while nothing ran, a track can have BPM and key but no grid and still read "analysed", and nothing queues a track when it's added where it'll be mixed.
2. **Drag and drop:** only Up next and a set's own list reorder by dragging; nothing moves between surfaces, and touch has no path.
3. **How the DJ pieces fit:** the docked Mixes panel and the Mix tab rank the same thing two ways, the working set is invisible outside the Mix tab, and the DJ mix setting sits in the player's "…" only.

**Briefs for Design** (draw every state named; answer on the canvas, naming the board, as before)

#### Brief A · The analysis indicator (concern 1; UX-2, 3, 4, 5, 12)

**Goal.** At any moment a person can see whether a track is **ready to mix** (it has a beat grid), what's being analysed now, which step it's on and how far along, and can ask for it from where they are. Members can prioritise their own tracks.

**Engine truth.**
- One sweep at a time. Per track the steps are:
  1. reading audio;
  2. tempo;
  3. key;
  4. energy;
  5. beat grid;
  6. phrases and sections.
- The sweep waits for downloads, imports, a drive that's away, a drive move or tag writing. An admin can stop it.
- A track can have BPM and key but no grid. Auto-mix needs the grid.
- Dev would publish `{track_id, title, step, queue_position}`.

**Where it lives.**
1. A global indicator: the topbar's running count with a popover (the Activity sheet on a phone).
2. Tracks: the line and per-row markers.
3. A set's page: per-row states and "3 tracks aren't ready to mix · Analyse them now".
4. The Mix tab header and the phone sheet.
5. Every Up next chip.
6. The lab's pickers.
7. Brief E's DJ panel.

**The pattern.** A 4px bar plus one line, "Analysing Canopy · beat grid (5 of 6)". The words carry the meaning. The popover holds a six-step checklist.

**States to draw.**
1. Ready to mix.
2. BPM and key only (no grid).
3. Not analysed, sweep idle ("next sweep in about 25 min" + Analyse now / Analyse this).
4. Queued ("3rd in line").
5. In progress, at each of the six steps.
6. Waiting, for each reason.
7. Stopped by an admin.
8. Failed (unreadable) and skipped (too long), with what still works: "won't auto-mix; plays straight on".
9. No steady beat: "Can't be beat-matched".
10. Done, announced once, with the chip turning from Waiting to its style.
11. A set with 12 of 40 not ready ("Analyse all 12").
12. The member's view.
13. Phone (390): a compact indicator in the sheet header and a two-line chip.
14. Reduced motion: a static bar.

**Triggers.** Adding to a set, the working set, Up next with DJ mix on, Play as a mix, the lab and the Brief E panel each queue the track first, and say so once ("Added · analysing, 2nd in line").

#### Brief B · Drag and drop across the DJ area (concern 2; UX-10)

**Goal.** Build and adjust sets and the queue by dragging, as in Traktor or Rekordbox, with equal keyboard and touch paths.

**Sources:** Tracks rows (and a multi-selection), Search's library tracks, album and artist track rows, Mix tab matches, the Mixes panel, Up next rows, a set's rows.

**Targets:**
- a set's list (insert between rows, with the new steps' Smooth/Jump previewed on the insertion line);
- a set in the sidebar or Your sets (append);
- the working-set strip (append, or after the playing track while a set plays);
- Up next (insert at a place, or Play next at the top);
- a transition gap (insert there, previewing both new chips);
- Brief E's DJ panel (cue as next; laptop and desktop only).

**States to draw (desktop, 1440).**
1. Rest (grip on hover and focus).
2. Lifted ghost (title, key, BPM, a count for many).
3. A valid hover for each target type.
4. The insertion line with a step preview.
5. An invalid target with its reason (file missing, not in the library, a locked row's place, a set you can't edit, mid-mix "can't replace the incoming track").
6. A duplicate ("In it already · #4 · drop to add again").
7. Auto-scroll near the edges.
8. Drop, then a toast with Undo.
9. Locked rows holding while others flow around them.

**Phone (390).**
1. Long-press lift.
2. Drag within a list.
3. Drop onto a bottom "Send to…" tray (the working set, Up next, Other sets, DJ panel).
4. Cancel.
5. A "Move" mode with ▲▼ as the non-drag path.

**Keyboard.**
1. Space to pick up.
2. Arrows to move, with live announcements.
3. Enter to drop, Escape to cancel.
4. "Send to…" in "…" for cross-surface moves.

Draw the focus on the lifted row.

**Constraints:**
- Library tracks with their file only.
- One drop is one undoable step.
- A stale drop gets the existing "changed elsewhere" Notice.

#### Brief C · Where to go for what (concern 3; UX-11, 13, 20)

**Goal.** Make 24a's model visible: **Sets** (things you keep), **Now and next** (the player: Up next, the Mix tab, auto-mix) and **Decks** (by hand; see Brief E for the compact bridge).

**Explore.**
1. **One "Mixes well with".** The docked panel either becomes the Mix tab following a chosen track, or goes. It needs one ranking, one wording and one set of actions (Play next, Add to the working set, More like this).
2. **The working set outside the Mix tab:** a tag and "Make this my working set" on set pages and in Your sets, and the sidebar's Sets row showing the working set's name.
3. **A set's page header:** Play, Play as a mix (auto-mix for this play), Export and the working-set control.
4. **Between tracks (Off / Auto mix; Smart crossfade goes, Ross 27 October):** one control, reachable from Up next's header, the Mix tab and Settings → Library (DJ), not only from the player's "…".
5. **A glossary pass** (set or mix set, Add to set or Add to mix set…, the analysis words, together with Brief A).
6. **Links between Sets, the Mix tab, the lab and Decks.**

**States to draw:**
- the tray at 1440 with Mix and Up next together;
- the phone sheet reduced from three levels ("Now playing" title, "‹ Now playing", Mix/Set) to two;
- a first-run state ("Start a set to mix into");
- a set playing as a mix: "Track 4 of 12" in the tray, the set's page highlighting the playing row, and Add inserting after it.

#### Brief D · The row's primary action (UX-25)

Should the visible row button be **Play** (audition now) or **Play next**, and how do their icons differ? Draw Tracks rows at rest, on hover and on focus, the phone row, and Up next's "play next" icon. Use one icon set across Tracks, Listening history, the Mix tab and Search.

#### Brief E · "DJ now": a compact DJ mode in the playing panel (Ross's request; UX-12, UX-14)

**Goal.** Without leaving what's playing, see the outgoing and incoming tracks side by side with their sections, beat grids locked and the blend zone. Choose the next track by dragging it in or picking from candidates. Let auto-mix drive it, or take over live. Expand it to full Decks.

**How it fits.**
- **Desktop:** a third mode of the tray, Now playing · Mix · **DJ** (or Mix grows into it). Alternatively, the waveform's place expands into DJ when a transition is armed.
- **Show** its relation to Up next (the next track is Up next's first row) and to the working set.
- **Laptop and desktop only** (Ross, 27 October). Nothing on the phone: the phone keeps today's handover view.

**Content.**
1. **Dual waveforms on one bar ruler:**
   - the outgoing lane above, the incoming below, aligned on the phrase;
   - section bands (intro, main, breakdown, drop, outro) from the grid;
   - downbeats and phrase ticks;
   - "beats locked" / "phrase-aligned" badges;
   - the shaded blend window with the style's lanes (bass swap point, filter, echo);
   - the playhead;
   - tempo and key for each, with the stretch percentage.

   This is the beat-match verdict UX-12 lacks. State "won't beat-match: <why>" in place when it applies.
2. **Next-track candidates:** Up next's next, Mixes well with (code ranking, then Vibe/AI), the working set, other sets, playlists and starred. Each shows readiness (Brief A) and the step it would make (Smooth/Jump). Drag in or tap "Cue next".
3. **Auto vs live:**
   - Auto shows the plan and moves the controls by itself, each marked "auto" (26j).
   - Touching any control takes over: the card turns to "You're mixing", and the automation holds without a jump.
   - Hand back lets auto finish.
   - Draw: idle with the next cued, counting down, mixing on auto, mixing taken over, handed back, and a pair that can't be auto-mixed ("plays straight on, or take it by hand").
4. **Controls (live):**
   - crossfader;
   - per-deck volume;
   - three-band EQ with kills;
   - a filter (HP/LP on one knob);
   - an echo / effects send;
   - Sync on and off, nudge, a cue point and a loop for the outgoing deck;
   - headphones (23b) where available.

 5. **Expand to full Decks:** the same lanes grown into 21e's two decks, with continuity (what you touched stays touched).
6. **Drag and drop into the panel:** from any source in Brief B. Drop on the incoming lane to cue, or on the blend zone to change the style. Invalid drops (not ready, missing file) explain why and offer Analyse now.
7. **Accessibility:**
   - lanes have text equivalents ("bar 12 of 32 · bass swaps at 16 · 0:23 left");
   - controls are real sliders with values;
   - announce once per state (UX-15).

**States to draw:**
- 1440: idle, cued, auto mixing, taken over, handed back, can't be beat-matched, analysing the incoming track;
- the smallest laptop width the tray supports (1280), as well as 1440;
- a drag hovering the panel;
- Vibe or AI candidates thinking, and the AI off.

**Constraints:**
- No crossfade style (Ross: auto, manual or nothing). The crossfader stays as a hand control; nothing is labelled a crossfade transition.
- Respect the engine: windows, not whole tracks, and the per-profile decode cap.

#### Brief E, options to explore: where DJ now lives (Ross, 27 October: "I want options")

Ross's idea, pitched for exploration and not decided: a **set builder / mix explorer rail** that pulls down from the top of Tracks, Albums and Artists, beside the list you're browsing. Into it you drag tracks to assemble a mix on the fly or add to an existing set; pick two tracks onto **A and B transport rows**, synced; and drag their sections against each other on one ruler and lock them there. Draw it as a real option beside Brief E's tray mode, and add any others you think are stronger. Ross will choose from the boards.

**Options to draw, side by side, at 1440 and 1280:**
- **E1 · In the playing panel:** Brief E as written, a mode of the tray (Now playing · Mix · DJ), or the waveform's place growing into DJ when a transition is armed.
- **E2 · The rail:** pulls down from the top of Tracks, Albums and Artists (and Search's tracks, if it fits). Two heights at least:
  - **collapsed:** a thin strip holding the Build row (the mix being assembled, or the set it's adding to) and A/B's names, leaving the list usable;
  - **open:** about half the height, with the A and B lanes on one ruler, the candidates and the controls.

  The open height expands into full Decks, as E1 does.
- **E3 and on (Design's own):** for example, a side dock where the Mixes panel sits today, or the rail and the tray as one surface seen from two places.

**For every option, show:**
- **What it replaces.** The aim is fewer places to mix, not one more (UX-11). Each option should say what it absorbs: the docked Mixes panel ("Mixes well with" becomes its candidates), the working-set strip (its Build row), and Brief E's tray mode if the rail wins, or the rail if the tray wins.
- **Build:** drag tracks in (Brief B's sources), reorder, lock; "Save as a set" or "Add to Sunday Set". The Smooth/Jump step shows on each join, as in a set.
- **A and B:** choose two tracks (drag onto a row, or "Send to A / B"), synced on the grid, with tempo, key and the stretch for each, and the ready-to-mix state (Brief A).
- **Sections side by side:** drag B along A's ruler so B's intro sits over A's outro (or breakdown over breakdown). It snaps to phrases and bars, can be locked, and becomes that pair's blend window when the mix plays. Show "won't beat-match: <why>" where it applies.
- **Hearing it while something else plays:** the pair can't share the speakers with what's playing. Draw both ways: preview in the headphones on a second output (23b, where the browser allows it), or "Preview takes over the speakers", pausing what plays and offering Back to it.
- **The way into it** from each page: a control, a key, and dragging a track to the top edge.
- **The handover to the player:** what happens to the rail's mix when you press Play (it becomes Up next with Auto mix on, or plays as a set).

**Dev's notes on the idea:**
- Placing B against A's sections is the planner's blend window chosen by hand (the engine supports it). **Rearranging a track's own sections** (a re-edit: moving its drop before its breakdown) is out: Ross doesn't want it (27 October). Sections move only against the other track.
- Laptop and desktop only, as Brief E.
- The rail opens over the list, so the collapsed height needs to leave at least about ten rows visible at 1280 × 800.

### 2026-10-27: Dev, auto-mix stage C of 27 October

Built on `feat/automix-c` from `integration` (1387e8cd), from BACKLOG "Auto-mix: beat-matched transitions in Up next", `Auto-mix (Up next, decks).dc.html` (26a–26j, approved by Ross), `DJ area map (six briefs).dc.html` (24a's keys), the README's Auto-mix and Keys sections and `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-24 (late).dc.html`. **Stage C: auto-mix in real playback, Up next's chips, Mix now, the handover view, Player settings → Between tracks and Smart crossfade.** Decks (21e; 26j's watching and taking over) comes after. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **No new route or table.** **Gauntlet:** the one server change is `core/dj/window.py` (already on `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt`; its test file is marked `gauntlet`). Interfaces: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Auto-mix stage C"; patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, "Auto-mix in the player" and the Decks tokens; rows: `SCREENS.md`. BACKLOG: "Auto-mix: Building (stage C)".

**What was built, board by board**
- **26a, 26b (Up next with DJ mix on):** a gap after each row from the playing one on (`static/media-player.js` draws it; `features/automix` fills it), each with the transition chip and its line ("auto · 8A → 9A, +2 BPM"; the phone's short line), the first with "in 2:58". Nothing with Off or Smart crossfade, nor on shuffle. **Mix now** beside Next in the bar and the tray (key X); the waveform marks where the mix starts in Deck B's cyan.
- **26a, 26c (the picker):** the pair and why ("9A → 4B, +1 BPM: the keys clash"), "Auto: Cut / Chosen because the keys clash", the seven styles with 26a's lines, a style the pair can't do listed `aria-disabled` with why ("Needs a drop: none found in Brass Tide", from the plan's `styles`), Length 2 · 4 · 8 · 16 · 32. It floats over the tray (a sheet on a phone) and stays open while you choose. A choice lasts for this queue (it's kept on the row, 283's rule); a queue from a set uses the set's chip, and a change asks "Save it to Sunday Set too?" in place.
- **26g (every chip state):** auto, yours, covering a jump (the dot stays amber), Waiting ("Sand Script is being analysed · Analyse now" for admins, asked again every 30 s), Crossfade ("couldn't beat-match: <why>"), Mixing now, In 6 bars with Cancel, and a set's chip with DJ mix off (dimmed, with what plays instead).
- **26d (while it mixes):** the record shows both tracks (going out dimmed with A's edge, coming in with B's and its tempo, "124.0 (−1.6%)"); the waveform's place holds two lanes on one bar ruler (the windows' own levels, worked out from the decoded audio before the decks take it: finer than the 200-peak waveform), the window shaded, the playhead, the bass swap or drop marker; "Long blend · 32 bars · bass swaps at bar 16 · 0:23 left"; Back to Night Ferry, Finish now, and Watch it on Decks disabled with "Decks comes in a later step". Pause pauses both.
- **26e (the bar):** counting down in bars with Cancel ("starts on the next phrase · Long blend · 32 bars"); mixing with both titles, the bar and the two-line handover bar, Back and Finish now; the fallback's status line "Couldn't beat-match: crossfading · <why>". A polite live region says each once.
- **26f (a phone):** the same record and lanes in the sheet; the screen-on warning lives in Player settings (324).
- **26h (Player settings → Between tracks):** Off / Smart crossfade / DJ mix as radio cards, Mix automatically and "Style when Auto can choose" (Auto's own pick, Long blends, Short blends) with DJ mix, the note on podcasts, audiobooks and radio, and on a phone the warning. Kept per browser; the "…" menu's Crossfade is Smart crossfade.
- **26i (a mix set's transitions):** the set's chip from the pair's plan (Auto naming its pick), always shown, dimmed with what plays instead while DJ mix is off; **Play as a mix** on a saved set (28e) plays it with its chips, DJ mix for that play.
- **24a's X** is in `NP_LETTER_KEYS` (`X: ['automix:X']`), behind the same guard as M, N and A; it acts only with DJ mix on and something to mix into, else the key is the page's. The shortcuts list has it.

**The handover** (`features/automix/controller.ts`). The player keeps playing every track on its `<audio>` element; the engine only ever plays a transition. With DJ mix on, for the playing track and the next (library tracks with their files): the pair is planned with its chip (stage A); about 45 s before `out.start` the grids and both windows come through the loader's line, one at a time; **two bars before `out.start`** (at least 3 s) deck A starts on the context's clock where the element will be 0.12 s later, the element fades out under it over 80 ms and plays on muted (keeping time for the bar, the record, lyrics, the Media Session and scrobbling), and the engine mixes deck B in on the phrase. **When the mix is over and deck B is back at its own speed** (the end of the window's tempo morph; 4 bars after Finish now), the player loads the incoming track with deck B as the hand-off it joins (the crossfade's own `startAudioPlayback` path), and the decks let their windows go. Why hand back rather than keep the incoming track on the engine to the next transition: the decks play decoded windows (a whole track would be 100–200 MB a deck in the browser), and everything else the player does (seek, scrobble, lyrics, Media Session, EQ, the waveform, the queue's own advance) is the element's. While the decks play, `ownsAdvance()` keeps `onAudioEnded` and the crossfade out of it; their level is the player's volume.

**What the listener does mid-mix:** Pause pauses both (the context is suspended as the listener's: `LifecycleWatch.setPaused`, so it isn't taken for an interruption); a seek during the countdown cancels that mix and the element plays on from where it was sent (it can come again if the seek went back before the run-up), during the mix it finishes over one bar; Next during the mix cuts to the incoming track where it is, during the countdown it cancels and skips as before; Previous, a row or a page's Play let the decks go at once; a queue edit that changes the next track (or DJ mix turned off) cancels a countdown, and a mix in progress finishes into its track, which is put back after the playing one if it was removed; a track whose window ends before the mix finishes it at once. Shuffle, repeat one and the sleep timer's end of track never arm a mix.

**Design's 252, as built in stage B, now in the player:** a countdown that's interrupted (the context suspended, or a phone's page hidden) is cancelled and the track plays out; a mix cut off finishes with a 2-second crossfade once sound is back; on a phone a page going hidden finishes the mix over one bar before the lock stops it. The warning is said in Player settings (26h), not promised away (324). **Design's 249 in the UI:** the first chip's "in 2:58" is `out.start` less the element's position, and the waveform's cyan mark sits at `out.start`; Mix now moves both to the next phrase.

**DJ mix needs a secure page** (found in the browser): AudioWorklet is only given to https or `localhost`, so SYNCHRO.S over plain http on a LAN address can't mix. `mixSupport()` says why: DJ mix is listed disabled with it, and pairs crossfade with the reason on their chips (312).

**Smart crossfade** (26h): the player's crossfade, plus, where the tracks are analysed: the fade ends where the outgoing track's sound does (from its waveform, at most 15 s early) and starts on the downbeat just before (within 2 s) when it has a grid; the next track starts past its own silence (up to 8 s in, a media fragment); and it comes in at the outgoing track's loudness (ReplayGain, else the analysed levels; only ever turned down), then the player goes back to its own level over 3 s. Not between an album's tracks in their order (322). Without that data it's the crossfade exactly as before. With DJ mix on, a pair that can't be beat-matched (or is still being analysed) crossfades at the plan's place the same way; a mix that misses its windows crossfades at the end.

**Small fixes from the stage B review**, each tested:
- **The window fetch has a deadline:** `AbortSignal.timeout(90_000)` on each request (the body included), so a hung request can't hold the tab's line; the next window goes ahead (`loader.test.ts`).
- **No writable temp directory:** where `TemporaryFile()` raises `OSError`, ffmpeg's errors go to `DEVNULL` and the window is decoded as usual, never a 500 (`tests/dj/test_window_route.py`, through the route too; it fails without the fix).
- **The night's three failures:** AI settings' "added by ross today", Activity's Today heading and Wanted's "Found today" date their fixtures within today (an hour back, or half the time since midnight, whichever is less); checked with the clock moved to 00:30 Los Angeles time, where all three failed before. The automations test that edits the follow-ups has 45 s (the same steps and checks).

**Measured in a real browser** (a scratch server with generated 124, 126 and 127 BPM tracks analysed by stage A's real analysis, real plans and windows, Chromium): planned on play, both windows fetched at 45 s, the decks took over at "bar 2 to go", mixed, and after Finish now the player held Static Bloom on its element, unmuted and playing, at the deck's place (19.9 s).

**Left out, and why:** Decks and 26j (watching auto-mix drive them, taking over, Hand back): Decks is the next build; the engine's `takeover()` and `handBack()` are ready for it. The decks don't pass through the player's EQ and normalization (321). Key lock (307) stays the planner's rule. Which BPM to show (300) and the sweep's progress in Up next (301) wait for answers. A draft set has no Play as a mix (319).

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/dj/test_window_route.py` and `pytest -m gauntlet`; `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check` (0 errors), `npm run build`; vitest on `features/automix` (new: the controller against the real executor on fake decks, the plans, the chip, the host and the handover pieces, Play as a mix), `features/engine`, `features/sets`, `features/mix`, the media player's tests (new: the crossfade's hint, the advance, the gaps, X) and the sets page; the full suite before the push.

**After the gauntlet review** (an independent review of d0d6511c: 3 High, 1 Medium, 4 Low). Each fix has a regression test; the vitest ones are named `[gauntlet H1]` and so on.

- **H1, the element could be left muted:** the takeover's mute, the element's fade and the hand-back's timers weren't tracked, so a row, Next or a failed mix in the first moments after the decks took over could mute the element after they'd let go. Every timeout and fade the controller starts is now its own (cleared, with a session count, whenever it lets go or stops), each checks it's still the same mix before acting, and the timers of the hand-back, the rejoin and the end of a mix (L4) are tracked too. Last line: whenever no mix holds the element, it's unmuted and its level put back (on every tick, on Play and when the decks let go). Tests: a row and Next at 0, 50 and 150 ms after the takeover, `mix()` throwing, and a muted element with no mix on Play (`controller.test.ts`).
- **H2, audio that doesn't come from the queue let a mix carry on:** a station, a podcast, a stream or a cleared track started mid-mix kept the decks playing under it, and the mix's end replaced it with the incoming track. Each of those loaders now calls `SoulSyncAutomix.stop()` first (`CONTRACTS.md`), and the controller lets go by itself when the element loads something it didn't ask for or the playing track stops being the outgoing one. Tests: a station mid-mix through the hook and without it, a stray `loadstart`, the hand-back's own load still going through (`controller.test.ts`), and every loader calling the hook first (`media-player-automix.test.ts`).
- **H3, Play as a mix with Smart crossfade crossfaded over the decks:** the player's crossfade tick now does nothing while auto-mix owns the advance. Test: a set played as a mix with Smart crossfade on starts no crossfade while the decks hold it, and crossfades as before once they let go (`media-player-automix.test.ts`).
- **M1, a mix finishing into a moved track skipped rows:** the incoming track is now moved to straight after the playing one (`SoulSyncPlayer.moveNext`) rather than jumped to; removed and added again as a new row, it's found by its id in the next few rows rather than added twice; removed, it's put back once. `insertNext` now saves the queue. Tests: moved down, removed and re-added, removed (`controller.test.ts`).
- **L1, X and A acted twice in the Import inbox:** one rule for pages with letter keys of their own: the inbox claims `j k a d x` while it has rows, and the player leaves those letters to it (`CONTRACTS.md`); the inbox also ignores a key already acted on. Tests: `media-player-mix-keys.test.ts`, the import route's test.
- **L2:** saving a transition to its set refreshes that set's page (its query is invalidated), so its next save isn't refused as stale. Test: `automix-host.test.tsx`.
- **L3:** the 120 ms tick runs only while DJ mix is on or a set is played as a mix (or the decks hold something); Smart crossfade fetches its grids and waveforms late in the track, not at its start, and keeps its cache of 40. Tests: no tick with DJ mix off; nothing fetched before the last two minutes (`controller.test.ts`).
- **From the re-review (M1b, L1b):**
  - `moveNext` put a row coming from above the playing one a place too far: the incoming row now takes the playing row's place in that case, and the row's real index is returned. Test `[gauntlet M1b]`, against the player's real `npReorderQueue`: from above (two cases), from further below, already next.
  - Smart crossfade looks at the pair in the track's last two minutes (was one). A waveform the server is still working out (its 202) is no longer kept as "none": it's asked for again after 10 s, twice at most, and the crossfade uses the grid meanwhile. Tests `[gauntlet L1b]`.
- **The lab broke on a waiting pair** (found on the live lab at 1387e8cd, a library mid re-grid): its plan summary read `out.start` and `in.start`, which a `waiting` plan doesn't have. The plan types now say so (`out` and `in` are optional; a `crossfade` plan's `out.start` can be null; `tempo` can be null), and every reader is narrowed: the executor, the windows and the lab's session take only a plan with both places (`isPlaced`), the controller arms, loads, takes over and draws lanes only for one, and Mix now gives up on one without. The lab's summary reads every status; for `waiting` it says which track and why ("Waiting for Night Ferry's beat grid · being analysed") and Play the mix is disabled with that reason. The chip's waiting line names the track the plan waits for (`waiting_for`, not always the incoming one) and says "isn't analysed yet" when the track hasn't been started. Tests (`[gauntlet lab]`): the lab's summary, page and session with `waiting`, `crossfade` and `ok` plans in the server's own shapes; the chips with each, closed, compact and with the picker open; the controller ticked through a whole track with a waiting plan and with a crossfade that has no outgoing start.
