### 2026-10-27: Dev, auto-mix stage B of 27 October

Built on `feat/automix-b` from `integration` (0f89648f), from BACKLOG "Auto-mix: beat-matched transitions in Up next", `Auto-mix (Up next, decks).dc.html` (26a–26j), `DJ area (Traktor, decks).dc.html` (21e's decks) and `Answers 170-236.dc.html` (23b), with Ross's answer to 175 (headphones: "Outputs…", another output where the browser allows, else split one output; kept per browser). **Stage B only: the browser's two-deck engine and a bench to hear it.** No new screen: stage C builds the chips, Mix now, the DJ mix setting and the handover view, and the Decks page comes after. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** one new route that decodes a library file for a caller (`api/dj.py`, already listed) with its decoder `core/dj/window.py` (added to `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt`), the kids guard extended to it (`api/content_guard.py`, listed), and the fold of track rows (`database/music_database.py`, listed); their tests are marked `gauntlet`. Routes and the engine's interface: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Auto-mix stage B". BACKLOG: "Auto-mix: Building (stage B)".

**Buffers, not media elements.** Each deck plays a decoded stretch of its track (a **window**: `GET /api/library/dj/window/<id>`, a minute or two of 16-bit stereo WAV at the browser's rate) from an AudioWorklet, not an `<audio>` element. Beat-matching needs to know, to the sample, where each deck is and to start one on another's phrase; an element's `currentTime` and `play()` can't promise either, and it can't play in an OfflineAudioContext, so it couldn't be measured. Whole tracks would cost 100–200 MB a deck in the browser, so only what a transition needs is fetched (with room to finish or go back): about 11 MB a minute over the network, 23 MB a minute a deck once decoded. The server decodes the window with ffmpeg on the analysis's timeline (since the gauntlet review, a quick seek to 5 s before the window and the rest decoded: below), so the grid's seconds and the window's samples agree whatever the format (an MP3's encoder delay included; tested to the sample). At most two decode at once; each has a deadline.

**The time-stretch, after checking licences** (SYNCHRO.S is MIT): **our own WSOLA in the AudioWorklet**, no library. Looked at: **SoundTouchJS** (LGPL-2.1: usable only as a separate, unmodified, dynamically loaded file; it's WSOLA too, the same family as ours, and the engine needs the stretcher to follow an exact position map rather than report its latency and place approximately), **Rubber Band** (GPL or commercial: no), **Signalsmith Stretch** (MIT, spectral, better on big ratios and on voices; WebAssembly: the one to swap in if WSOLA's artefacts are heard on real music, as a second `mode` beside `stretch` in the deck processor), and the browser's own `preservesPitch` on media elements (licence-free, but no sample-exact start or place). Within the planner's 8% cap WSOLA is at its best. Under 3% the planner's plain speed change is used (a resample: the pitch moves, at most half a semitone); past it, the stretch keeps the pitch. Either way the tempo changes smoothly.

**How the engine works** (`webui/src/features/engine/`):
- **Decks** (`worklet/deck-processor.js`): one AudioWorkletProcessor plays deck A and deck B along **position maps** the main thread sends: knots of (context frame, track seconds, rate). Resample mode reads the source at the map's place (4-point Hermite); stretch mode places 43 ms Hann grains, half overlapping, each centred where the map says and moved at most 4 ms to where the source best continues the grain before (a normalised correlation on a quarter-rate copy, then a few lags at full rate, leaning to the map's own place; over silence it doesn't move). At a rate of 1 it gives the source back exactly. Nothing runs on the main thread per sample.
- **The beat clock** (`clock.ts`): through a transition both decks' places are functions of one count of master beats from the plan's `out.start` (outgoing) and `in.start` (incoming): beat for beat they're on the same count, so the incoming downbeat lands on the outgoing phrase and the two can't drift apart, a drifting track's listed beats included. Half and double time count. The master tempo moves smoothly (a smoothstep) from the outgoing track's to the incoming one's across the window, both decks together, so the incoming deck ends at its own speed for the player to carry on with.
- **Drift from the grids themselves** (`refine.ts`): grids are a few milliseconds out here and there, and a period a hair off adds up over 32 bars. Before a transition the engine finds where each deck's kick really is at every beat of the overlap (the steepest rise of the low band within ±35 ms of the grid's beat) and moves the incoming deck by the difference, smoothed (a median over 8 beats, a knot every 4), within ±25 ms; with fewer than 8 clear beats it leaves it.
- **Each deck's chain** (`graph.ts`): trim (loudness), a DJ mixer's EQ (low shelf 150 Hz, mid 1 kHz, high shelf 7 kHz, each to a −40 dB kill), high-pass and low-pass, volume, crossfader, and an echo (a delay with feedback through a high-pass and a damping low-pass) whose return bypasses the volume and crossfader so an echo out rings on; then the master, a limiter over −1 dBFS, and the player's analyser (`window.SoulSyncPlayer.audioGraph()`), so the visualisers see the mix as they see the player.
- **The styles** (`score.ts`) as beat-timed lanes, scheduled whole and ahead as AudioParam automation, so a busy page or a throttled tab can't make them late: long and short blend (the incoming deck in over the first half with its bass killed, the bass swapped over one beat on the middle downbeat, the outgoing deck out over the second half), bass swap (a quarter-beat swap mid-window), filter sweep, drop swap (the swap on the incoming drop), cut (on the one), echo out (the last beat into a ¾-beat echo, cut on the one, the tail thinning), and the crossfade fallback. The incoming deck comes in at the outgoing track's loudness (ReplayGain, else the analysed levels) and is back at its own by the end (310).
- **The executor** (`executor.ts`): start and Cancel; **Finish now** (every control to the end over one bar from the next beat, the decks on the beat through it, then the incoming deck's tempo back to its own over 4 bars); **Back** (over one bar, the outgoing deck back to its own tempo after); **Take over** and Hand back (26j).
- **Interruptions** (`lifecycle.ts`): the context's `statechange` (`suspended`, Safari's `interrupted`, `closed`), a hidden page on a phone, and a clock that stopped while the state says running.
- **Headphones** (`outputs.ts`, 23b): "On another output" sends the cue through a MediaStream into an `<audio>` element with `setSinkId` (Chrome, Edge, Firefox 116+, Safari 18.4+); where a browser can't, it says 23b's sentence. "Split one output" sends what the speakers got (past the player's analyser, so the visualisers still hear the mix) to the left ear and the cue to the right, each summed to mono. Test: left and Test: right play a tone in one ear. Kept per browser (`soulsync-dj-cue`). The control is in the lab only; the Decks page draws 23b.

**Measured sync** (`sync.offline.test.ts`: the engine and its worklet in a real OfflineAudioContext, through node-web-audio-api; synthetic tracks with known beats, the incoming grid deliberately 3 ms late and 0.04% slow, a 16-bar blend, every beat of the overlap timed in each deck's output): **within 0.81 ms at worst** (median 0.00 ms) for a 1.6% tempo gap (resample), **1.62 ms** (median 0.56 ms) for 5.6% (the stretch), **0.58 ms** for an incoming track drifting from 125 to 128 BPM. Without the correction from the audio, the same wrong grid drifts to 15.2 ms by the end, which is what it's for. The worklet alone puts a click within 3 ms of its map while stretching at 0.94–1.08.

**Measured CPU** (`worklet/cpu.test.ts`: the worklet's own `process()` timed in Node's V8, Chrome's engine, both decks playing): **about 1% of one core resampling, 1.6% stretching** (26 and 41 µs a 128-frame block; the budget is 2,667 µs). The main thread does one pass a transition (the windows' quarter-rate copies and the onset search, a few milliseconds). Memory: the two windows, typically 40–80 MB decoded.

**Design's 250 (taking over: can every scheduled change stop at its current value with no jump?) Yes.** Every change the engine schedules also goes into its own model of the AudioParam's timeline (`automation.ts`, the spec's rules for set, linear and exponential ramps), so it always knows each control's value. Holding uses `cancelAndHoldAtTime` where the browser has it (Chrome, Edge, Safari); where it doesn't (Firefox), `cancelScheduledValues` and the cut ramp rescheduled to end there on its own value: a ramp from the event before to that point lies on the old ramp, so nothing moves (a plain `cancelScheduledValues` would drop the ramp in progress and jump back to where it began). The hold takes effect 30 ms ahead (what the audio thread may already be rendering). The decks' places hold too: their maps are replaced from that moment on with the same place and the tempo they're at, so the beats stay locked while you're "mixing". Tested on every control, with and without `cancelAndHoldAtTime`.

**Design's 251 (Back halfway through a blend: sound, or only before the bass swaps?) Sound, at any point.** Back returns every control from wherever it is over one bar, starting on the next beat, the two decks still on the beat; after the bass swap that's the swap made the other way on a downbeat, the move a DJ makes to undo a clash. What's worth limiting is going back too late: the outgoing track is then brought back only to end. So Back is offered while the outgoing track has at least 4 bars of its own left after the bar back (`canGoBack`; 305 asks how to show it past that). After Back the outgoing deck returns to its own tempo over 4 bars.

**Design's 252 (phones: can SYNCHRO.S tell that Web Audio was suspended, and crossfade instead?) Yes, it can tell, and it does what it can.** It hears the AudioContext's `statechange` (`suspended`, `interrupted` on Safari for a call or the lock, `closed`), a page going hidden (on a phone, where the lock follows; a computer keeps playing a hidden tab, so there it's ignored) and a clock that stopped with the state still `running`. While Web Audio is suspended nothing at all can be heard (the player's own audio goes through the same context), so nothing can crossfade at that moment. What it does: a countdown that's interrupted is cancelled (the track plays out, as without DJ mix); a mix that was cut off finishes with a 2-second crossfade from wherever it stopped as soon as sound is back, rather than carrying on a long blend whose place the listener has lost; and on a phone a page going hidden finishes the mix over one bar at once, before the lock can stop it. 26f's line stands (308 asks about its words).

**Plain playback is untouched.** Normal playback, radio, podcasts, gapless and the player's own crossfade don't use the engine; the player only gains `SoulSyncPlayer.audioGraph()`. Handing over between the player's `<audio>` and the decks (the outgoing track from the element into deck A a few bars before the mix, and the incoming one back to the element after, at its own speed) is stage C's, with the DJ mix setting.

**The lab** (`/automix-lab`; admins, in a development build or with `?lab=1`): find an outgoing and an incoming track in your library, choose a style (or Auto) and a length, a run-up (how many bars of the outgoing track play first) and whether to line the beats up from the audio, then **Show the plan** or **Play the mix**. It plays on the player's audio graph (the player pauses; the visualisers and the output device follow), with the phase, the bar, the decks' places, rates and modes live, and Cancel, Finish now, Back, Take over and Hand back while it mixes; Headphones has 23b's two choices and Test: left / Test: right. Both tracks need their grids (the DJ analysis).

**Small fixes from stage A's re-review**, each with a test:
- **L1:** a decode that failed stamped the grid's error row with the file, so when the analysis later worked its grid looked current and waited a week; it isn't stamped now, and a full analysis that works looks again for a grid an error row stands for (rows from before too; not one a crash left).
- **L2:** when a scan folds an old track row into its keeper, a finished DJ row or grid (done, or for a grid "no beat") now replaces the keeper's unfinished one (an error, still analysing); finished rows on the keeper still stay.
- **L3:** grid failures no longer end the BPM and key sweep: they're counted apart (`grid_failed`), with their own run of 25 that stops only the looking for grids (the rest wait for the next sweep). A track whose DJ data was kept counts as done; Activity adds "No beat grid found for N tracks".
- **L4:** a syncopated break was gridded on its kicks, half a beat or a sixteenth off, at up to 0.51 confidence (enough to beat-match). The grid now checks the four sixteenths on the kick and the backbeat (the snare's band differing between the beats in turn) and moves to the one that clearly leads; when none does, the confidence is cut. New fixtures: an Amen-style break (136–172 BPM, now every beat within 5 ms, median under 4) and two-step (within 4 ms); four-on-the-floor is unchanged (within 3 ms). A break's grid is held at 0.45 confidence until it's been heard on real breaks (311), so it's never beat-matched: going out, the pair crossfades; coming in, Auto picks echo out. **`GRID_VERSION` is 2: the sweep finds every grid again** (one decode a track, as stage A's top-up; plans say Waiting meanwhile, which nothing shows yet).

**New dev dependency:** `node-web-audio-api` 2.2.0 (BSD-3-Clause, a Rust implementation of the Web Audio API with prebuilt binaries), for the one test that renders the engine in an OfflineAudioContext; not in the app or the image.

**Left out, and why:** the chips, Mix now, the setting, the handover view and Decks are stage C and after; the element-to-deck handover is stage C's (above); vocals still aren't detected; a stretch better on voices (Signalsmith) only if WSOLA's artefacts are heard.

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/dj` (the window route, the review fixes, the grid accuracy with the break and two-step fixtures), `tests/mix/test_vibe.py`, `tests/activity`, `tests/database`, `tests/stars`, and `pytest -m gauntlet`; tsc, `npm run check`, `npm run build` (the worklet ships unchanged as its own asset; the lab is a lazy chunk), vitest on `src/features/engine` (75 tests, the OfflineAudioContext ones included) and the full suite before the push.

**After the gauntlet review** (an independent review of 8e818038: no High, 2 Medium, 8 Low). Each fix has a regression test; M1's, M2's and L1's are marked `gauntlet`.

- **M1, the sweep rested on every paused top-up:** once 25 grids in a row had failed, each remaining track that only needed its grid came back "grid paused" but the sweep still saved and rested at least half a second for it, so a large library at `GRID_VERSION` 2 "ran" for hours doing nothing while new tracks' BPM and key waited. Those tracks now count as done (their BPM, key and energy are), with a `grids_waiting` count while it runs, and go by with no rest and no save: the run reaches its total in moments.
- **M2, one profile could take every window decode:** the two decode slots were server-wide only. A profile now has one at most (its place is taken before a server-wide slot, so a request that can't have its own never holds one, even briefly), ffmpeg runs at niceness 10, and it seeks: a quick seek to 5 s before the window, then the rest decoded and dropped. That lands every sample exactly where a decode from the file's start does (tested to the sample on FLAC, MP3 CBR and VBR, AAC and Opus, early and late in a 5-minute file; Ogg Vorbis within a few hundred samples, see the re-review below), and a window 3½ minutes in now costs about 0.1–0.15 s instead of up to 1.5 s. The file goes to ffmpeg as `file:` and its absolute path, so no name reads as one of its protocols.
- **L1:** a HEAD to the window (or the player's waveform) runs the GET route, so the kids guard now checks HEAD the same; a hide-explicit profile's HEAD is refused and decodes nothing.
- **L2:** the window is read from ffmpeg straight into one buffer behind its header and sent in 1 MB pieces, never copied whole again; `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
- **L3:** `start` and `dur` need at least one digit (`.` alone is refused), and `dur` is at least 0.05 s.
- **L4:** the words on a break's 0.45 cap now say what happens: going out, the pair crossfades; coming in, Auto picks echo out (above, and 311).
- **L5:** the lab opens where reading `localStorage` throws (blocked site data); it splits the headphones then.
- **L6:** the limiter's automatic makeup gain (every DynamicsCompressorNode adds one) is taken back: a hard knee makes it exactly the spec's +0.57 dB (measured the same in the OfflineAudioContext), and a gain after it removes it, so below the limit the engine plays at the player's level (measured within 0.004 dB).
- **L7:** a finished run's grid failures are kept in its note (`grids:<n>`, `grids_paused:<n>`) and read from there, so a run from before this change shows none and Activity counts nothing over `track_grid` per row.
- **L8:** the 60 s deadline is kept as it is: with the seek, a decode's cost is the window's length, wherever it starts.

**After the re-review** (of d7171830: every finding above fixed; 2 new Medium, 2 Low). Each has a regression test; N1's and N2's server tests are marked `gauntlet`.

- **N1, ffmpeg's errors could stall a decode:** its stderr was a pipe nobody read while it ran, so a damaged file printing more than a pipe holds (64 KB; a damaged MP3 can print about 200 KB) stopped ffmpeg, and the window, for the whole 60 s deadline, holding a slot, the profile's place and a thread. Its complaints now go to a temporary file (the first 300 bytes are logged on failure). Tested with over 1 MB of errors: done at once, nothing left behind (no slot, place or file handle); against the old pipe the same test waits the full minute.
- **N2, the per-profile cap broke two decks at once:** the lab asked for both windows together, so the second was refused and asked again only once, a second later, while decodes take 0.6–1.7 s (more on a NAS). The server keeps one decode a profile; the browser now fetches a tab's windows one at a time (`loader.inLine`, so stage C has it too) and asks again after a 429 for as long as `Retry-After` says (every 429 carries it), backing off, for up to 10 s in all. Tested: two decks load against a server capped at one, one after the other and never refused.
- **N3, an AAC window in the first 5 s lost its priming frame:** even a quick seek to 0 makes ffmpeg drop it. There's no quick seek in the first five seconds now; tested to the sample on AAC at 0, 1 and 3 s (and FLAC and both MP3s).
- **N4, Ogg Vorbis isn't sample-exact:** ffmpeg trims Ogg packets, so a Vorbis window can sit a few hundred samples off (177 near the end of the reviewer's file), and this was so before the seek too. The words above and in CONTRACTS now say so; the engine's beat correction (±25 ms, `refine.ts`) takes it up. The test allows 400 samples.

### 2026-10-27: Dev, auto-mix stage A of 27 October

Built on `feat/automix-a` from `integration` (feba5654), from BACKLOG "Auto-mix: beat-matched transitions in Up next" and `Auto-mix (Up next, decks).dc.html` (26a–26j), approved by Ross. **Stage A only: the server's analysis the mixer needs and the pair planner.** Nothing in the browser changes yet; stage B is the two-deck engine with time-stretch, stage C auto-mix itself. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** a schema change (`track_grid`), routes scoped to the caller's library, and a background job that decodes library files: `core/dj/grid.py`, `core/dj/grid_store.py` and `core/dj/planner.py` are added to `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` (`api/dj.py` and `database/**` were already there); the access, cap and migration tests are marked `gauntlet`. Routes: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Auto-mix stage A". BACKLOG: "Auto-mix: Building (stage A)".

**The library, after checking licences** (SYNCHRO.S is MIT): **librosa (ISC) with our own code**, on numpy and scipy (BSD), all already in `requirements.txt` for Sample Studio and DJ stage 1. **No new dependency; the Docker image is unchanged.** Left out: **Essentia** (AGPL); **madmom** (its code is BSD, but its beat and downbeat models are CC BY-NC-SA, non-commercial); **beat_this** (code and weights MIT, the best open beat and downbeat tracker today, but it needs PyTorch and torchaudio, several hundred MB in the image, downloads its weights at run time, and some of its training data is under limited licences), so it wasn't compared. librosa's own beat tracker is used only to follow a drifting tempo: it works in whole frames (2% steps at 100 BPM), which is why the first analysis can be a few percent out.

**How it works** (`core/dj/grid.py`, signal processing only):
- **Beat grid:** an onset envelope weighted to the low band's attacks (the kick: a broadband one follows house music's off-beat hats half a beat off), its clear peaks, and the one constant period and phase they fit best: a comb (a narrow von Mises bump as six harmonics) searched within 4% of the expected tempo and then to a millionth of it. The expected tempo is stage 1's BPM (a tag, enrichment or the first analysis), so nothing is analysed twice; with no BPM, or one the onsets plainly disagree with (not its half or double), a scan of 70–180 BPM leaning to 124. The on-beat onsets then say how steady it is (their spread about the grid, and how far a run of them bends): steady is a **constant grid** (first beat and period); a drift keeps the **beats**, each pulled onto its onset, with the gaps (a breakdown without a kick) drawn as a curve that meets the tempo on both sides.
- **Downbeats:** 4/4 assumed; the phase whose beats stand out most from a stronger kick and attack, the harmony turning (chroma) and the timbre changing; the confidence is how far it stands out. Accents in threes say `not_4_4`.
- **Phrases and sections:** bar-long features (timbre, harmony, loudness, kick), their self-similarity matrix, and its checkerboard novelty at 8 and 16 bars find the section changes; they're kept on the line of fours they mostly sit on (a track can start on a pickup bar). Phrases run every 8 bars from each change (marked 8, 16 or 32). Parts are labelled intro, outro, breakdown, drop or main from their loudness and kick against the track's main level, best effort.
- **Mix regions:** in, the intro from its first bar of steady drums; out, the outro up to its last kick; else a 16-bar stretch with the kick near that end (`beat_only`), else the first or last 8 bars (`edge`, a low confidence). **Vocals aren't detected** (it would take a model); an intro with harmony in it still counts.
- **Loudness:** the file's ReplayGain track gain when it has one, else the analysed level.

**Accuracy** (`tests/dj/test_grid_accuracy.py`, on generated tracks with a known answer: a kick with an accented one, off-beat hats and bass, chords turning on the bar, a crash on each section; no real music is committed):
- Steady grids at 100.5, 124, 128, 140 and 174 BPM, some with a lead-in of silence: the tempo exact to 0.02 BPM and **every beat within 3 ms** of the truth (the test allows 5); the first downbeat on the right beat every time, also without the accent (the harmony finds it) and on a track that opens on beat two.
- A stage-1 BPM at half time, 2% or 2.4% out still finds the exact tempo; a wrong one 20% out is overruled.
- A tempo drifting 122 → 127 and 126 → 124 BPM: kept as beats, **every beat within 6 ms** (the test allows 10), through a 16-bar breakdown without a kick.
- Section changes on the exact bar (16, 48, 64, 96 of 112; an odd 28-bar section restarts the phrases), labelled intro, main, breakdown, drop, outro; the mix-in region the intro, the mix-out the outro.
- A waltz says `not_4_4`; a pad without a beat has no grid (`no_beat`); under 20 s is `too_short`.
- Real music, checked locally only (three Creative Commons clips from librosa's example data, not committed, no ground truth): a waltz at 150.0 BPM (confidence 0.86, the downbeat uncertain, 0.13, and it wasn't recognised as 3/4), a drum and bass loop at 136.0 (0.54) and a swung lounge track at 130.0 (0.37, near the 0.3 floor); the tempos agree with librosa's own tracker. **Honest limits:** very good on steady four-on-the-floor music, weaker on breakbeats and swing (lower confidences, which is what the fallback reads), the downbeat is a heuristic, and sections are best effort.
- Cost: about 1–5 s of analysis for a 10-minute track (the most for a drifting one, or the first after a start; at nice 10) and about 215–320 MB of memory on top of the decode (corrected after the gauntlet review; the first figure, about 200 MB, missed the decode's neighbours); the decode is shared.

**In the sweep** (`core/dj/worker.py`): a new track's grid comes from the decode its BPM, key and energy already made (`analyze_track(..., keep_audio=True)`): one decode a track. A track whose DJ data is done for this file only gets its grid (the **top-up**: one decode for the grid alone, its known BPM as the place to look), through the same sweep, so the existing priority, rests, waits (downloads, imports, a drive move, an unplugged drive) and Stop apply, and a provisional row before each decode keeps a crash from repeating. A grid that fails or finds nothing never costs the track its DJ data. Status gains `grids: {done, no_beat, pending}`.

**Stored** (`track_grid`, an add-only migration, ledger `track_grid_v1`, loud on failure; `core/dj/grid_store.py`): a steady track keeps its first beat, period, downbeat, confidences, and phrases, sections and regions in bars: **about 0.4 KB a track**. Only a drifting track keeps its beats, as millisecond gaps (about 4 bytes a beat, 2–5 KB).

**The API:** `GET /api/library/dj/grid/<id>` (the grid, downbeats, phrases, sections, mix-in and mix-out with times and confidences, or `analyzed: false` with a reason); `POST /api/library/dj/grids` (a queue's tracks, up to 50, body at most 8 KB); `GET /api/library/dj/plan?from=&to=&style=&bars=` (the planner). All scoped to the caller's library like the other DJ routes.

**The pair planner** (`core/dj/planner.py`, pure code, no audio): for a pair, a style (26a's seven, or Auto) and a length in bars, where the outgoing mix starts (a phrase late enough that the window ends with the outro; echo out and cut on the outro's first phrase; drop swap at the last breakdown), where the incoming track starts (its intro's first downbeat with steady drums; for drop swap, so its drop lands as the window ends), the tempo ratio (half and double time count; a plain speed change within 3%), and whether it can be beat-matched (not over 8%, nor with a grid under 0.5 confidence or not 4/4). **Auto:** keys that mix and within 3% → long blend (32); within 8%, or a key not known → short blend (8); a key clash → cut when the next track opens on drums, else echo out; over 8% → echo out. A style that needs a beat-match and can't have one, or any style when the outgoing track has no grid it can trust, falls back to a **crossfade with the reason**; a style the pair can't do (drop swap without a drop) stays listed as unavailable with why. Not analysed yet: `waiting`, 26g's Waiting chip.

**Design's 249** ("Can the engine know the mix's start point when the pair is queued, for the waveform's marker and 'in 2:58'?"): **yes, as soon as both tracks are analysed.** `GET /api/library/dj/plan?from=<playing>&to=<next>&style=<the chip's>` answers `out.start` in the playing track's own seconds (the marker), and "in 2:58" is `out.start` less the playback position; a changed chip asks again (it's cheap: no audio). Until both have grids the answer is `waiting`. For a track longer than the analysed 10 minutes the start is estimated from its length (the plan's `note` says so).

**Left out, and why:** everything in the browser (the chips, Mix now, the setting, the handover view, Decks driven by auto-mix) is stages B and C. Vocal detection needs a model. The grid's BPM isn't written back over stage 1's (300).

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on the new tests (`tests/dj/test_grid_accuracy.py`, `test_grid_store_and_worker.py`, `test_planner.py`, `test_grid_routes.py`, `tests/database/test_track_grid_migration.py`), `tests/dj`, `tests/sets`, `tests/mix`, the migration and upgrade tests and the web_server import test; `pytest -m gauntlet`.

**After the gauntlet review** (an independent review of b4fc649f: no High, 3 Medium, 6 Low). Each fix has a regression test (`tests/dj/test_grid_review_fixes.py`, the noise tests in `test_grid_accuracy.py`); M1–M3's are marked `gauntlet`.

- **M1, a track that can't be analysed waited for ever:** a track skipped as too long, unreadable or missing had no grid row, so its chip and plan said Waiting for good. Its grid row now records the same state with the DJ row, and a track that failed before grids existed is read from its DJ row: the grid says `too_long`, `error` or `unreachable`, and the plan crossfades with the reason ("A Mix is too long to analyse", "… couldn't be analysed", "… can't be opened"). Tested from the sweep through the routes to the plan.
- **M2, noise got a confident drifting grid:** the drifting path scored its beats after pulling each onto an onset, so they scored themselves. Hits are now counted on the beats as the tracker placed them, and a drifting grid needs its onsets to line up on a tempo half a minute at a time (noise 0.06–0.13, drifting tracks about 0.6; over a whole 10-minute ramp the one-tempo figure is 0.09, which is why it's windowed). White, pink and brown noise and random clicks get no grid, and the plan says "has no steady beat"; the drifting tests, and a new 10-minute one, keep their beats within 10 ms.
- **M3, a plan's cost grew with the stated length:** the phrases past the analysed part were listed out to the track's `duration`. The length is now believed only to 30 minutes (and to the analysed length for a track analysed whole), and the last phrase that fits is worked out by arithmetic: a 2^40 ms length plans in well under 50 ms.
- **L-a:** a failed grid counts toward the sweep's 25-failures-in-a-row stop and Activity's failure count, and is tried again after a week (one a crash left, never).
- **L-b:** when a scan folds an old track row into its new one, the DJ data and grid follow it (the new row's own stay); and a grid kept for another file than the DJ data's (a reused id) is due again.
- **L-c:** the top-up only skips BPM, key and energy while the file's size and modification time match its DJ data. A file changed since is analysed again whole, and with Settings' "Write BPM and key into files" on, that analysis may write them into the file, as for any new track (CONTRACTS says so).
- **L-e:** damaged samples (inf or NaN) are treated as silence, and a level is never inf or NaN.
- **L-f:** the memory figure above is corrected.

### 2026-10-26: Dev, the Mix tab of 26 October

Built on `feat/mix-tab` from `integration` (c85f7fc6), from `Mix tab.dc.html` (29a–29d), `DJ area map (six briefs).dc.html` (24a, keys updated), `Start here - Design notes 2026-10-26.dc.html` and BACKLOG "Star tracks and mix sets", "The expanded player's Mix tab", approved by Ross on 26 October. SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** a schema change (`dj_working_sets`), per-profile data and calls to the AI provider: `core/dj/ranking.py`, `core/dj/vibe.py` and `core/dj/store.py` are added to `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` (with `api/dj.py`, `api/sets.py`, `core/sets/**` and `database/**`, already there); the access, cap and migration tests are marked `gauntlet`. Routes: `CONTRACTS.md`, "The Mix tab" (and `after` in "Sets"); patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, "The Mix tab"; rows: `SCREENS.md`. BACKLOG: "Mix tab: Building".

**What was built, board by board**

- **29a, the tray in Mix:** a mode of the tray, not a fourth tab: the **Now playing · Mix** switch at the top of the record (M), remembered per browser; the tray is 500px. **This track:** 72px art, title, artist and the time, the key chip with its name and BPM, the star, "Energy 6 of 10" over the ten-step meter (EnergyMeter's new `steps` variant), and "Not analysed yet · Analyse it now" (the existing `POST /api/library/dj/start {track_id}`, admins). **Mixes well with:** the top five in the tab's order with their reasons ("Same key · +2% BPM · energy ↑1"), Play next, Add to set ("✓ #7"), More like this (the list follows that track, "Back to Night Ferry" at its head, the playing track left out); Suggest from Now playing / End of set; the Vibe switch, off; "From your library, files present · starred first among equals". **The working set:** the picker (your sets, New mix set with the playing track, No working set), the strip of the last three plus now (dashed), the energy line (the set solid, the step to now dashed), the join's sentence in the step's words, and the primary "Add Night Ferry to Sunday Set" → "✓ Added · #7" for 4 s; the keys line.
- **29b, Visuals:** a "Mix" button in the header and the 520px drawer: the key, BPM and energy, the star, "Playing Sunday Set · track 4 of 12" with the next transition's pill and "next: Static Bloom", Mixes well with "leaving out the set's own tracks", rows with Play next, + and More like this, and the foot on where Add puts a track. **While a set plays, Add inserts after the playing track** (`POST /api/sets/<id>/tracks` with `after`, the same revision and playlist rules as at the end); Play next leaves the set for one track and the queue returns to it. One panel at a time on the right; Escape closes the drawer first.
- **29c, the phone:** the switch at the top of the Now playing sheet opens Mix in the record's place: "‹ Now playing", Mix · Set. Mix: the key, BPM, the meter, the star, Mixes well with with Vibe, rows with the short reason, Play next and +, More like this on a long-press. Set: the picker, the energy line, the set's rows (number, title, key · BPM · step, the shared "…" in 24a's order) and "Add Night Ferry · as #7".
- **29d, the states:** no working set (the card in the strip's place; New mix set, and A or Add make "Set from Monday 26 Oct" with the track); nothing fits (Widen the search: 10% and any key); not analysed (info, "(12 tracks ahead)", Analyse this one now); not from your library (a preview: "Mix works on your own tracks"); radio with an identified track that's in the library (the switch in the station's record; the tab works for that track); podcasts and audiobooks (no tab; M mutes, N skips as before).
- **Keys (24a):** **A** adds the playing track to the working set, **N** plays the top match next, **M** switches the tray to Mix (in Visuals, the drawer; collapsed, it expands into Mix; on a phone, it opens the sheet in Mix). They go through `static/media-player.js`'s one handler, so S's guard holds: nothing in text fields, dialogs, popovers or menus. **X** is reserved for Mix now and not bound. The shortcuts list says them.
- **Add to mix set…** lists the working set first with a "Working set" tag (28a's Recent, 281).

**The server** (all code; no AI but Vibe):
- `GET /api/library/dj/mixes/<id>` gains `rank=mix`, `set_id`, `wide`, `exclude` and `limit` (5 then); without them it answers as before, for the Mixes panel, Swap and Export to Traktor. The order (`core/dj/ranking.py`): the store's key-then-tempo order, then a starred boost, recently played (the profile's own listening pile: two days well down, a week a little), style closeness (shared genres of the two tracks' artists and albums, the same artist), and with a set its tracks left out and its energy direction followed (the last four known energies). Each match carries `reason`, `reason_short` and `signals`.
- **The working set** is one row per profile in the new add-only table `dj_working_sets` (ledger `dj_working_sets_v1`), only ever one of the profile's own sets; `GET`/`PUT /api/sets/working`, `POST /api/sets/working/start`.
- **Vibe: wired through the AI provider.** `POST /api/library/dj/mixes/<id>/vibe` ranks the top 20 in code, then asks the provider (`core/ai`: the Assistant's key, model and switches) to re-order them through one tool; only track details go (title, artist, album, genres, BPM, key, energy), numbered; refused with its reason when the AI is off for the profile (`not_configured`, `members_not_allowed`, `key_refused`) or at its limit (`cap_reached`: the server's one monthly budget, not a per-profile one); two at once; each request is counted for the server and the profile. The switch is disabled with that reason; thinking dims the list and says so; an error keeps code's order and says so.

**Left out, and why**

- **Preview** (headphone preview): it needs DJ stage 3's second output (`setSinkId`); each row keeps a hidden slot for it.
- **X (Mix now)**: auto-mix isn't built; the key is reserved, not bound.
- **Play as a mix**: still waits for auto-mix, so "while a set plays" is worked out from the queue (294).
- **The warning when an edit made a jump** (the brief's "while a set is playing"): the next transition's pill turns amber and says "a jump: mix on a break"; no separate warning for edits.
- **Lyrics** in the Visuals header (29b draws it): Lyrics isn't built.

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/mix` (new: the order, Vibe, the working set and Add after), `tests/database/test_dj_working_sets_migration.py` (new), `tests/dj`, `tests/sets`, `tests/stars`, `tests/playlists`, the migration and upgrade tests and the web_server import test: 745 passed; `pytest -m gauntlet` 158 passed. `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check` (0 errors), `npm run build`; vitest on `features/mix` (new), `features/dj`, `features/sets`, `features/stars`, `features/visuals`, the player, the radio tray, the visuals keys, the new Mix keys test and `routes/library`.

**After the gauntlet review** (an independent review of 61879e7f: no High, 1 Medium, 7 Low). Each fix has a regression test; M1's and L1's are marked `gauntlet`.

- **M1, Vibe could be set off from another site:** the route took a body that wasn't JSON (a form or text/plain POST, which needs no preflight) as `{}`, so another site could spend AI usage, with no cap by default. Vibe now takes a JSON object only (400 "Send it as JSON" otherwise, as the Assistant and Sets do), and never reaches the provider. The other new writes (`PUT /api/sets/working`, `POST /api/sets/working/start`, the add with `after`) already went through Sets' `_body`, which refuses anything but a JSON object; tested the same way.
- **L1, a Vibe slot could leak:** the provider factory's import and the model lookups ran after the slot was taken but outside the `try`; a failure there kept the slot for good (two failures, no Vibe until a restart). Everything after the slot is taken is inside the `try` now. The Assistant (`core/ai/assistant.py`) had the same shape and is fixed the same way.
- **L2, `?set_id=²` was a 500:** `str.isdigit()` takes Unicode digits that `int()` doesn't. Query numbers (`set_id`, `set_at`, `limit`) are ASCII digits only now (`[0-9]{1,15}`, positive), and the body's are whole numbers that aren't bools.
- **L3, the AI's note can be steered by a title:** it's kept, but shown quoted as the AI's note ("Ordered for feel by the AI. The AI's note: “…”"), with control and format characters (bidi, zero-width) dropped, spaces collapsed, at most 160 characters, and dropped altogether when it holds a link (`://`, `www.`) or an email address.
- **L4, an `order` of `inf` or a huge number was a 502:** numbers that aren't finite whole numbers in range are ignored (`OverflowError` and `ValueError` caught).
- **L5, Add after the playing track shifted locked rows and left stale chips:** two rules, one place each, server side (`core/sets/store.py`): **locked rows keep their places** (`insert_keeping_locked`, the set page's `moveRow` rule: rows up to the insert stay, each locked row after it keeps its index and number, the unlocked ones, the new first, fill the free places), and **a row whose next track changes** from one track to another has its transition reset to Auto, `{style: "auto", bars: 16}` (`reset_changed_joins`), since it described another join; a row with no chosen transition, or no next track (the last), is left.
- **L6, docs and code:** the AI cap is **one figure for the whole server**, not per profile; CONTRACTS, this entry and Vibe's "at its limit" words say so ("This server's AI budget for the month is spent (one budget for every profile), so Vibe waits for the 1st."). While a set plays, the energy direction is read from the last four tracks up to where Add puts a track (`set_at`), not the set's end. With Widen, the candidates are read with the four keys that mix first, then the closest tempo, so the 2,000-row read cap can't drop them in favour of other keys.
- **L7, the keys:** M acts only for a track the tab works for (a library track, or a station's that's yours), never a preview: M then mutes and N skips, as before. The bindings are one table in `static/media-player.js` (`NP_LETTER_KEYS`), each mapping tested.
- **287, answered by Ross (26 October): mute and skip move to ⇧M and ⇧N.** With a Mix tab, M switches to Mix and N plays the top match next; ⇧M mutes and ⇧N skips to the next track. With no Mix tab, M and N still mute and skip, and ⇧M and ⇧N do the same, so the shifted keys always mute and skip. The table now reads M → Mix, then mute; ⇧M → mute; N → Mix, then next; ⇧N → next; A → Mix. The shortcuts list (`?`) says "Mute: Shift M" and "Next or previous track: Shift N, P".

**Checks after the review:** ruff; pytest on `tests/mix`, `tests/ai`, `tests/dj`, `tests/sets`, `tests/stars`, `tests/playlists`, the migration and upgrade tests and the web_server import test: 877 passed; `pytest -m gauntlet` 169 passed. `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check` (0 errors), `npm run build`; vitest on `features/mix`, `features/dj`, `features/sets`, `features/stars`, the media-player keys and the design tokens.

### 2026-10-26: Dev, stars and mix sets of 26 October

Built on `feat/stars-mix-sets` from `integration` (0c95c687), from `Stars and mix sets.dc.html` (28a–28g) and `DJ area map (six briefs).dc.html` (24a: where things live, one "…" order, the keys), approved by Ross on 26 October, and BACKLOG "Star tracks and mix sets" (the Mix tab in that entry is the next build). SYNCHRO.S is the name shown; code, routes and storage keys keep SoulSync's. **Gauntlet:** a schema change (`track_stars`) and per-profile data: `api/stars.py` and `core/stars/**` are on `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt` (with `api/sets.py`, `core/sets/**`, `api/dj.py`, `core/dj/traktor.py`, `database/**` and `web_server.py`, already there); the access and migration tests are marked `gauntlet`. Routes: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Stars", "Sets" (`/tracks`, `/holding`, a row's `note` and `transition`, `gone_track`) and "DJ data" (`starred` on browse, `RANKING` in the `.nml`); patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` (The star, One "…", "Add to mix set…", A mix set's page beyond 22c); rows: `SCREENS.md`. BACKLOG: "Stars and mix sets: Building".

**What was built, board by board**

- **28a, the star on Tracks:** a reserved 32px column after Energy; the outline star on the row's hover and keyboard focus, the filled star (accent-light) once starred; nothing for a track without a library file. **One control for every surface** (`src/features/stars/`): React rows use `<StarButton>`, the shell and legacy pages get the same button from `createStarButton` (`window.SoulSyncStarButton`), one stylesheet (`static/stars.css`) and one store per page (`window.SoulSyncStars`), so starring in the bar shows on the row at once. A page's rows are looked up in one request (`POST /api/stars/status`), never one per row; Tracks' browse already says each row's star. It's on Tracks, Listening history, the Artist page's tracks (the album view too), Search's library tracks, Mixes well with, a playlist's rows the library has (matched by title and artist), a mix set's rows, and the player. **Starred** is a filter chip with its count on Tracks (`?starred=1`, 24a), not a page; a starred track whose file has gone stays listed, at 62% with "File missing", and can be unstarred.
- **28a, 24a, the "…":** one order for every DJ-area track (`features/dj/track-menu.ts`): Play next, Mixes well with…, Add to mix set…, Add to playlist…, Open artist, Open album (Versions… waits for Versions). Tracks, Listening history, the Artist page and a mix set's rows use it; the player's "…" gains Add to mix set… for a library track. 28a's picker, below.
- **28a, 28b, 28d, 28g, "Add to mix set…":** a 360px popover at the "…" (a sheet on a phone): "Add Copper Sky to…", the search focused, Recent (five sets, last edited first) with "adds as #25 · edited today" or "In it already · #4" and a quiet Add again, New mix set… named in place; then the toast "Added Harbour Light to Sunday Set · #25" with **Open it**. Many (28b): Tracks' selection bar's primary is now Add to mix set… with Export to Traktor… beside it and **Star all** first in its "…"; a set that holds some opens "Add 4" / "Add all 6" in place; tracks whose file is missing are left out and said. The server adds at the end (`POST /api/sets/<id>/tracks`), only library tracks with their file, and moves the set's revision on in the same write, so an open page's save from an older base is refused rather than undoing it (see After the gauntlet review, H1); the playlist follows by Sets 1's rules. A set page open in the same window reads itself again after an add when it has nothing unsaved.
- **28c, the player:** the star after the title in the bar (outline at 62% at rest), in the phone's mini bar, and first in the record's control row (also in the phone's sheet). Not for a preview, a station, an episode or a book.
- **28d, the phone:** only a starred row shows its 44px star; the row's sheet starts with Star (Unstar). Tracks' phone row is art, the track, the star, "…" as 28d draws it (Play next in the sheet; question 278).
- **28e, 28f, a mix set's page:** a note per track as a third line, typed in place ("Add a note" on hover and focus only); the transition chip at the end of each known step's line (26i's pill, the step's dot, "Long blend · 32 bars", then "auto" or "yours"; first on the line on a phone) opening 26a's picker (Auto, Long blend, Short blend, Bass swap, Cut, Echo out, Filter sweep, Drop swap; Length 2 · 4 · 8 · 16 · 32; a cut has none). It's saved with the set as `{style, bars}`, Auto by default; auto-mix isn't built, so the chip only says what's saved. **File missing** keeps the row's place, number, star and values at 62% with the warning tag, "Skipped when played" under it (the step goes around it, as Sets 1 already worked it out), and its "…" leads with Find it again and Add to Wanted; the meta line ends "1 file missing". **Being worked out** (info) with "—" values; its steps read Not known yet and carry no chip. "Save as mix" reads **"Save as mix set"** everywhere (the draft's primary, Famous mix's, the words around them).
- **28g:** the empty set is "Nothing in Warm-up yet" with the line about Add to mix set… and Suggest a set… (disabled until Sets 2), Start from a famous mix…, Add the playing track. "A, with the track already in the working set" waits for the working set (the Mix tab).
- **Keys (24a, updated):** **S** stars the playing track everywhere (the focused row's track first, 28a's note); Visuals' favourite moved from S to **⇧S** (`media-player-v2.js`, `visuals.logic.ts`); the shortcuts list says both.
- **Export to Traktor:** a track the caller starred goes out with `INFO RANKING="255"` (Traktor's five stars); the preview counts them (`starred`).

**The schema** (`MusicDatabase._add_track_stars_table`, ledger `track_stars_v1`): `track_stars (profile_id, track_id, starred_at)`, key `(profile_id, track_id)`, an index on `(profile_id, starred_at)`. Only adds (`atomic=False`): a step that can't finish is an ERROR naming it in `schema_health` and runs again on the next start (tested: fresh install, the oldest release's upgrade, a short table, a step that can't finish, a deleted profile's stars). `dj_set_tracks.transition` now holds `{style, bars}` as compact JSON; no schema change there.

**Stars are per profile and library-scoped** (`core/stars/store.py`): every read and write filters on the caller's profile, admins included; only a track in the caller's library scope (the DJ store's `_scope`) with its file can be starred, so a member can't star, or learn of, another profile's own track; bodies are capped (`_read_json_capped`, 192 KB). Semgrep's `sqlalchemy-execute-raw-query` flags four new queries (`core/stars/store.py` `_starred` and `unstar`, `core/sets/store.py` `_without_file` and `append_tracks`) because they're f-strings: only `?` placeholders and the library scope's fixed fragment are formatted in, every value is bound, so each carries `# nosemgrep: sqlalchemy-execute-raw-query` with that reason above it (`docs/GAUNTLET.md`'s rule for false alarms).

**Design's questions to Dev 253–258** are all about the Data visualiser (the analyser's samples, the AudioContext's rate, the frame sampling, full-scale counts, the hidden tab, the texture's cost): nothing in this build settles them; they're answered with Data.

**Design's answers this settles:** 261 (an empty set) is 28g, built; 269 (the Away tag) is 28e's File missing, built.

**Left out, and why**

- **Play as a mix** (28e's primary, 28f's) and the chips' playing: auto-mix isn't built (26a–26j).
- **The working set** (Recent's first place, "Working set" tags, key **A**, 28g's "A, with the track already in the working set"): it comes with the Mix tab (BACKLOG, "A per-profile working-set setting").
- **Versions…** in the "…": Versions isn't built (25a–25g).
- **Search's "…"**: Search's rows have none (question 276); the star is there.
- **Album pages** are the Artist page's album view (`?album=`), which has the star and the shared "…"; the admin's "Edit tags and files" table doesn't.

**After the gauntlet review** (an independent review of b91995ec: 1 High, 1 Medium, 4 Low; access, limits, the migration, text cleaning and the four `nosemgrep` marks checked out). Each fix has a regression test; H1's and M1's are marked `gauntlet` (pytest) and named `[gauntlet H1]` (vitest).

- **H1, an add elsewhere undone by an open page's autosave:** the page counted revisions up and took a refusal naming one it had sent for its own, so after another tab's add (R → R+1) its save at R+1 was refused silently and the next, at R+2, wrote its rows over the add. Now **compare and set**: `PUT /api/sets/<id>` carries `base_revision` (what the page last read or had confirmed); the server writes only while the stored revision equals it, in one conditional update, and answers the new one, which the page adopts. One save in flight per window, the newest edit sent after it. A refusal means someone else changed the set: autosave stops, the edits stay on the page, and a warning Notice says "This set changed somewhere else" and what isn't saved, with **Reload it** and **Keep mine as a draft** (this page's version as the browser's draft); nothing is retried over the top. The set page refetches on window focus and when the picker reads or adds, and takes a newer set in place only when nothing there is unsaved (the editor is no longer remounted by revision, so it can't drop edits). Bounds: 0..2^53 − 1. The older `revision` field is still read when `base_revision` is absent.
- **M1, stars lost when a scan re-keys a track:** `_absorb_track_row` now moves every profile's star to the keeper (`INSERT OR IGNORE … SELECT`, then deletes the old rows; a star already on the keeper stays) and repoints a mix set's rows too. (The orphan-star sweep first added here was taken out again in the re-review, H-A below.)
- **L1:** `star()` reads what's starred once per request, not once per track.
- **L2:** `POST /api/stars/status` looks `track_ids` and `songs` up apart, so 500 of each never cut each other off.
- **L3:** the playlist sync after an add or a save reads the set's rows again under a lock (`store.follow_latest`), so of two changes at once the last sync writes the newest rows (the test forces the old order and fails without the lock).
- **L4:** S on a track not looked up yet waits once per id and gives up after 10 s, so quick presses or a failed lookup don't stack listeners; the store sends one star change per track at a time.
- **Re-review of 59152c82** (H1, M1 and L1–L4 confirmed fixed; four more, each with a test, H-A's and M-A's marked `gauntlet`):
  - **H-A, the orphan-star sweep deleted stars on tracks only away for a while** (a full refresh's `clear_server_data()` stopped part way, an unplugged drive): the next scan swept them, and when the same ids came back the stars were gone. **The sweep is removed**; a star on an id that's away is kept and reattaches. The per-profile cap counts only stars on tracks the library has now (a join on `tracks`), so dead ids can't fill it. The re-key move stays. The test deletes the rows, runs the orphan cleanup, the removed-content and superseded-row passes, writes the rows back, and finds both profiles' stars.
  - **M-A, saves without a base could still overwrite blindly:** `PUT /api/sets/<id>` now **requires `base_revision`**, renames included (the page already sends it for every save). A body with only the old `revision`, or with neither, gets 409 `{stale, revision, reason: "reload"}` and no write. A page from before this change shows its "changed in another window" error (or, when the stored revision is one it sent, nothing), once per edit, with no retry loop, and never overwrites. The old revision checks (`_check_revision`, the 10,000 step) are gone with the path that used them.
  - **L-A:** the absorb that repoints a set's rows counts those sets' revision on in the same transaction, so an open page holding the old id gets a conflict instead of saving it back as Missing.
  - **L-B:** the Notice's list of what isn't saved names the first five, then "…and N more".
- **The `nosemgrep` id:** CI's rule id is the rule's path under the checkout, so the full `python.sqlalchemy.security…` form wouldn't match it; Semgrep matches the short id `sqlalchemy-execute-raw-query` as a suffix. Confirmed: the first push's Semgrep job failed on these four lines, the second (same lines with the mark) passed with no new findings, and a local run with the pinned rules from a differently named folder reports none with the short id and all four with the long one.

**Checks:** ruff; pytest on `tests/stars`, `tests/sets`, `tests/dj`, `tests/playlists`, the migration and upgrade tests (`tests/database/test_track_stars_migration.py`, `test_dj_sets_migration.py`, `test_track_dj_migration.py`, `test_upgrade_from_old_releases.py`, `test_schema_migration_steps.py`, `test_schema_foreign_keys.py`), the web_server import test, and `pytest -m gauntlet`; `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`; vitest on stars, sets, dj, the library pages, Search, the Artist page, playlists, the player and the visuals keys.

### 2026-10-24: Dev, Sets 1 of 24 October
