### 2026-10-24: Dev, the gauntlet for risky changes

Built on `ci/gauntlet` from `integration`. A new CI lane, the gauntlet (`.github/workflows/gauntlet.yml`), that only risky changes go through, on top of the normal checks. A change is risky when it touches a file listed in `.github/gauntlet-paths.txt`: code that deletes or moves files, the database schema and migrations, setup editing, auth, sessions, profiles and admin gating, outbound HTTP, or CI itself. The gauntlet runs Bandit and Semgrep against committed baselines (only new findings on changed lines fail), the upgrade and migration tests on their own, and the disaster-scenario tests tagged `@pytest.mark.gauntlet` (the drive move's same-folder, crash-during-switch, edited-during-copy and unplugged cases, the kids guard's ordering, the migration failures). A PR labelled `gauntlet`, or a run started by hand, goes through it whatever it touches. No app code changed.

`docs/GAUNTLET.md` says what it runs, how to read its results and how to regenerate the baselines, and lists what the lead commits to for gauntlet work: an independent review for data loss and security holes, findings fixed or answered in the HANDOFF entry before merge, a green gauntlet run before the change goes into Ross's private build, and zip notes saying the change went through the gauntlet.

### 2026-10-23: Dev, DJ stage 1 (BPM, key, energy, tags, Mixes well with)

Built on `feat/dj-stage1` from `integration` (5ea8dc4b), from Ross's plan for DJ features (BACKLOG, "DJ area: BPM, key and mixing (Traktor Pro 3)", new, Exploring; stage 1 Building). No boards: everything visible follows existing patterns and is listed for Design (150–154). Routes added: `/api/library/dj/status`, `start`, `stop`, `tracks`, `browse` and `mixes/<track_id>` (`CONTRACTS.md`, "DJ data"). Patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("DJ data on a track row", "Library → Tracks", "Mixes well with…").

**What was built**

- **One analyzer.** Sample Studio's `analyze_track` now also returns energy (`core/sample/energy.py`), `ANALYZER_VERSION` 4. The library job reuses a current `sample_analysis` row instead of decoding again and saves its own result there, so Sample Studio opens analysed tracks straight away.
- **Energy, 1–10:** the RMS level of the loudest half of the track (−30 dBFS counts 0, −8 dBFS 1) weighted 0.6, plus onset density from the BPM's own onset envelope (0 to 8 a second) weighted 0.4; `1 + round(9 × score)`. Relative, for sorting and picking, like Mixed In Key's.
- **The library job** (`core/dj/worker.py`): one track at a time; the thread nices itself to 10 (Linux, which Docker on a Mac is; ffmpeg decodes inherit it) and rests at least as long as each track took; it waits while a download batch (runtime state) or an auto-import (`auto_import_history` processing) runs and while the library's drive is away (`core.drives.markers.library_away`, the drive watch's last look); a single missing file is marked `unreachable` and retried a day later. Resumable per track and versioned by the analyzer. First sweep two minutes after boot, then every 30 minutes for new tracks; Stop holds the automatic sweeps until a restart or a Start.
- **Activity:** a Library job (`library_jobs`, `kind: dj_analysis`) with progress, what it's waiting for, and Stop, under Activity's Library jobs filter (`kind: library`) beside Look for releases and the drive move. `integration` (fe1efaba, with the drive move and the radio tray) was merged into this branch: the feed dispatches `dj_analysis` beside `drive_move`, both deferred starts (drive watch, DJ analysis) are kept, and Library → Tracks keeps its list while the drive is away, with the drive's Notice above it, like Listening history (Albums and Artists show the away page instead).
- **Storage:** new table `track_dj` (Camelot `musical_key` plus `key_name`, `key_confidence`, `energy`, `dj_analyzed_at`, `dj_analyzer_version`, a source per value, the file signature, status, tags written) and `sample_analysis.energy`, through `_add_track_dj_table` (atomic=False, fails loudly into `schema_health`, ledger `track_dj_v1`). The upgrade test caught the 3.5.0 path: `_migrate_sample_analysis_text_ids` rebuilt `sample_analysis` with a fixed column list after the column was added, dropping it; the rebuild now includes `energy`.
- **Which value wins:** the file's tag, then enrichment's `tracks.bpm` (Beatport, Deezer, Tidal, HiFi, the BPM Backfill job), then analysis; key: tag (any spelling, kept canonical), then analysis. What analysis heard is kept beside (`analyzed_bpm`, `analyzed_key`). Analysis BPMs over 200 are halved and under 60 doubled.
- **Tags** (`core/dj/tags.py`, setting off by default): ID3 `TBPM`/`TKEY`, Vorbis `BPM`/`INITIALKEY`, MP4 `tmpo` plus `----:com.apple.iTunes:initialkey`, the frames SYNCHRO.S's enrichment already writes. Only gaps are filled unless "Also replace" is on; a key under 0.5 confidence is never written; saves go through `save_audio_file` (temp copy, audio verified, atomic replace). After a write the stored signatures follow the file's new size and mtime (the file's mtime does change, so Traktor and media servers see the new tags) and `notify_library_import` asks for the debounced slskd rescan.
- **Library:** the Tracks page; BPM and key on Listening history's, the Artist page's Tracks tab (the album page's tracks) and Search's library track rows; "Mixes well with…" in Tracks', History's and the Tracks tab's "…" menus. Settings → Library: a "BPM, key and energy" card with the three switches.

**Traktor Pro 3: confirmed vs assumed.** Confirmed from the code here and the formats' specs: the frames above are the ones SYNCHRO.S and Mixxx write, and ID3 `TBPM` is defined as a whole number. **Assumed, not tested against Traktor** (no Traktor in this environment): that Traktor Pro 3 reads `TKEY` and `INITIALKEY` in the short musical form ("Am", "F#m") as its initial key, and `tmpo` plus the iTunes freeform key from M4A. Traktor displays and writes its own Open Key or musical notation, so SYNCHRO.S never writes Camelot ("8A") into a file; it reads Camelot and Open Key from files other apps wrote. Worth a check on Ross's Mac: tag a few tracks, Import Music Folders in Traktor, and see whether the Key and BPM columns fill without Analyze.

**Accuracy on the fixtures** (generated tracks: a kick on every beat, off-beat hats and a i–iv–V–i chord loop at 22.05 kHz; 12 s in the tests, the figures here from 16 s renders): BPM within 3% on 90, 120, 128, 140 and 174 (measured 89.1, 117.5, 129.2, 143.6, 172.3; 100 gave 99.4), keys 5 of 5 on the Camelot wheel (A minor, C major, F♯ minor, E♭ major, D minor; confidence 0.84–0.91); energy 1 for a quiet hum, a soft pad below the loud kick track (6–7). Real music will do worse on key (Krumhansl–Kessler is the classic, simple method); the confidence and "key uncertain" are there for that.

**Decided by Dev**

1. A table of its own (`track_dj`) rather than columns on `tracks`, keyed like `sample_analysis`; `tracks.bpm` stays enrichment's.
2. Writing tags is off by default (it changes every file's mtime and the media servers rescan); a question for Ross.
3. Tracks is its own sidebar row; "And keys that mix" only shows once a key is picked; Energy is a number column.
4. A replaced file (an upgrade) isn't re-checked by the sweep (that would stat every file every 30 minutes); its row stands until the analyzer version moves or the row is cleared. Sample Studio still re-checks the signature when a track is opened there.
5. The existing BPM Backfill maintenance job is unchanged (it fills `tracks.bpm` through findings); the DJ job reads its result as enrichment.
6. **Size caps and a bounded decode.** Tracks over 30 minutes or files over 400 MB are skipped and shown as "Too long to analyse" (a DJ mix or a whole-album rip; checked from the file's header and size, nothing decoded). Of the rest, only the first 10 minutes are decoded, straight to mono at 22050 Hz (ffmpeg `-t 600 -ar 22050 -ac 1`; without ffmpeg, `soundfile` reads that many frames), so one track costs about 53 MB at most. A partial result isn't saved as Sample Studio's analysis. A provisional `analyzing` row is written before each decode; a track still marked so at the next start becomes an error ("SYNCHRO.S stopped while analysing this track …; skipped") rather than being tried again on every boot.
7. **Sample Studio's own analysis** shares the decoder: it now decodes straight to mono at 22050 Hz too (ffmpeg resampling while it reads; the librosa resample remains for installs without ffmpeg), with no time limit, so a long file opened in Sample Studio is still analysed whole but no longer held at its own rate (a 2-hour 24/96 FLAC needs about 0.6 GB instead of about 8 GB).
8. **Drive moves and other writers.** The sweep waits while a drive move copies or switches (`core.drives.holds`), while the move has paused it (its pause hook), and while the library's Write Tags batch runs. The move's "waiting for" list names "BPM/key analysis" while a track is mid-analysis or mid-write. Right before a write it checks again; a track that has to wait goes back in the queue unwritten.
9. **Tag writes, safely.** The write holds the per-file lock the other tag writers use; uses the atomic save with no in-place fallback (a full disk leaves the file as it was and reports the error); writes only when the file's signature still matches the one analysed (otherwise the track goes back in the queue and nothing is stamped as current); and only to the library's own file (the stored path as it is, or a path inside the configured library folders, never a copy in downloads or staging). Only a signature produced by this write moves Sample Studio's analysis and stems on.
10. **Which tags count as present.** Any BPM or key frame or field counts, even one that doesn't read as a value ("128 BPM", "0"), so with "Also replace" off it's never overwritten; a BPM is only written between 20 and 400. With "Also replace" on, a value already equal isn't rewritten.
11. **Tag layout kept on the DJ path:** an MP3's ID3v1 tag stays and ID3v2.3 stays 2.3; a FLAC keeps an ID3 block (`save_audio_file(preserve_layout=True)`). Every other caller keeps today's normalising save (v1 dropped, v2.4, FLAC's ID3 removed).
12. **Profile scope.** "Mixes well with…" and the track rows' lookup read only the caller's library, like Tracks; if the scope can't be read, they show nothing rather than everyone's tracks.

**Questions for Design:** 150–155 in the list above (SCREENS.md gains the Tracks row); the larger ones (stage 3's browser DJ area, where Traktor export lives) are in the BACKLOG entry.

**Tests:** Python: `tests/dj/` (new: `test_camelot.py` the wheel both ways and every spelling, mixing and tempo windows; `test_analysis_fixtures.py` BPM, key and energy on generated tracks; `test_tags.py` every format, gaps only, overwrite, refused writes, audio untouched; `test_store_and_worker.py` which value wins, sharing with Sample Studio, tags and signatures, unreachable and broken files, resumable versioned sweeps, waiting, Stop and its hold, the scheduler; `test_dj_routes_and_activity.py` the routes, admins only, filters, sorts, Mixes well with, Activity's rows), 125 tests; `tests/database/test_track_dj_migration.py` (fresh, an old release, a short table, a failing step loud in `schema_health` and retried); `test_web_server_import_side_effects.py` lists the new deferred start. Run with the sample tests, the migration and upgrade tests and every test that reads an edited file (710 files): green (one upgrade failure fixed above; a batch of errors came from the scratch folder being cleared mid-run and passed on a rerun). `ruff check` clean. Web: `npm run check`, `tsc`, `npm run build` clean; new `dj.format.test.ts`, `tracks-page.test.tsx`, a History DJ test; `nav-shell` and `nav-routes` tests know the Tracks row; `-library.grid.test.ts` knows `bpm` and `key`; the full Vitest suite, 644 files and 11,502 tests: 11,498 passed on the first run; of the 4 that didn't, the grid test's expectation was missing the new fields (fixed), and podcasts' Notes, Radio's nts.live line and Wanted's Follow… source were timing misses under the loaded full run (this machine was also running the Python set); all 4 files pass on a rerun (76 of 76). CI: sanity-check green; webui failed once on `artist-detail/-route.test.tsx`'s Remove through the Safe action, the known timing miss (it passes on its own, 45 of 45, and webui was green on earlier pushes of this branch); webui-visual differs because Library gained a sidebar row (non-blocking; the baselines need refreshing with the Tracks row when this merges).

**Screenshots** (session scratchpad, not in the repo), 1440×900 and 390×844 with the built shell in Chromium: Tracks (analysis running), Tracks filtered (120–128 BPM, 8A and the keys that mix), Mixes well with… The console shows only what the offline harness blocks (CDN scripts, socket.io) and the legacy bubble snapshots, as on Albums.

Built on `ux/radio-tray` from `integration` (5ea8dc4b), at Ross's request: the radio player now matches the music player. **This reverses 4a's "radio has no tray on a desktop: its page is the station's"** (and `player.ts`'s special case that forced `data-player="bar"` while radio played): the bar expands into 6a's 360px tray while a station plays, with the same toggle, state, Collapse and Escape as music, filled with radio's content, and the Visuals overlay is in reach from it. No board exists, so it follows 6a and the radio boards; Design is asked to draw it as a check (question 140). In the same branch, Ross's add-on from his screenshot of the collapsed radio bar at about 2000px (a big empty middle, a small meter and the track squeezed at the right): **the bar's centre is fluid**, and **a station's logo never shows the broken-image glyph**. No routes changed. The patterns are in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("Radio in the player", "The tray in radio mode", "The "…" menu while a station plays", the Visuals overlay's radio header).

**What's built**

- **The tray in radio mode.** Record: the station's 112px logo (letters under our logo route), Now playing with its source phrase, the title at 18/600, the artist as a link to Search, the release and age; the readout (Stream "AAC 256 kbps · relayed" / "direct", Path "NTS 1 → This device"); Add to Wanted or the tag, and "…". Deck: the 72px meter (no seek), Live and the time listened, Stop and the stream picker (only with several streams), Scan now (132px, its states, "Scan again") with the sheet's card under it while it runs, volume and output. List: Heard today (N) / Recent stations, Add per Heard row, Play per station, the auto-add row with 9c's confirm, and "All of it on NTS 1's page". Code: `index.html` (`[data-player-radio-tray]`, after the music columns), `src/shell/player-radio.ts` (`drawTray`, `drawHeard`, `drawRecent`, `drawTrayAuto`), `src/shell/player.ts`, `static/player.css`; words in `radio.format.ts` (`streamReadout`, `heardToday`, `heardRowView`, `stationPossessive`), `radio.scan.ts` (`radioVisualsHeader`, `ownLogo`) and the new `radio.recent.ts`.
- **Visuals while radio plays.** "…" → Visuals (F) works from the radio record (the "…" was unreachable on a desktop before: the record was hidden and the tray never opened; F already worked). The menu says "More for this station" and drops what a live stream can't do. The overlay's header names the identified track over "artist · station", or the station over "Live · Listening…", with the track's art or the station's logo. Checked in Chromium on a relayed stream: SYNCHRO.S, MilkDrop and Classic all draw from it (the overlay's tap and Classic read the player's analyser on the one audio element, which the relay feeds same-origin); the direct-mode card is unchanged. The overlay's transport now hides what the player hides (Previous and Next for radio; a `[hidden]` control there used to stay visible).
- **Phone.** The sheet (11d, 11e) is unchanged but gains a **Visuals** link row after Station page; it closes the sheet and opens the overlay. The radio tray never shows on a phone.
- **The bar.** Expand now shows for radio, after Station and Stop (moved in the markup so the focus order matches); the left button expands the tray (its name: "NTS 1, live. Expand the player") instead of going to the station page; Station still does. Live, the station, the one action and Scan now are as before.
- **Ross's add-on: the fluid bar.** The meter and the track block share the room between the station and the controls and follow the window: the meter `flex: 1` from 120px to 960px (under 1180px it may give way to 48px first, then the track ellipsises, as phase 1 decided), the track block up to `clamp(240px, 30vw, 560px)`. The meter's bars are counted from its width by a ResizeObserver (a bar every 6px in the bar, 8px in the deck, whole-pixel widths so they draw crisply, 12–160) and read through the meter's own 128-bin tap off the player's analyser (the player's own has 32 bins), spread logarithmically up to about 10 kHz so a wide meter moves across its width. Measured: 120px/20 bars at 1440, 406px/67 at 1920, 960px/160 at 2560; the deck 688px/86 at 1440.
- **Ross's add-on: the logo bug.** The bar's art showed the broken-image glyph because every redraw un-hid an image whose load had already failed. Now an image that fails stays hidden (the player remembers it), and in radio mode the bar and the mini bar show the station's letters under it. The same rule everywhere a logo is drawn: the tray's record and Recent stations rows and the sheet's head (`fillLogo`: letters under, our own route only, an image that fails hides), the Visuals header (both the radio header and the music one in `media-player-v2.js`); the station cards and page already did it (`StationLogo`).

**Decided by Dev (no board; redraw where you'd do it differently)**

1. **Auto-add under the list**, above the foot, not in the record (the record is full at 360px, and the switch reads as "this list, every day"); 9c's confirm takes the list's room and scrolls there, its buttons side by side (question 146).
2. **The scan in the deck**: the sheet's card (bar and words) under Scan now; the answer becomes the record's Now playing (question 144).
3. **Recent stations kept in this browser** (`localStorage` `ssRadioRecentStations`, eight), the playing one first in the selected style with the Playing tag; Play restarts on that station (question 142).
4. **Heard today** reads `GET /api/radio/heard?station_id=&days=1`, keeps today's rows in the browser's day, newest first; the count is the rows shown (question 143); rows put the state in words in its tone and only "Add" / "Upgrade" as buttons (compact labels, named in full for a screen reader), as the mini bar does.
5. **The foot** on Recent stations reads "All stations on the Radio page" (the station's page doesn't list stations).

### 2026-10-23: Dev, Move your music to another drive (14a–14t)

Built on `feat/drive-move` from `integration` (89906309), Phase C of Design's "Build order for Dev, 23 October afternoon", from `Move to another drive.dc.html` (14a–14t) after Ross's yes to the drive move, to the once-up-front Docker step with a pasted helper, and to **music folders only** (library, downloads and staging; settings and the database stay where they are). Design's two rules hold throughout: **copy, check, then switch; never move first**, and **a drive that's away is never an empty library**. Routes: `CONTRACTS.md`, "Your music's drives"; patterns: `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("Move your music to another drive", "The drive-away Notice", "The Folders card"); BACKLOG's entry is Built. Answer 90 lands with it: Activity's **Library jobs** filter.

**Per board**

- **14k, 14t (first, since it protects every install today).** SYNCHRO.S writes `.synchros-folder.json` at the root of the library, downloads and staging folders, and a watch looks every 30 seconds (`core/drives/markers.py`). A folder that's missing, unreadable, or empty without its marker while SYNCHRO.S wrote one there before (or the library stores tracks under it) reads as "isn't connected"; an empty mount never gets a marker. Every install gets its markers on the first look. The warning Notice ("Your library's drive isn't connected", "Last seen today at 09:12 · looks every 30 s") replaces Albums', Artists' and a library artist's content (the title and "494 albums when Music HD was last seen" stay), sits above Listening history, and leads Home's alert band, for everyone; members get it without Check again. Home names Downloads' or Staging's drive when only that one is away. While the library's drive is away, Maintenance's jobs that find missing files, clean up or move files skip the run and say why in it, Maintenance's fixes refuse, the deep scan keeps its "stale" rows, and downloads, auto-import and the automations that write to the music folders wait.
- **14a.** Settings → Library → Folders → Music gets the card in place of the three path fields (which stay in the page, hidden, because its save reads them): "Move to another drive…" in the head; rows `1fr 300px 120px auto` at 72px (what each folder holds and who reads it, the container path in mono over "on Macintosh HD", the size, Change…); the drive line under a hairline with free space, the 640px 4px bar, "Getting full" past 90% and "Almost full" (danger) past 98%. Change… opens a dialog with the folder and "This doesn't move anything. To move your music, use Move to another drive…"; it writes the page's field and lets the page save it, as Unlock did. Maintenance → Tools → Management gets the tool row "Move your music to another drive" (Copy the library, downloads and staging to a new drive, check them, then switch).
- **The flow (14b–14j; phones 14l–14s).** Full screen, like first-run setup: the 64px header with the promise line (always true: "Nothing has changed yet", "Copying · your music is still on Macintosh HD", "Switched to Music HD · the old copies are kept", "Nothing switched · your music is on Macintosh HD"), Leave then Close; the six-step row; the pinned footer with one primary. On a phone the 56px bar (×/‹, title, "n of 6"), the promise as a 40px line, and the footer's primary full width at 48px.
- **14b Choose:** the three folders with their counts and who reads them, the total, the drives as option cards (a backup drive shown, not offered; hidden on a phone), "Folder on Music HD" with Browse…, "They'll go to" with the three paths, and "Choose each folder separately" (each folder its own path; "Imports will copy rather than move" when one lands on another drive than the library). With no drive visible, the "Where to" area says so and leads to the Docker step.
- **14c, 14d, 14m The Docker step:** the success-tinted note, "Your computer" (Mac / Linux / Windows, guessed from how the library is mounted, saved when changed), the three numbered cards (Terminal; the install folder field and the one-use command with Copy, "What it does, in order", "See the lines it adds"; "Come back here"), and the undo line. The page looks every 3 seconds: a request that fails is "SYNCHRO.S is restarting · 24 s so far", then "SYNCHRO.S can see Music HD now" (with Back to choosing), "restarted, but still can't see /Volumes" (the three causes, Check again, Copy what to send for help), or, pressed before anything ran, "hasn't restarted since you opened this step". A phone says to do it on the computer and offers the `/move` link (which opens the flow there), with "Show the steps anyway".
- **14e Check:** space, writing (a test file, timed for the estimate), the format (exFAT with its two option cards; NTFS on a Mac; a symlink test catches exFAT behind Docker Desktop's sharing), a folder that isn't empty, a slow drive, the media server that can't see the drive, and slskd (below). A danger row turns "See the plan" off with the reason beside it; exFAT asks for the choice.
- **14f Plan:** the three tiles, the five steps, the "Also compare every file's contents" switch with its cost, "Paused while it copies" with the automations that move files named, "Carries on", the stop sentence.
- **14g Copying, 14h Check and switch:** the bar and "41.2 of 93.2 GB · about 46 min left · 31 MB/s", per-folder rows (small folders first; the current one with its bar and file), the info line; Stop and undo asks in place ("Stop and delete the half-made copy? 41.2 GB copied so far." Keep copying / Stop and undo). Then one numbered list: copied, checking ("3,102 of 3,671 match · none different so far"), switch SYNCHRO.S, point slskd and Plex, start what was paused.
- **14i Done:** the checks after the switch (files found at the new path, slskd, the media server, what started again), "The old copies are still on Macintosh HD" with **Remove the old copies…** (the Safe action: Files / Frees / Deleted for good, the list with "Download the full list", "I checked the list", the danger "Delete 3,671 files") and **Switch back to Macintosh HD…** (asks in place; files added on the new drive since are copied back first). Home reminds a week after the switch with the same two buttons.
- **14j Failure:** one danger Notice for every failure, always with **"Your music is untouched on Macintosh HD"** and the way on: the drive went away (Plug it in and carry on: keeps the files that match, copies the rest), it filled up, files didn't match (Copy them again), old files couldn't be read (Carry on without them, which leaves them where they are), the switch was refused (switched back already; Try the switch again), SYNCHRO.S restarted mid-copy.

**The job** (`core/drives/move.py`). One move at a time; its state is the server's (`drive_moves`, one row per file in `drive_move_files`), so it survives a restart and reads the same on a second device, and a `library_jobs` row puts it in Activity's Library jobs with Stop. The copy walks each folder without following a link (links are skipped; nothing leads out of the tree), writes each file to a part file, syncs and renames it, keeps its time, and is resumable (a copy whose size matches is kept). The check compares count and size, and the contents (SHA-256) when the switch is on. The switch writes markers at the new folders, then changes the three folder settings **and every stored path** (tracks, history, retag, repair findings, manual matches, import history…) in **one database transaction** (`ConfigManager.write_in_transaction`, holding the settings' save lock), then points slskd and the media server at the new place; any refusal puts slskd.yml back byte for byte, takes the new folder out of the media server, and reverses the transaction. Downloads in progress finish first; downloads, Wanted's searches, auto-import and the automations that move files wait while it copies, and start again after (a restart releases them too). A Settings page loaded before the switch can't point the folders back at the old copies when it next saves (`core/drives/settings_guard.py`). Remove the old copies deletes only the exact files this move copied and checked, each only while it's still the same size and its copy is still there, through a 15-minute plan, never by pattern.

**The helper** (`core/drives/compose_tool.py`, served by `core/drives/helper.py`). The command the page shows is `cd ~/SoulSync && curl -fsSL "http://<this address>/api/drives/helper.sh?t=<token>" -o drives-helper.sh && sh drives-helper.sh` (saved first, so its question reads the keyboard and `sh drives-helper.sh --undo` works later). It checks Docker and python3 (on a Mac it skips Apple's stub python3 when the Command Line Tools aren't installed and says to run `xcode-select --install`, step 1 of the Mac guide), finds the compose file, finds the services that mount the music folders (SYNCHRO.S by its `/app/Transfer`, `/app/downloads`, `/app/Staging` mounts, slskd, Plex, Jellyfin or Navidrome by image or name, and any service sharing a music folder), prints the lines it will add and asks "Go ahead? (y/n)", keeps `docker-compose.before-drives.yml`, edits the file with a small YAML block editor that only **inserts** lines (comments, anchors and layout untouched; it refuses a flow list, a `<<:` merge it would hide, tabs, more than one document), checks the result with `docker compose config`, runs `docker compose up -d` and prints "Done. Go back to SYNCHRO.S."; `--undo` puts the copy back byte for byte and restarts; `--dry-run` only shows. A service that's not in the file (Ross's Plex) is said, not an error. On Ross's file it adds `/Volumes:/Volumes` to `soulsync` and `slskd` and `./slskd:/slskd-config` to `soulsync`. The token is the script route's only credential: 32 random bytes kept as a hash, one fetch, ten minutes, constant-time compare, a new one retires the old; the page makes a fresh command when one is used or expired.

**Design's questions for Dev, answered**

- **The compose folder:** the install records it. The app can't read `com.docker.compose.project.working_dir` without the Docker socket, and SYNCHRO.S shouldn't be given the socket for this. 14c's field is filled from the setting `drives.compose_folder`, `~/SoulSync` (the Mac guide's folder) until someone edits it; the command follows the field, and only plain path characters are accepted, since it goes into a shell.
- **The helper:** as asked (above), at `/api/drives/helper.sh`, admin-minted, one-use, short-lived. The **PowerShell twin isn't built yet**: Docker Desktop on Windows has no Python, so the twin would be a second YAML editor in PowerShell, and one that rewrites someone's set-up file shouldn't ship until it has run against real Windows installs. Windows gets the same lines as three by-hand steps meanwhile (question 122).
- **slskd:** not through its API in practice. `GET`/`PUT /api/v0/options/yaml` need remote configuration on **and** a signed-in session (`OptionsController` takes JWT only); the API key SYNCHRO.S holds is refused, and remote configuration is off by default. So the helper gives SYNCHRO.S slskd's config folder, and the switch edits `slskd.yml` there with the same comment-keeping editor: `shares.directories` (the library's share replaced; the old share named by the setting `drives.slskd_library_share`, else the only share, else one called music, library or transfer), a `shares.filters` entry for `._` files on exFAT, and `directories.downloads` when Downloads moves. slskd watches the file, so new shares apply at once; SYNCHRO.S then asks for the rescan (`PUT /api/v0/shares`, which the API key may do). YAML outranks `SLSKD_SHARED_DIR` (slskd loads the file after the environment). A new download folder needs a restart, which slskd's API allows only for a signed-in admin, so Check asks for slskd's sign-in once when Downloads moves (question 120).
- **Plex, Jellyfin, Navidrome:** Plex and Jellyfin, yes: the switch adds the new folder to the Music library (Plex `addLocations`, Jellyfin `/Library/VirtualFolders/Paths`) and keeps the old one until Remove the old copies takes it out, so play counts and ratings carry over; Check asks the server whether it can see the drive first (Plex's browse, Jellyfin's ValidatePath). Navidrome reads its music folder from its own settings (`ND_MUSICFOLDER`), not its API, so Check and Done say to change it there.
- **The job, "isn't connected":** as described above.
- **90, Library jobs:** built: a "Library jobs" Show filter after Automations holding Look for releases you don't have and the move, both with Stop (the move's Stop is Stop and undo); library jobs left the Automations filter.

**Decided by Dev (no board shows these)**

1. **The helper is saved, then run** (`curl … -o drives-helper.sh && sh drives-helper.sh`) rather than piped to `sh`: a piped script can't ask y/n, and the undo needs the file.
2. **Paths are the container's** (`/Volumes/Music HD/…` reads the same inside and out once the drives folder is mounted at the same path); the old folders show as `/app/Transfer` (question 125).
3. **The draft's step follows the server** (the checks, then the plan), so the flow reopens where it was on any device; the Docker step and the Check again boot id are remembered in the tab.
4. **Sizes are decimal** ("93.2 GB"), as the Mac's Finder says them.
5. **A switch back** copies back anything added or changed on the new drive since the switch, then runs the switch in reverse; the copy on the new drive stays. slskd.yml gets the reverse edit applied to the file as it is then (the library share and the download folder go back, the "._" filter the switch added goes), so later edits to it stay. When Downloads moved, slskd restarts again, so switching back asks for slskd's sign-in once (kept only for that request).
6. **The recycle bin isn't used** for the old copies (it lives on the same disk, so it frees nothing); the dialog says it can't be undone.
7. **"Almost full"** is the danger tag's word past 98% (14a draws only "Getting full").
8. **The flow sits under the shared dialogs** (z-index 10900), so the Safe action opens over it; the app's floating help and notification buttons step aside while it's open.
9. **A destination has to be a folder of its own.** It may not be, sit inside or hold any of the three music folders (moving or not), compared by path and by what's on disk, so a bind mount that shows the library at a second path is refused too; Check also looks for a random file it writes in each music folder. Two destinations may not overlap. A marker only marks a folder as an earlier move's when it's for the same role and isn't a folder SYNCHRO.S uses now.
10. **Nothing already at the new place is trusted.** A file the move hasn't marked copied is copied (over whatever is there); the check compares the old file's size and modification time with what was copied, and Remove the old copies skips an old file whose size or time changed since, or that is the same file as its copy.
11. **Anything going wrong after the settings switch switches everything back**: the folder settings and stored paths, slskd.yml, a second slskd restart when it had restarted, and the media server's new folder. If the settings themselves can't go back, the failure says so ("The switch didn't finish · nothing was deleted") and offers only Try the switch again; Stop and undo never deletes a copy the settings point at. Check runs the same slskd.yml edit as a dry run, so a file it can't edit shows before anything is copied.
12. **While a move copies, Maintenance's file jobs and fixes, the reorganize queue, tag writes and reorganizes wait** (the routes answer that a move is copying), as downloads, auto-import and automations already did.
13. **The helper runs `docker compose` without `-f`** when it found the default file, so `docker-compose.override.yml` and `COMPOSE_FILE` apply, and it checks its edit together with those files (with `COMPOSE_FILE` it edits the first file listed). Each run keeps its own "before" copy and a SHA-256 of what it wrote; `--undo` puts the copy back only while the file is still exactly that.
14. **The pasted command checks the script**: the address is limited to plain URL characters and quoted, and `shasum -a 256 -c` (Mac) or `sha256sum -c` (Linux) checks the saved script against the one SYNCHRO.S serves before `sh` runs it.
15. **slskd's session** is forgotten once the switch is done, and when a move is stopped, left or finished. **slskd.yml's earlier text** is kept encrypted and never sent to the page.
16. **The slow parts of Choose and Check run in the background** (the folder sizes, the write test, the walks); the page reads the move again while `overview.measuring` or `checks.running`. Every change under `/api/drives/` is sent as JSON and refused from another site (`Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site`).

**The Mac guide's manual steps stay valid.** Nothing in the guide changes: an install that never uses the move keeps its compose file as it is (the marker files are the only thing written, at the folders' roots), and the Docker step's lines are additions the guide's set-up accepts as they are; `--undo` returns the file to exactly what the guide made.

**Tests.** Python (`tests/drives/`, 131): the marker rules and the watch (an empty mount reads away and gets no marker; last seen survives a restart), the guards (Maintenance jobs, the deep scan, auto-import, automations, downloads), the paths (outside the drives, `..`, links out of the drives folder or to another drive, the app's own folders, the moving folders), the helper against a copy of Ross's Mac compose file in a temp folder with a `docker` shim on PATH (dry run; no; yes: backup written byte for byte, `docker compose config` run on the new file, every key and value and comment kept, `up -d` run, "Done"; a second run changes nothing; undo restores the original byte for byte; no compose file; no python3; Docker refusing the file), the YAML editor's refusals and CRLF, slskd.yml edits keeping comments, the token (once, expiry, retired, wrong tokens, hashes only), the move end to end on real folders with a real ConfigManager (one transaction for settings and paths; links never followed; exFAT leaves `._` behind; checksum catches a damaged copy and Copy them again fixes it; the drive going away and carrying on; unreadable files left out; Stop and undo deletes only what it copied; a refusing media server or slskd switches everything back; a restart mid-copy; remove deletes exactly the checked files and skips a changed one or one whose copy went; switch back; the 7-day reminder; a second device), the routes (members refused everything but the state, which carries no paths; the helper route open through the gates and one-use), the Settings guard, and Activity's Library jobs; plus every test that reads the files changed. `ruff check` is clean. Web: `src/features/drives/drives.test.tsx` (19) and the Activity tests updated; the full vitest run, `npm run check`, `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` are clean, and the built shell loads with no console errors in Chromium.

**Screenshots** (1440×900 and 390×844, Lilac, in the session scratchpad beside the board renders; not in the repo): the Folders card, Change…, Maintenance's row, Choose (with and without drives), the Docker step and its four Check again answers, Check (exFAT), the Plan, Copying, Stop and undo asking, Check and switch, Done, Remove the old copies…, a failure, the phones, and "isn't connected" on Albums, Artists, an artist, Listening history and Home, plus a real move on the scratch install (refused by an unreachable Plex and switched back, then switched, then the old copies removed) and Activity's Library jobs.

The patterns are in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("Scan now", "Scan now services", "The Spotify pause", "Listening history", "Look for releases you don't have", and the `DialogFrame` and `ActionSheet` rows).

### 2026-10-23: Dev, AudD listens automatically when the station doesn't say

Built on `feat/audd-auto` from `integration` (b3eefa91), from Ross's decisions of 23 October. AudD now identifies tracks on its own, but only when needed, and within a daily cap set in Settings → Radio. Routes changed: `GET`/`PUT /api/radio/settings` gain `settings.audd_auto` and `settings.audd_auto_daily_cap`, and `scan.audd_auto` (`CONTRACTS.md`, "Internet radio"); now playing's and Heard's `source` can be `audd`. BACKLOG gains "AudD on every track change" (Exploring). The row's pattern is in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("Scan now services").

**When AudD is asked.** Only all of these together:

- **The station is watched:** someone is playing it, or a profile has auto-add on for it.
- **The station isn't naming the track:** its feed isn't naming one (or isn't healthy), and its stream title is empty, missing or generic (a show, the station's own name, an advert), as the stream-title parser already reads it.
- **The free AcoustID fingerprint answered "no match".** An AcoustID error isn't a miss, so AudD isn't asked then.

It rides on what's there: the clip the relay already captures for the automatic fingerprint (the same relay audio Scan now's tap uses), and the hub's fingerprint step (`RadioHub._run_fingerprint`), which asks AudD with the same clip when AcoustID found nothing. No second listener. AudD's call is Scan now's own (`scan_providers.audd`, through `guarded_json`).

**The cap and pacing.** `radio.audd_auto` (on by default) and `radio.audd_auto_daily_cap` (100 by default, 0 to 1000, 0 turns it off). Each request is counted before it's sent, so the cap is never passed; it resets at local midnight. Scan now by hand and Test never count against it (they still count in the month's usage, as before, and automatic requests count there too). Per station: at least 3 minutes between automatic requests, and one per track window; a window ends when the stream title changes, or after 10 minutes on a station that never sends titles. A capped, paced or failed request spends its window, so nothing is retried in a loop (the fingerprint's own 150-second gap still holds too). AudD refusing the token or saying there are no requests left stops automatic AudD until local midnight and sets the card's Refused / No requests left tag (phase 2's `radio_scan_keys`); a new token, or a later answer AudD accepts, lifts it.

**What people see.** An automatic answer reads "found by AudD" (a press keeps "found by AudD · Scan now"), and flows into Heard and auto-add like the automatic fingerprint's, with the same guards (owned or wanted, the quality target, the daily auto-add limit). When both miss, the reason says "neither AcoustID nor AudD recognised it". Settings → Radio's Scan now services card gains the row "Listen automatically when the station doesn't say": a plain line, the Daily cap field and the switch (admins), with "12 of 100 automatic today" under AudD's month counts and, when it has stopped, why ("Today's 100 are used. It starts again at midnight."). Members read the line only. The card's sentence no longer says the automatic identification never uses the paid services.

Code: `core/internet_radio/audd_auto.py` (new: the rules, the cap, pacing, stopping for the day), `hub.py` (`audd_needed`, `station_names_track`, the step after the fingerprint, the title window), `settings.py`, `store.py` (`claim_audd_auto`, `audd_auto_used`: the day's count shares `radio_scan_usage`, as provider `audd_auto` keyed by local day), `api/radio.py`; `src/shell/radio-settings.ts` (`autoLine`, `autoUsageLine`, `autoStateWords`, `renderAuto`), `src/features/radio/radio.format.ts` (`audd`), `radio.types.ts`, `static/radio-settings.css`, `index.html`.

**Decided by Dev**

1. **On by default with the cap at 100**, as Ross set it; nothing is sent until an AudD token is saved, so an install without AudD sees no change.
2. **Watched means played or auto-add on.** A station watched only for auto-add, with nobody playing it, gets automatic AudD only once something captures its audio: today only the relay does, as for the automatic fingerprint.
3. **A request that couldn't be counted isn't sent**, and an AudD error leaves AcoustID's "no match" reason as it was.

**Questions for Design:** 110–112 in the list above.

**Tests:** Python: `tests/internet_radio/test_audd_auto.py` (new, 26 tests, fake clocks, AudD faked at the outbound guard: a named station or feed sends nothing, generic titles do, AcoustID finding it or erring sends nothing, only watched stations, off / cap 0 / no token, the cap and its midnight reset, Scan now's Test not counted, one request per window, 3 minutes apart, a title-less station's slow new window, per-station pacing, Refused and No requests left stop it for the day and set the tag, a new token lifts that, errors not retried, Heard and auto-add with their guards, Settings save and read, members, and the token in no log or answer), with the rest of `tests/internet_radio` and the files that read the files changed. `ruff check` is clean. Vitest: `radio-settings.audd-auto.test.ts` (new: the words, the switch, the cap, today's count on AudD's row only, why it stopped, members) and `radio.format.test.ts` ("found by AudD"), with the other radio files. `npm run check` has no errors; `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` are clean.

**Screenshots** (in the session scratchpad; not in the repo): Settings → Radio's Scan now services card with the new row at 1440 and 390.

### 2026-10-23: Dev, phase 4 (the visualisers: MilkDrop and Classic)

Built on `ux/oct23-4` from `integration`, from Phase 4 of Design's "Build order for Dev, 23 October" and `Visuals (Winamp visualisers).dc.html` (10a–10f), with Ross's decisions of the same day taking precedence where they differ from the boards: **auto-cycle is Off by default** (the select keeps Off · Every 30 s · Every 2 min · On track change); **phones start at Full**, like desktop, and the frame-rate guard handles weak ones; presets come from npm plus **your own folder**; **AVS isn't built** (its family button isn't shown; a later spike, `BACKLOG.md`). The patterns are in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("The Visuals overlay (10a–10f)", the new `LabelledSwitch`), the routes in `CONTRACTS.md` ("Visuals"), the licences and where the presets come from in `BACKLOG.md`.

**Per board item**

- **The chrome (10a):** the overlay's header is now 64px on `#101010` (Close; the art, title and "artist · album"; ‹ the visual's name over "by author · family" ›; "Visuals" with `aria-expanded` and `aria-pressed`; Fill the screen) and the transport is the bar's centre at 72px (elapsed, the waveform, total, Previous / Pause / Next, mute, volume, "? shortcuts"). Both fade after 3 s still and come back on movement, a touch or a key; the panel keeps them up. The waveform, times and volume are the player's own (`player.ts` drives them in the overlay too).
- **The panel (10a, 10b, 10d):** the 520px drawer with the family row (SYNCHRO.S / MilkDrop / Classic), each family's content and the foot. SYNCHRO.S is today's 18 tiles three across; Classic is two tiles with stills and one setting each, Auto-cycle disabled with its reason and Quality as "Full".
- **MilkDrop:** Butterchurn (WebGL 2) loaded with `import()` the first time MilkDrop is opened; the index route is asked for at the same moment, never on page load; each preset is fetched by key when picked and kept. The browser: search over names and authors, Show All / Favourites N / Recent N, the Author select, "N presets · A–Z · N authors", Random preset, 56px rows with the Playing / Previewing / Loading / Won't load tags and a 44px star; hover or focus for 400 ms previews on the stage, leaving goes back after a second, Enter or a click keeps it. The foot: Auto-cycle, Blend (2 s crossfade or a cut), Quality Full / Half / Quarter.
- **Your own presets:** an admin sets a folder (Player settings → MilkDrop → Your own presets); its `.milk` files join the list named from the file ("Author - Title.milk", " · yours" in the row) and are converted in the browser when picked (`milkdrop-preset-converter`, loaded only then). The server lists the folder and hands a file back only by key, after checking it's still inside the folder, `.milk`, a regular file and under 512 KB.
- **Classic (10b):** Winamp's 19-bar spectrum with falling peaks (16 segments, green → yellow → red, peaks hang then fall faster) at 720px wide at 1440, centred in the room the panel leaves, and the oscilloscope (Lines / Dots / Solid), drawn on their own canvas from the same analyser.
- **The credit (10c):** after every change for 4 s and whenever the chrome is up, bottom-left: the name over "by author · family · n of N · what moved it"; the V / L hints the first three times, then only in "?". On a phone it spans the width above the mini transport with the star.
- **Keys:** inside the overlay V next, Shift+V previous, R random, L star (MilkDrop); Esc closes the panel, then the overlay; F toggles it (and does nothing with nothing playing). Outside the overlay L is still the A–B loop (question 83, still open). The shortcuts list says which is which.
- **States (10d):** loading (the last preset keeps running, a spinner in the credit); won't load (the warning card in the credit's place with Next preset / Try it again, the row tagged, auto-cycle and V skip it for the session); no WebGL 2 (one warning Notice with "Show SYNCHRO.S's visuals"); Reduce motion (system or Player settings → Reduce visual effects: opens still with the art and "Play visuals this time" for the session, plus a Player settings link); a direct radio stream the analyser can't hear (after 3 s of silence: the card with "Play through SYNCHRO.S", which restarts the station through `POST /api/radio/play` with `force_relay: true`); nothing playing (the "…" row is disabled-neutral with "Play something first"); the frame-rate guard (under 24 fps for 5 s drops Quality one step, once per preset, and says so in the credit with "Put it back").
- **Phone (10e, 10f):** the panel is a 560px sheet with a grabber; the family and Show rows scroll sideways; Author and Blend are in Player settings → MilkDrop; Quality starts at Full.
- **Fixed in passing:** the help's first-run "New here? Click for setup help!" tip no longer shows over the visuals (the floating buttons already hid there).

**How it's put together.** The legacy engine (`static/media-player-v2.js`) still opens and closes the overlay and paints the SYNCHRO.S visuals; it now paints only while `data-visuals-engine` on the overlay says `synchros`, and hands the overlay's keys, the header's ‹ › and Escape to `window.SoulSyncVisuals` (`src/features/visuals`), which owns the panel, the credit, the states, MilkDrop's and Classic's canvases, and an analyser tapped off the player's (no output, so nothing is heard twice; Butterchurn is fed its samples and never touches the audio graph). The new pieces load the first time the overlay opens or Player settings shows; Butterchurn and the converter load later still.

**Bundle size:** the page's entry chunk grew by 0.8 KB (637.7 KB, 191.9 KB gzipped; was 636.9 / 191.6) and the shell by 0.2 KB (`player.ts`). Loaded on demand: the visuals UI 52.7 KB (16.6 KB gzipped) plus 12.9 KB of CSS when the overlay first opens; Butterchurn 198 KB (44 KB gzipped) when MilkDrop is first opened; the converter 767 KB (249 KB gzipped) only when one of your own presets is picked; each preset 1–30 KB when picked. The built shell loads with no console errors in Chromium (no `process is not defined`; the converter brings its own `process` shim, Butterchurn reads no Node globals).

**Decided by Dev (the boards don't show these; redraw where you'd do it differently)**

1. **MilkDrop starts on** the preset you had, else Butterchurn's own example ("dedicated to the sherwin maxawow" by Flexi, martin + geiss), else the first A–Z; it doesn't change until you pick (auto-cycle Off).
2. **V and auto-cycle walk the list as the browser shows it** (the search, Show and Author you left), else everything A–Z.
3. **"Won't load" lasts the session** (kept in the tab): a reload tries them again.
4. **Favourites are MilkDrop only**; L in the other families does nothing. R in Classic swaps Bars and Scope.
5. **Auto-cycle is one setting** for MilkDrop and the SYNCHRO.S visuals (the existing key); Player settings' select now offers the board's four plus the older 1 and 5 minutes, which still work if they're what's saved.
6. **Quality applies to MilkDrop**; the SYNCHRO.S visuals keep their own Quality in Player settings, Classic always draws at full size.
7. **"Reduce visual effects"** (Player settings; was "Reduce motion", which slowed the visuals) now opens the overlay still, as 10d draws.
8. **The radio card** shows only on a direct station after 3 s of silence, and goes away as soon as there's sound.
9. **The credit's words** for the other families: "Classic · drawn by SYNCHRO.S · 1 of 2 · next" and "drawn by SYNCHRO.S · 6 of 18 · random"; the first credit on opening ends "where you left it" and carries no hints.
10. **Focus:** opening the panel lands on the selected family; closing it returns to the Visuals button.
11. **The old drawer** (Up next, Recent, Lyrics, Details, Settings inside the visuals) is gone, as 10a's header has no ☰ (question 100).
12. **`LabelledSwitch`** is new in `components/form` (10a's 104 × 44 switch with its word); radio and Modules still draw their own and can move to it.

**Tests:** Python: the new `tests/visuals/test_visuals_api.py` (34: names and keys, the index from a build and a folder, search and author, one preset by key, keys that aren't found, links and oversize files never read, a file swapped for a link after listing, the listing cap, favourites per profile and their limits, the migration's table, the setting for admins and members) and every test that reads the files changed (`index.html`, `web_server.py`, `music_database.py`, `main.tsx`): 3,756 passed; `ruff check` is clean. Web: the full vitest run passes (640 files, 11,439 tests), with new tests for the pure rules (`visuals.logic.test.ts`: the list, steps and random, auto-cycle, Quality and the frame-rate guard, the credit, the keys, the states), Classic's drawing (`classic.test.ts`), the controller (`visuals-controller.test.ts`: the index only on MilkDrop's first opening, picks and Recent, your own converted, keys per family, favourites and their rollback, won't load and Try it again, loading, no WebGL 2, Reduce motion and "Play visuals this time", the radio card and the relay, the guard and Put it back, auto-cycle Off / every 30 s / on track change, hover preview, the hints), the panel and cards (`visuals-panel.test.tsx`), `LabelledSwitch`, the build's preset copy, the player's Visuals row and the overlay's transport, and the legacy keys (V / Shift+V / R / L to the visuals inside, L the A–B loop outside, Esc panel first, F with nothing playing). `npm run check` has no errors, `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` are clean.

### 2026-10-23: Dev, phase 3 (Listening history, releases you don't have, Remove through the recycle bin)

Built on `ux/oct23-3` from `integration`, from Phase 3 of Design's "Build order for Dev, 23 October": `Listening history (Library).dc.html` (13a, 13b), approved by Ross, and Design's answers 64, 67, 68, 73, 74, 76 and 80. Ross answered 76 **no**: no Any-quality profile and no route; the button is renamed. 64, 68, 74, 76 and 80 are out of the open list. The routes are in `CONTRACTS.md` ("Listening history"; "Library: Albums, Genres and Artists" for Remove, the album counts and the job; "Activity feed" for the job's rows); the patterns in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("Listening history", "Look for releases you don't have", "Remove from library, from the Artist page", and the nebula and Follow… rows).

**Per answer**

- **64, Listening history (13a, 13b).** Its own Library page, `/library?view=history`, and sidebar row after Albums with the clock icon (the top bar and tab say "Listening history"; `nav-routes.ts` lights the row for `view=history`). The header: title, sentence, the 320px filter ("Filter by title, artist or album") and the secondary "Open Stats". Kind (Music / Podcasts / Audiobooks with counts, Music first) and Range (Today / 7 days / 30 days / All time; 7 days by default, the pick remembered per browser, `soulsync.library.history.range`; the URL holds the kind and the filter, and a range only when it names one). The meta line ("412 plays in the last 7 days · from SYNCHRO.S's player and Plex · yours only"). Day groups with the 12px uppercase head and the whole day's play count. Rows `72px 48px 1fr 200px 100px` at 64px: the time in tabular monospace, 48px art, the title over "artist · album · format · length" (artist and album link to the Artist page and its album; the format in `#fbbf24` below your quality target; the source phrase only when the play wasn't in SYNCHRO.S's player: "on Plex, Living room", "on Jellyfin", "from Last.fm", "heard on FIP Jazz"), the tag slot ("Below your target" only), and on hover or focus-within the 44px accent-light Play (queues the track after the current one, `window.playNext`) and "…" (Open artist, Open album, Add to playlist…). A track that left the library: the row at 62%, "no longer in your library", no Play. "8 of 412 · Show 50 more". Empty: "Nothing played in the last 7 days" with "Try 30 days" (the next wider range) and "Shuffle your library". Podcasts and Audiobooks: "show · 23 min left" (accent-light) or "Finished", Resume (accent-light) or a ghost Play; audiobooks say "sample" (67). The phone (13b): the title with a "Stats" link, Kind sideways, Range as four equal buttons ("All"), `44px 1fr 44px` rows with the time at the end of the meta line. The Stats card is now a short "Recently played" (8 plays) with "All of it in Listening history"; its Kind filter moved to the page. Code: `src/routes/library/-ui/history-page.tsx`, `history.module.css`, `-library.history.ts`; `core/playback/history.py`, `api/player.py`.
- **Routes for 64.** Music: `GET /api/library/listening-history` reads the listening history as Recent does, every source in the caller's pile (Recent reads only the player's own plays), with `server_source`, the media server's player name and a Heard match. **The player name is new:** Plex's history names a device id; the poll now turns it into the player's name through the server's device list (one request per poll) and keeps it in the new column `listening_history.player_name` (migration in `_add_listening_history_owner`; `core/listening_import/dedup.py` writes it and fills it on a matched play). Jellyfin and Navidrome don't say, so their plays read "on Jellyfin". Podcasts and Audiobooks: `GET /api/player/plays` takes `range`, `tz`, `q` and `offset` and answers `total` and `days` (without `range` it answers as before). One route counts every kind and range: `GET /api/library/listening-history/counts`. **Design's question, answered:** the history doesn't carry the format, and its length is only what the source reported; the row reads the format, the quality verdict and the length from the library track the play is linked to, as the queue does (the source's length is kept for a play with no track). So an upgraded album shows its new format on old plays.
- **68, Look for releases you don't have.** In Library → Artists' header "…" in phase 1's slot (after the hairline, before "Follow every artist you don't follow…"), for admins, and as the action of the empty Show → Incomplete. It asks first (the 680 dialog: Artists to look up, At most 1 a second, Adds to Wanted Nothing), then starts a stoppable background job. Activity shows it under Automations ("Looking for releases you don't have" · "212 artists · 40 done", with Stop); when it ends, the Artists header's sentence says what it found for a day ("· The last look found 61 releases you don't have, by 18 artists"). **The job** (`core/library/missing_releases.py`): one at a time for the install, on its own daemon thread. It asks about every library artist the metadata cache lists no release for (the release index now records which artists have any: `ReleaseIndex.listed`, `artists_without_releases`), one at a time, at most one a second, through the Artist page's discography fetch (`get_artist_discography` in `core/metadata/discography_strict.py`: each service gets the artist's own id from the library, else a name search), which fills the metadata cache the index reads. No new worker framework: the job framework the app has for this shape is the threaded worker plus Activity's recorded histories, so each run is a row in a new `library_jobs` table and Activity reads it, with the live state for the running one. **Spotify's pause:** when the discography chain includes Spotify, it waits while Spotify is paused or cooling down (`SpotifyClient.is_rate_limited()`, `get_post_ban_cooldown_remaining()`), and Activity says "waiting for Spotify's pause, 9 min left"; a rate-limit refusal waits for the pause, then asks once more; the calls run under `spotify_feature("looking for releases you don't have")`, so a pause it triggers names it; the client's own rate-limit handling and post-pause easing apply to every call. Five refusals in a row (not rate limits) end the run as failed. Stop is checked before each artist and while it waits. A run a restart cut off reads "Stopped after … when SYNCHRO.S restarted". When it ends it rebuilds the release index and counts the releases you don't have (and aren't on Wanted) by the artists it checked. Routes: `GET /api/library/missing-releases`, `POST …/start`, `POST …/stop` (admins).
- **74, Remove from library on the Artist page.** It opens Library's Safe action for every album of that artist through the `library_album_remove` plan (`remove-preview` with `artist_id`): the file list, "I checked the list", then the files go to the recycle bin and the albums leave the library; once nothing of theirs is left (a guest track on someone else's album keeps them) the artist's row goes too, with its `similar_artists` and `rematch_hints` rows. Closing the done dialog goes to Library → Artists. The page no longer calls the database-only `DELETE /api/library/artist/<id>` (the route stays for other callers); its helper `-artist-detail.delete-artist.ts` is gone.
- **76 (Ross: no).** The nebula's "Accept any quality for Lantern" is "Search by hand for Lantern…" with "pick a file below your target" as its meta, on two lines; it still opens Search by hand on the most-tried stuck track. No route, no profile; "Change quality" stays out of the Wanted row's menu.
- **80.** One batch count is cheap: one query on the service's indexed artist-id column (`idx_artists_deezer_id`, `idx_artists_spotify_id`, …), joined to albums by `idx_albums_artist_id` and checked against tracks by `idx_tracks_album_id` (a test checks the plan uses the id index). `POST /api/library/artists/album-counts` answers a page of results; the Follow… dialog's artist rows say "You have 6 albums" (nothing when the library has none, since it may simply not know that service's id). Labels and podcasts stay without; authors keep "You have N".
- **67 and 73** were built in phase 1; 73's slot now holds 68.

**Decided by Dev (the boards don't show these; redraw where you'd do it differently)**

1. **The Kind counts are all time** and the meta line counts the range (13a's 1,204 beside 412; question 93).
2. **"Yours only"** when the pile is the profile's own or no other profile shares it; otherwise "shared with 2 profiles that don't keep their own" (#1293's shared pile).
3. **Two words for a gone track:** "no longer in your library" when the play was linked to a library track that has gone, "not in your library" for a play that never was (a Last.fm import of something you don't have). Both fade and have no Play.
4. **Last.fm and ListenBrainz imports** say "from Last.fm" / "from ListenBrainz" in the source phrase.
5. **The Heard match** (question 92): a play in SYNCHRO.S's player whose artist and title match a Heard row this profile added to Wanted.
6. **Days** are the browser's (the page sends `Date.getTimezoneOffset()`), so "Today" starts at your midnight; 7 and 30 days are rolling.
7. **The phone keeps the filter field** under the title (question 91), and music rows keep Play without the "…" (question 94).
8. **No meta line over an empty list**; the empty title says it.
9. **Look for releases is admins'** and asks first (question 95). It fetches for artists with no release listed at all, singles included, so an artist with only singles cached isn't asked again. An artist the services return nothing for is asked again next run.
10. **Library jobs file under Automations** in Activity (question 90), and a running one counts in "N running".
11. **The Artist page's Remove** plans every album row of that artist, files or not (tracks without a file are forgotten, not moved).

**Components**

- No shared component changed. ### 2026-10-23: Dev, phase 2 (Scan now services, the phone radio sheet, Change logo)

Built on `ux/oct23-2` from `integration`, from Phase 2 of Design's "Build order for Dev, 23 October" (approved by Ross): `Radio follow-ups (scan now, sheet).dc.html` 11c, 11d and 11e, and Design's answers 58 (the Settings card), 66 and 82. 58, 66 and 82 are done and out of the open list. Routes changed: `GET /api/radio/settings` gains `scan.services` (each service's tag state and the date its key was saved) and answers members with Scan now's rows and tags only; `POST /api/radio/scan/test` returns `services` too; new `PUT /api/radio/links/<id>` `{logo_url}` (`CONTRACTS.md`, "Internet radio"). The patterns are in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` ("The phone radio sheet", "Scan now services", "A row "…" menu on a legacy settings table", and the sheet sentence in "Scan now").

**Per answer**

- **58, Settings → Radio's "Scan now services" card (11c).** Under Track identification: the title and 11c's sentence; AcoustID, AudD and ACRCloud as rows `32px 1fr 150px 220px auto` at 72px: the 32px number chip, the name at 16/600 over one line of what it needs ("Free · a fingerprint · uses the AcoustID key from Track identification above", "Token set, 14 March 2026 · Replace the token…", "Keys set, 2 October 2026 · Replace the keys…", "A project's host, access key and secret, from the ACRCloud console"), a StatusTag (Ready / Not set up / Refused / No requests left), "41 requests in October · 29 found the track" ("Not asked" before a service is set up), Test, and Remove for the paid two. An unset service opens Host, Access key and Access secret (AudD: API token) under its row with "Save and test" as the card's one primary, which saves the fields (the host still checked by the server: a name under `acrcloud.com`) and then runs the service's Test. "Replace the token…" opens the same fields in place with Cancel. The foot line says what Test costs. Members see the rows and tags only. **Backend:** the month's requests and finds per service were already counted (`radio_scan_usage`, bumped where a scan asks a service and when it finds the track, and by paid Tests). New: the music database's `radio_scan_keys` (provider, the date a key was saved through `PUT /api/radio/settings`, and the last thing a scan or Test heard from that service about its key: `ok`, `bad_key` or `out_of_credit`), read into `scan.services` on the settings route. Never a key in the answer, the table or the logs. - **66, Change logo….** Settings → Radio's links table: each row keeps Check and gains a 44px "…" with Change logo… and Remove. Change logo… opens the address under the row, with a 56px preview (the letters, the current logo over them), "Save logo" and Cancel; it goes through the new `PUT /api/radio/links/<id>` `{logo_url}`, which runs adding's check and fetch (the outbound guard, then one fetch: PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP or ICO up to 512 KB) and changes nothing when the logo can't be used (the server's sentence stays in the panel, the address kept). An empty address goes back to the letters. Built-in and directory stations have no override (only links have the route). **Decided by Dev (the boards don't show these; redraw where you'd do it differently)**

1. **One set of fields at a time.** With both paid services unset, the first (AudD) opens its fields and ACRCloud shows an outline "Set up…" until AudD is done (or "Set up…" is pressed, which moves the fields), so the card keeps one primary.
2. **What sets a tag:** Refused is the service refusing the key at its last answer (a scan or a Test); No requests left is the service saying so this month (a new month reads Ready again); any answer from the service, a match or not, or a newly saved key clears either. AcoustID also takes Setup check's own check when that's newer. A service that didn't answer, or was too busy, doesn't change its tag.
3. **"Token set" without a date** for keys saved before dates were kept (and AcoustID's row never shows a date: its key lives in Track identification, and can be saved from other pages).
4. **Save and test** says "Saved." then the Test's own sentence in the card's status line ("Saved. ACRCloud accepted the keys."), and the focus moves to the row's Test. A missing field is named ("Fill in the access secret first.") and nothing is sent.
5. **Members** get no counts and a line that says what kind of service it is ("Free · a fingerprint", "Paid · an account with a monthly allowance"); the page's other cards are hidden for them (question 87).
6. **Remove** still clears the keys at once, as built (question 88).
7. **The row "…" holds Remove** with Change logo…, so each link row has one visible action (Check); 5b drew Check and Remove side by side before there was a third.
8. **Not found and failed in the sheet** are one line under "Scan again" (failed in the danger colour), since there's no result card; the answer of a found scan is only Now playing.
9. **The sheet's volume** is its own slider that drives the player's (the deck stays hidden), kept across redraws so a drag isn't cut short by a scan's once-a-second poll. It keeps the player's thumb (11d draws the bar alone).
10. **An adding-disabled track** (named, but not yet in Heard) shows Add disabled with "It can be added once it's in Heard on radio." under it, in the same place as "Nothing identified to add yet."
11. **The station's name in the head links to its page** (11d draws it as a link) as well as the Station page row.

### 2026-10-23: Dev, slskd rescans its shares after imports

Built on `feat/slskd-rescan` from `integration`. Ross's slskd had shared 0 files for days while SYNCHRO.S downloaded 88 GB into the library. slskd scans its shared folders once, when it starts; it had started with an empty `/music`, never rescanned, and kept sharing nothing as the library grew to 3,627 files. The Soulseek network blocks people who share nothing.

**What changed**

- **After imports, slskd is asked to rescan.** `core/slskd_share_rescan.py`. The import pipeline calls `notify_library_import()` where a file has landed in the library (both successful ends of `core/imports/pipeline.py`'s post-processing, next to `emit_track_downloaded`), and `core/downloads/lifecycle.py` calls it again when an atomic album batch is published out of staging. The note only records the time, so it never slows or fails an import.
- **Debounced on one background thread.** A rescan goes out 2 minutes after the last import in a burst, and never more than 10 minutes after the first, so an album is one rescan and a long Wanted run rescans every 10 minutes. Before asking, SYNCHRO.S reads slskd's state (`GET /api/v0/application`, `shares.scanning`); if a scan is running it doesn't queue another and looks again 30 seconds later. Then `PUT /api/v0/shares`. slskd starts the scan in the background and answers 200 even when one is running, which is why the state is read first.
- **Through the existing slskd client.** `SoulseekClient._make_request` (`get_shared_soulseek_client()`), so the configured URL, API key and timeout apply and no new HTTP path exists. Nothing happens when slskd isn't configured or the setting is off. A failure (slskd down, a refused key) is one WARNING per outage, without the key; the burst is kept and retried 10 minutes later.
- **Startup check, for installs like Ross's.** Once slskd answers (it's asked every 30 seconds for up to 10 minutes), if it shares 0 files (`shares.files`, or the per-share `files` from `GET /api/v0/shares` on older slskd) while the library folder holds an audio file, one rescan is requested. One-shot, in its own thread (`@_deferred_start("slskd share check")`).
- **Setting.** "Rescan slskd's shares after imports", on by default, in Settings → Sources → Soulseek, under the API key: "Keeps what you share on Soulseek up to date with your library. The network blocks people who share nothing." Stored as `soulseek.rescan_shares_after_import`.
- **Activity.** Each rescan adds "Soulseek shares updating" to the activity feed, without a toast.
- **README.** The "share files in slskd" notes now say SYNCHRO.S rescans automatically. slskd still has to share the library folder itself; SYNCHRO.S can only ask it to rescan what it's set to share.

**Not drawn:** the checkbox follows the other Soulseek settings; no board.

**Tests:** `tests/test_slskd_share_rescan.py` (30: bursts coalesce, the ceiling, a scan in progress, the single worker thread on a real clock in under a second, disabled and unconfigured make no calls, the startup check's cases including waiting for slskd and the older share listing, failures swallowed and logged once without the key, the real client's helper used with its key, the wiring and the setting's markup); `tests/test_web_server_import_side_effects.py` lists the new deferred start.

### 2026-10-23: Dev, four leftovers

Built on `fix/small-leftovers` from `integration`. No boards; one change Design should know about is the Home card's name (3).

1. **AcoustID lookups.** The download identifier (and through it the radio's automatic fingerprint) and Scan now share one lookup path, `core/acoustid_api.py`, which goes through the outbound guard like Scan now's other services: `api.acoustid.org` only, over TLS, with timeouts and a capped answer. Scores, thresholds and what each error says are unchanged. No part of an API key is written to the logs any more.
2. **Visuals.** A panel opened inside the Visuals (Up next, Recent, Lyrics, Details, Settings) now starts under the header instead of at the top, so the ‹ › arrows and the close button stay reachable; it looks the same otherwise (a top border where it meets the header). Off's tooltip says "No visual, just the track and the controls" (the ambient glow went with the player rebuild; Off is the black overlay with the track and the controls). Code: `static/media-player-v2.js` (`npv2PlaceVisualsPanel`), `static/player.css`.
3. **Home's Following card.** It counted artists only; it now counts everything the Wanted page's Following tab lists: artists, labels, podcasts and authors (podcasts while the Podcasts module is on, authors while Audiobooks is on and the profile may see it). The card is labelled "Following", like the tab (was "Followed artists"), with "Open Following". Its title is the total and the new tracks, "31 followed · 52 new tracks" (one kind is named: "18 artists · 52 new tracks"), and on wide screens the footer says which kinds: "18 artists, 6 labels, 4 podcasts, 3 authors · Next search in about 2 hours". Following only a podcast or a label sets the card up. The summary's new `following` field is in `CONTRACTS.md` ("Home targets"). Redraw if you'd say it differently.
4. **Radio automations.** A radio automation (trigger "A track I don't have played on a favourite station") created or turned on while no station was being watched now starts the radio watcher at once, instead of after the next play, favourite, Radio page visit or restart. Nothing happens while the Radio module is off.

- **7e, Genres:** four columns of 2×2 mosaics (the genre's most-played covers), the name, "212 albums · 180 in your library", "32 you don't have · 11 below quality target" or "All of it in your library"; hover "Add 32 missing to Wanted"; the tile opens Albums with that chip picked; Show All / With gaps; Sort Most albums / Name A–Z; Select picks genres ("Add 49 missing across 3 genres").

**Routes** (`CONTRACTS.md`): `GET /api/library/releases`, `GET /api/library/genres`, `GET /api/library/artist-index`, `POST /api/library/releases/summary`, `POST /api/wishlist/bulk` with `action: "add"` (the route's `grab` / `skip` / `retry` are unchanged), `POST /api/library/artists/follow`, `POST /api/library/albums/upgrade`, `POST /api/library/albums/remove-preview` and `/remove`. All read the acting profile in the caller's library scope; a bulk add goes through `add_to_wishlist_detailed` for that profile, so per-profile Wanted, download rights (requests) and request limits hold.

**Query cost** (seeded libraries, `core/library/release_index.py`): five set-based reads per build, none per album, on existing indexes (no migration needed). 4,000 albums, 43,000 tracks and 10,500 cached releases: a build in about 150 ms, a page in 1–7 ms, "Select all" in about 3 ms. 12,000 albums and 128,000 tracks: about 500 ms and 4–20 ms. The build is cached 30 seconds per profile and library, built once per key at a time, and cleared by these routes' own writes.

**Design's two questions, answered**

- **"Select all": server-side, by filter.** The bar keeps the filter it was pressed under, sends it with the albums unticked since (`exclude_ids`) and any hand picks, and the server resolves it against the whole library. The confirm is counted the same way, so what it says is what's added. Up to 500 albums a call ("That's 1,204 albums. Add up to 500 at a time: pick a genre or an artist to narrow it.").
- **Genre: both.** The album's own genres, which are what the media server read from the files' tags, filled in by SYNCHRO.S's enrichment (Deezer, Last.fm, Discogs, Qobuz, AudioDB, Bandcamp) where the tags had none; an album without any takes its artist's (which adds Spotify's artist genres). A release you don't have takes the metadata service's genres, else its artist's. Folded by case.

**Decided by Dev (the boards don't show these; redraw where you'd do it differently)**

1. **Where "albums you don't have" come from:** the releases a metadata service listed for your artists, from SYNCHRO.S's metadata cache (filled when a discography is fetched: the Artist page, a followed-artist scan, enrichment), albums and EPs only, matched by the source's artist id where the library has one, editions folded against what you have. An artist whose discography hasn't been fetched shows no gaps until it is (question 68).
2. **"Missing" buttons** ("Select all N missing", the genre line's, a genre or artist tile's) add releases you don't have at all and that aren't on Wanted. An album you have part of is added (its missing tracks) only when picked by hand, so the counts beside "17 you don't have" stay honest.
3. **A missing album that's on Wanted** is drawn with its art and the info tag (as 7a's Lantern); one that isn't is the empty dashed square, even when the service has a cover (question 70).
4. **Genre chips:** All plus the six genres with most albums, then "N more ▾", as 7a draws them (the README says "top seven"; seven doesn't fit one row at 1440 with real counts; question 71).
5. **"Tag albums you own"** (the board's tweak) isn't built: owned albums carry no tag.
6. **Tag order:** "11 of 12 found" wins over Below quality target on an album that's both; Below target still counts it.
7. **Genre sort** files each album under one heading: the picked genre, else its first.
8. **One album asks nothing** (a tile's hover); an artist's hover asks when it's more than one album.
9. **Upgrade and Remove from library are admins' tools** (they act on the shared library); members don't see them. Remove acts on the albums you picked that you have, not on a "Select all".
10. **Remove from library = the recycle bin**, not database-only: a media-server scan would otherwise bring the albums straight back (question 74).
11. **An artist with nothing missing** gets Play (their top tracks) on hover, the old grid's button (question 75).
